(4.8)

Then, using (
4.6
),
\mathsf{f}_{Q}\geq 0
, and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle x_{\mathfrak{n}}^{2}\leq c_{\small\ref{;22b}}(N_{\mathfrak{n}})x_{\mathfrak{n}}+\beta c_{\small\ref{;22c}}(N_{\mathfrak{n}}).

(4.9)

Solving (
4.9
) yields
{x}_{\mathfrak{n}}\leq\sqrt{c_{\small\ref{;22b}}(N_{\mathfrak{n}})}+\sqrt{c_{\small\ref{;22b}}(N_{\mathfrak{n}})+4\beta c_{\small\ref{;22c}}(N_{\mathfrak{n}})}
.
Hence, we obtain (
4.7
) from (
4.6
) and (
4.8
).
∎

4.2.
L^{2}
-uniform bound of logarithmic derivative of
\mu_{R}^{N}
.

Let

\mu_{R}^{N}=\mu^{N}\circ\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}}^{-1}
, where
\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}}(\cdot)=\mathsf{s}(\cdot\cap\overline{S}_{R})
.
Let
\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
is the configuration space over
\overline{S}_{R}
.
Then,
\mu_{R}^{N}
can be regarded as a probability measure on
\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
.
Let
\mu_{R}^{N,[1]}
be the one-reduced Campbell measure of
\mu_{R}^{N}
defined by
d\mu_{R}^{N,[1]}=\rho^{N,1}({x})d\mu_{R,{x}}^{N}d{x}
.
Here,
\mu_{R,{x}}^{N}
is the reduced Palm measure of
\mu_{R}^{N}
conditioned at
{x}
and
\rho^{N,1}
is the one-point correlation function of
\mu_{R}^{N}
. Note that the one-point correlation function of
\mu_{R}^{N}
coincides with that of
\mu^{N}
on
\overline{S}_{R}
.
We can regard
\mu_{R}^{N,[1]}
as a measure on
\overline{S}_{R}\times\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
.

Let
\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N}
is the logarithmic derivative of
\mu_{R}^{N}
, that is,

\displaystyle\int_{\overline{S}_{R}\times\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R}}\mathsf{h}\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N}d\mu_{R}^{N,[1]}=-\int_{\overline{S}_{R}\times\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R}}\nabla_{{x}}\mathsf{h}d\mu_{R}^{N,[1]}

(4.10)

for each
\mathscr{F}_{R}^{[1]}
-measurable
\mathsf{h}\in C_{0}^{\infty}(S_{R})\otimes\mathscr{D}_{\circ\mathrm{b}}
, where

\mathscr{F}_{R}^{[1]}=\mathscr{B}(\overline{S}_{R})\times\mathscr{F}_{R}
and
\mathscr{F}_{R}=\sigma[\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}}]
.
Thus, we have generalized the concept of the logarithmic derivative to the random point field
\mu_{R}^{N}
on
\overline{S}_{R}
.

We regard
\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N}
as an
\mathscr{F}_{R}^{[1]}
-measurable function on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}
such that

\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N}({x},\mathsf{s})=0
for
{x}\notin\overline{S}_{R}
and consider

\mu_{R}^{N,[1]}
to be a measure on
(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S},\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\mathscr{F}_{R})
such that

\mu_{R}^{N,[1]}(S_{R}^{c}\times\mathsf{S})=0
. Then, (
4.10
) can be written as

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{h}\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N}d\mu^{N,[1]}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\nabla_{{x}}\mathsf{h}d\mu^{N,[1]}

(4.11)

for each
\mathscr{F}_{R}^{[1]}
-measurable
\mathsf{h}\in C_{0}^{\infty}(S_{R})\otimes\mathscr{D}_{\circ\mathrm{b}}
. We often use the second interpretation because it allows us to regard
\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N}({x},\mathsf{s})
as a function defined on the same set
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}
independent of
R\in\mathbb{N}
.

Remark 4.1
.

If we replace
C_{0}^{\infty}(S_{R})\otimes\mathscr{D}_{\circ\mathrm{b}}
by
C^{\infty}(\overline{S}_{R})\otimes\mathscr{D}_{\circ\mathrm{b}}
as the space of test functions in (
4.10
), then a local-time-type singular drift appears in the representation of
\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N}
, indicating the reflection of particles on the boundary
\partial S_{R}
.

Let
\mathfrak{m}^{N}
be the labeled density of
\mu^{N}
with respect to the Lebesgue measure on
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{N}
, where
\mathfrak{m}^{N}
is given by (
2.18
).
Then,
\mu_{R}^{N,[1]}
has the labeled density
\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{N,[1]}
defined on

\overline{S}_{R}\times\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\overline{S}_{R}^{k}
such that, for
0\leq k\leq N-1
and
k+l=N-1
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{N,[1]}({x},\mathbf{s})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathscr{Z}_{R}^{N,k}({x})}\int_{(\overline{S}_{R}^{c})^{l}}\mathfrak{m}^{N}({x},\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t})d\mathbf{t},\quad\text{ for }\mathbf{s}\in\overline{S}_{R}^{k},

\displaystyle\mathscr{Z}_{R}^{N,k}({x})

\displaystyle=\int_{\overline{S}_{R}^{k}\times(\overline{S}_{R}^{c})^{l}}\mathfrak{m}^{N}({x},\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t})d\mathbf{s}d\mathbf{t}\quad\text{ for }{x}\in\overline{S}_{R}.

Because
\mathfrak{m}^{N}(\mathbf{{x}})
is a symmetric function of
\mathbf{{x}}=({x}^{i})_{i=1}^{N}
,
\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{N,[1]}
is well defined.

The logarithmic derivate
\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N}\!:\!\overline{S}_{R}\times\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R}\!\to\!\mathbb{R}^{d}
is given by
\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N}({x},\mathsf{s})=0
for
\mathsf{s}(\overline{S}_{R})\geq N
and it does by

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N}({x},\mathsf{s})=\nabla_{{x}}\log\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{N,[1]}({x},\mathbf{s})

(4.12)

for
\mathsf{s}(\overline{S}_{R})\leq N-1
,
where
\mathsf{s}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\delta_{s^{i}}
is defined by
\mathbf{s}=(s^{i})_{i=1}^{k}\in\overline{S}_{R}^{k}
.

Let
0\leq k\leq N-1
and
k+l=N-1
.
Let
\mathbf{t}\in(\overline{S}_{R}^{c})^{l}
.
We set, for
\mathbf{s}=(s^{i})_{i=1}^{k}\in\overline{S}_{R}^{k}
and
\mathsf{s}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\delta_{s^{i}}\in\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
,

\displaystyle\langle f\rangle_{R}^{N}({x},\mathsf{s})=\frac{1}{\int_{(\overline{S}_{R}^{c})^{l}}\mathfrak{m}^{N}({x},\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t})d\mathbf{t}}\int_{(\overline{S}_{R}^{c})^{l}}(\check{f}\mathfrak{m}^{N})({x},\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t})d\mathbf{t}.

(4.13)

where
f\!:\!\overline{S}_{R}\times\mathsf{S}^{N-1}\!\to\!\mathbb{R}
,
\mathsf{S}^{N-1}=\{\mathsf{u}\in\mathsf{S};\mathsf{u}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=N-1\}
, and

\check{f}\!:\!\overline{S}_{R}\times(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{N-1}\!\to\!\mathbb{R}
is the function such that

\check{f}({x},\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t})=f({x},\mathsf{s})
and that
\check{f}({x},\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t})
is symmetric in
(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t})
.

The following identity plays an important role in our analysis.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
\mathsf{d}^{N}
and
\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N}
be as in (
4.1
) and (
4.12
), respectively. Then,

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N}({x},\mathsf{s})=\langle\mathsf{d}^{N}\rangle_{R}^{N}({x},\mathsf{s}).

(4.14)

Proof.

Let
k+l=N-1
. Let
\mathsf{s}
,
\mathbf{s}
, and
\mathbf{t}
be as in (
4.13
).
Then,

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N}({x},\mathsf{s})=\frac{1}{\int_{(\overline{S}_{R}^{c})^{l}}\mathfrak{m}^{N}({x},\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t})d\mathbf{t}}\nabla_{{x}}\int_{(\overline{S}_{R}^{c})^{l}}\mathfrak{m}^{N}({x},\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t})d\mathbf{t}\ \text{ by \eqref{:23q}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\int_{(\overline{S}_{R}^{c})^{l}}\mathfrak{m}^{N}({x},\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t})d\mathbf{t}}\int_{(\overline{S}_{R}^{c})^{l}}\nabla_{{x}}\mathfrak{m}^{N}({x},\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t})d\mathbf{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\int_{(\overline{S}_{R}^{c})^{l}}\mathfrak{m}^{N}({x},\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t})d\mathbf{t}}\int_{(\overline{S}_{R}^{c})^{l}}\{\nabla_{{x}}\log\mathfrak{m}^{N}({x},\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t})\}\mathfrak{m}^{N}({x},\mathbf{s},\mathbf{t})d\mathbf{t}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathsf{d}^{N}\rangle_{R}^{N}({x},\mathsf{s})\ \text{ by \eqref{:21w}, \eqref{:23t}}.

Thus, we obtain (
4.14
).
∎

As we explained around (
4.11
), we consider
\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N}
as a function on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}
.
We now obtain a uniform
L^{2}
-bound of the logarithmic derivatives
\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N}
of
\mu_{R}^{N}
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Assume
(
A1
)
–
(
A3
)
. Let
\mathsf{f}_{Q}
be as in (
4.5
).
Let
R\geq Q+1
. Then,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{f}_{Q}|\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}|^{2}d\mu^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}\leq c_{\small\ref{;22z}}.

(4.15)

Proof.

Recall that
\mu^{N,[1]}(d{x}d\mathsf{s})=\rho^{N,1}({x})\mu^{N}_{x}(d\mathsf{s})d{x}
and
0\leq\mathsf{f}_{Q}({x})\leq 1
. Then,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{f}_{Q}|\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N}|^{2}d\mu^{N,[1]}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{f}_{Q}\big|\langle\mathsf{d}^{N}\rangle_{R}^{N}\big|^{2}d\mu^{N,[1]}

by Lemma
4.4

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{f}_{Q}\langle|\mathsf{d}^{N}|^{2}\rangle_{R}^{N}d\mu^{N,[1]}

by Jensen’s inequality

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{f}_{Q}|\mathsf{d}^{N}|^{2}d\mu^{N,[1]}

\displaystyle\text{ by \eqref{:22z}, \eqref{:23t}}.

Hence, we obtain (
4.15
) from Lemma
4.3
.
∎

4.3.
Strong convergence of density functions of
\mu_{R}^{N}
.

Let
\Lambda_{R}
be the Poisson random point field with intensity measure
1_{S_{R}}d{x}
.
Let
\mu_{R}=\mu\circ\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}}^{-1}
.
Let
\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}
and
\mu_{R}^{N,[1]}
be the one-reduced Campbell measures of
\Lambda_{R}
and
\mu_{R}^{N}
, respectively.
We denote by
\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{N,[1]}
the Radon–Nikodym density of
\mu_{R}^{N,[1]}
with respect to
\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}
.
The existence of
\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{N,[1]}
is obvious from (
2.18
).
Let

\displaystyle\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R,k}^{[1]}=\{({x},\mathsf{s})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S};\,{x}\in\overline{S}_{R},\mathsf{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash\overline{S}_{R})=0,\mathsf{s}(\overline{S}_{R})=k\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{R,k}^{N,[1]}=\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{N,[1]}1_{\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R,k}^{[1]}},\quad\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N,[1]}=\mathfrak{m}_{R,k}^{N,[1]}\wedge l.

(4.16)

Let
\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
be the configuration space over
\overline{S}_{R}
. Then, we naturally regard
\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R,k}^{[1]}
as a subset as a subset of
\overline{S}_{R}\times\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
as follows:

\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R,k}^{[1]}=\{({x},\mathsf{s})\in\overline{S}_{R}\times\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R};\,\mathsf{s}(\overline{S}_{R})=k\}
.

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
\mathsf{f}_{Q}
be as in (
4.5
). Let
Q,R\in\mathbb{N}
such that
R\geq Q+1
.
Then, for all
k\in\{0\}\cup\mathbb{N}
and
l\in\mathbb{N}
, the following hold.

i

\{\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}\}_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{N}}
is relatively compact in
L^{2}(\Lambda_{R}^{[1]})
.

ii
There exists a family of non-negative functions
\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{[1]}
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}=\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{[1]}\text{ in }L^{2}(\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}),

(4.17)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{[1]}=\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l+1}^{[1]}\wedge l.

(4.18)

Proof.

Note that
\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N,[1]}({x},\mathsf{s})
is symmetric in
(x,s^{1},\ldots,s^{k})
when regarded as a function on
\overline{S}_{R}^{k+1}
by the identification
({x},\mathsf{s})=({x},s^{1},\ldots,s^{k})
, where
\mathsf{s}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\delta_{s^{i}}
. Let

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{R}^{[1]}[\mathsf{h}]({x},\mathsf{s})=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\sum_{{x}\in S_{R}}|\partial_{{x}}\mathsf{h}({x},\mathsf{s})|^{2}+\sum_{s^{i}\in S_{R}}|\partial_{s^{i}}\mathsf{h}({x},\mathsf{s})|^{2}\Big).

Then, using the permutation invariance of
\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N,[1]}
in
(x,s^{1},\ldots,s^{N-1})
and noting that
k+1
particles exist in
\overline{S}_{R}
for
({x},\mathsf{s})\in\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R,k}^{[1]}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathbb{D}_{R}^{[1]}[\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N,[1]}]d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}\leq 2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathbb{D}_{R}^{[1]}[\mathsf{f}_{Q}]\big(\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N,[1]}\big)^{2}+\mathsf{f}_{Q}^{2}\mathbb{D}_{R}^{[1]}[\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N,[1]}]d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\lvert\nabla\mathsf{f}_{Q}\rvert^{2}\big(\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N,[1]}\big)^{2}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{f}_{Q}^{2}\mathbb{D}_{R}^{[1]}[\log\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N,[1]}]\big(\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N,[1]}\big)^{2}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\lvert\nabla\mathsf{f}_{Q}\rvert^{2}\big(\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N,[1]}\big)^{2}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{f}_{Q}^{2}(k+1)|\mathsf{d}^{N}|^{2}\big(\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N,[1]}\big)^{2}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}

\displaystyle\leq l^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\lvert\nabla\mathsf{f}_{Q}\rvert^{2}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}+(k+1)l\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{f}_{Q}\lvert\mathsf{d}^{N}\rvert^{2}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N,[1]}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}\quad\text{by \eqref{:22z}, \eqref{:25b}}.

Hence, combining this with (
4.5
) and Lemma
4.3
yields the following.

\displaystyle\limsup_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathbb{D}_{R}^{[1]}[\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}]d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}<\infty.

(4.19)

As before, we regard
\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}({x},\mathsf{s})
as a function on
S_{R}^{k+1}
.
Note that
\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}=0
on
(\partial S_{R})\times S_{R}^{k}
from (
4.5
) and
R\geq Q+1
.
Hence, from the Rellich embedding theorem and (
4.19
), there exists
q>1
such that

\{\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}\}_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{N}}
is relatively compact in
L^{q}(S_{R}^{k+1},d{x}^{1}\cdots d{x}^{k+1})
,
where we take
1<q<n/(n-1)
for
n=d(k+1)
.
According to this and (
4.5
),
\{\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}\}_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{N}}
is relatively compact in
L^{q}(\Lambda_{R}^{[1]})
.
From (
4.16
),
\{\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}\}_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}(\Lambda_{R}^{[1]})
.
Hence,
\{\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}\}_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{N}}
is relatively compact in
L^{2}(\Lambda_{R}^{[1]})
.
This implies i.

From i and a diagonal argument, there exist a subsequence of
\{N_{\mathfrak{n}}\}
, denoted by the same symbol, and a limit
\{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}_{Q,R,k,l}^{[1]}\}
such that

\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}_{Q,R,k,l}^{[1]}
in
L^{2}(\Lambda_{R}^{[1]})

(4.20)

for all
R\geq Q+1
and
k\in\{0\}\cup\mathbb{N}
and
l\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then, we obtain the uniqueness of limit points of
\{\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}\}_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{N}}
from the weak convergence
\lim\mu^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}=\mu
given in
(
A2
)
. Hence, the full sequence converges to
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}_{Q,R,k,l}^{[1]}
in (
4.20
).
According to (
4.5
), we set

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{[1]}=\begin{cases}\mathsf{f}_{Q}^{-1}\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}_{Q,R,k,l}^{[1]}&\text{on }S_{Q+1}\times\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R}\\
0&\text{on }(\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash S_{Q+1})\times\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R}\end{cases}

(4.21)

From (
4.20
) and (
4.21
), we have (
4.17
).
From (
4.16
) and (
4.17
), we obtain (
4.18
).
∎

Theorem 4.1
.

i

\mu
has a
k
-density function
\mathfrak{m}_{R,k}
on
\overline{S}_{R}^{k}
for each
R,k\in\mathbb{N}
.

ii

\mu
has a one-point correlation function
\rho^{1}
.

Proof.

Using the consistency (
4.18
), we define the function
\mathfrak{m}_{R,k}^{[1]}
by
\mathfrak{m}_{R,k}^{[1]}\wedge l=\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{[1]}
.
Then, we define function
\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{[1]}
by
\mathfrak{m}_{R,k}^{[1]}=1_{\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R,k}^{[1]}}\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{[1]}
. Here, we used
\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R,k}^{[1]}
are disjoint sets.
We define
\mu_{R}^{[1]}=\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{[1]}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}
.
Then,
\mu_{R}^{[1]}
becomes the one-reduced Campbell measure of
\mu_{R}
. Note that

\mathfrak{m}_{R,k}^{[1]}({x},s^{1},\ldots,s^{k-1}):=\mathfrak{m}_{R,k}^{[1]}({x},\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\delta_{s^{i}})

is symmetric on
\overline{S}_{R}^{k}
for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
Hence, we can construct the
k
-density function
\mathfrak{m}_{R,k}
of
\mu
on
\overline{S}_{R}
from
\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{[1]}
by the formula

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{R,k}({x}^{1},{x}^{2},\ldots,x^{k})=c_{\small\ref{;2'f}}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k}^{[1]}({x}^{1},{x}^{2},\ldots,{x}^{k}).

The constant
c_{4.4}
is determined by the normalization due to the value
\mu_{R}(\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R}^{k})
.

The one-point correlation function
\rho^{1}
is given by the formula

\displaystyle\rho^{1}({x})=\mathfrak{m}_{R,1}({x})+\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(j-k)!}\int_{\overline{S}_{R}^{j-1}}\mathfrak{m}_{R,j}({x},{x}^{2},\ldots,{x}^{j})d{x}^{2}\cdots d{x}^{j}.

(4.22)

From (
2.22
),
\int_{\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{s}(S_{R})\mu(d\mathsf{s})<\infty
.
Hence, the right-hand side of (
4.22
) is finite.
Hence,
\rho^{1}
is the one-point correlation function of
\mu
.
∎

4.4.
The logarithmic derivative
\mathsf{d}^{\mu}
of
\mu
.

This subsection aims to construct the logarithmic derivative
\mathsf{d}^{\mu}
of
\mu
.
For this purpose, we begin by constructing the logarithmic derivative
\mathsf{d}_{R}
of
\mu_{R}
, where

\mu_{R}=\mu\circ\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}}^{-1}
.

Let
\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R,k}^{[1]}
,
\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{N,[1]}
, and
\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N,[1]}
be as in (
4.16
). Let

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}_{R,k,l}^{N,[1]}

\displaystyle=\{({x},\mathsf{s})\in\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R,k}^{[1]};0<\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{N,[1]}({x},\mathsf{s})<l\},

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{R,k,l}^{N}({x},\mathsf{s})

\displaystyle=1_{\mathsf{M}_{R,k,l}^{N,[1]}}({x},\mathsf{s})\nabla_{{x}}\log\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N,[1]}({x},\mathsf{s}).

(4.23)

Let
\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{[1]}
be as in Lemma
4.6
. Then, using (
4.18
), we define
\mathsf{M}_{R,k,l}^{[1]}
and
\mathsf{d}_{R,k,l}
by

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}_{R,k,l}^{[1]}

\displaystyle=\{({x},\mathsf{s})\in\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R,k}^{[1]};0<\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{[1]}({x},\mathsf{s})<l\},

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{R,k,l}({x},\mathsf{s})

\displaystyle=1_{\mathsf{M}_{R,k,l}^{[1]}}({x},\mathsf{s})\nabla_{{x}}\log\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{[1]}({x},\mathsf{s}).

(4.24)

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
\mathsf{f}_{Q}
be as in (
4.5
).
Then, for each
k\in\{0\}\cup\mathbb{N}
and
R,l\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\mathsf{d}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]})^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{d}_{R,k,l}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{[1]})^{1/2}\quad\text{ weakly in $L^{2}(\Lambda_{R}^{[1]})$},

(4.25)

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{R,k,l}({x},\mathsf{s})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{d}_{R,k,l+1}({x},\mathsf{s})\quad\text{ for }({x},\mathsf{s})\in\mathsf{M}_{R,k,l}^{[1]}.

(4.26)

Proof.

From Lemma
4.5
, (
4.16
), and (
4.23
), we easily deduce that
\{\mathsf{d}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]})^{1/2}\}_{\mathfrak{n}}
is bounded in
L^{2}(\Lambda_{R}^{[1]})
.
Hence, we have a subsequence of
\{N_{\mathfrak{n}}\}
, denoted by the same symbol, and a limit
\widehat{\mathsf{d}}_{R,k,l}
such that

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathsf{d}}_{R,k,l}=\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\mathsf{d}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]})^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad\text{ weakly in $L^{2}(\Lambda_{R}^{[1]})$}.

(4.27)

From Lemma
4.6
, we deduce

\displaystyle(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{[1]})^{1/2}=\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]})^{1/2}

\displaystyle\text{ in }L^{4}(\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}).

(4.28)

Hence, from (
4.27
) and (
4.28
), we obtain for any
\mathsf{h}\in C_{0}^{\infty}(S_{R})\otimes\mathscr{D}_{\circ\mathrm{b}}

\displaystyle\int_{S_{R}\times\mathsf{S}}\widehat{\mathsf{d}}_{R,k,l}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{[1]})^{1/2}\mathsf{h}d\Lambda_{R}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\int_{S_{R}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{d}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]})^{1/2}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]})^{1/2}\mathsf{h}d\Lambda_{R}.

(4.29)

We set

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathsf{d}}_{R,k,l}({x},\mathsf{s})=\begin{cases}\widehat{\mathsf{d}}_{R,k,l}({x},\mathsf{s})(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{[1]}({x},\mathsf{s}))^{-1/2}&\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{[1]}({x},\mathsf{s})>0\\
0&\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{[1]}({x},\mathsf{s})=0\end{cases}.

(4.30)

Then, from (
4.24
) and (
4.28
)–(
4.30
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{S_{R}\times\mathsf{S}}\widetilde{\mathsf{d}}_{R,k,l}\mathsf{h}\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{[1]}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}=\int_{S_{R}\times\mathsf{S}}\widehat{\mathsf{d}}_{R,k,l}\mathsf{h}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{[1]})^{1/2}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}\quad\text{ by \eqref{:26p}}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\int_{S_{R}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{d}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}\mathsf{h}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]})^{1/2}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]})^{1/2}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}\quad\text{ by \eqref{:26u}}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}-\int_{S_{R}\times\mathsf{S}}\nabla_{{x}}(\mathsf{h}\mathsf{f}_{Q})\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}\quad\text{ by \eqref{:23r}}

\displaystyle=-\int_{S_{R}\times\mathsf{S}}\nabla_{{x}}(\mathsf{h}\mathsf{f}_{Q})\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{[1]}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}\quad\text{ by \eqref{:22z}, \eqref{:25a}}

\displaystyle=\int_{S_{R}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{d}_{R,k,l}\mathsf{h}\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,k,l}^{[1]}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}\quad\text{ by \eqref{:26j}}.

Hence, we have
\widetilde{\mathsf{d}}_{R,k,l}=\mathsf{d}_{R,k,l}
.
From this, (
4.27
), (
4.28
), and (
4.30
), we obtain (
4.25
).

From (
4.23
),
\mathsf{d}_{R,k,l}^{N}
satisfy
\mathsf{d}_{R,k,l}^{N}({x},\mathsf{s})=\mathsf{d}_{R,k,l+1}^{N}({x},\mathsf{s})
for
({x},\mathsf{s})\in\mathsf{M}_{R,k,l}^{N,[1]}
for all
l\in\mathbb{N}
.
This property is inherited by
\mathsf{d}_{R,k,l}
from (
4.27
)–(
4.29
). Hence, we obtain (
4.26
).
∎

Let
\mathsf{M}_{R}^{[1]}=\{({x},\mathsf{s});0<\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{[1]}({x},\mathsf{s})<\infty\}

and
\mathsf{d}_{R}({x},\mathsf{s})=1_{\mathsf{M}_{R}^{[1]}}({x},\mathsf{s})\nabla_{{x}}\log\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{[1]}({x},\mathsf{s})
.
Then, from (
4.24
), we derive the following:

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{R}({x},\mathsf{s})=\lim_{l\to\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathsf{d}_{R,k,l}({x},\mathsf{s}),\quad\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\lvert\mathsf{d}_{R,k,l}({x},\mathsf{s})\rvert\leq\lvert\mathsf{d}_{R}({x},\mathsf{s})\rvert.

(4.31)

Let
\mathsf{f}_{Q}
,
c_{\small\ref{;22z}}
, and
\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}
be as in (
4.5
), (
4.6
), and (
4.12
).

Lemma 4.8
.

For all
R\geq Q+1
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\lvert\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}\rvert^{2}\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}\leq c_{\small\ref{;22z}},

(4.32)

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]})^{1/2}=\mathsf{d}_{R}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{[1]})^{1/2}\quad\text{ weakly in $L^{2}(\Lambda_{R}^{[1]})$},

(4.33)

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]})^{1/2}=(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{[1]})^{1/2}\quad\text{ in $L^{2}(\Lambda_{R}^{[1]})$}.

(4.34)

Proof.

Recall that
\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}=d\mu_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}
. Then, from Lemma
4.5
, we obtain (
4.32
).

From (
4.32
),
\{\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]})^{1/2}\}_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{N}}
is relatively compact under the weak convergence in
L^{2}(\Lambda_{R}^{[1]})
.
From Lemma
4.7
and (
4.31
), the limit points are unique and coincide with
\mathsf{d}_{R}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{[1]})^{1/2}
. Hence, we obtain (
4.33
).

From
(
A2
)
, we have
\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\mu^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}=\mu
weakly. Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{[1]}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}<\infty.

Then, from Lemma
4.6
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\lvert(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]})^{1/2}-(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{[1]})^{1/2}\rvert^{2}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}+\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{[1]}-2(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]})^{1/2}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{[1]})^{1/2}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}=0.

This implies (
4.34
).
∎

Let
\mathscr{F}_{R}^{[1]}=\mathscr{B}(\overline{S}_{R})\times\sigma[\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}}]
. Then,
\mathsf{d}_{R}
is
\mathscr{F}_{R}^{[1]}
-measurable.

Theorem 4.2
.

i

Let
\mathsf{f}_{Q}
and
c_{\small\ref{;22z}}
be as in (
4.5
) and (
4.6
). Then, for all
R\geq Q+1
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}|\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathsf{d}_{R}({x},\mathsf{s})|^{2}d\mu_{R}^{[1]}\leq c_{\small\ref{;22z}},

(4.35)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{h}\mathsf{d}_{R}d\mu_{R}^{[1]}=\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{h}\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}d\mu_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]},

(4.36)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{h}\mathsf{d}_{R}d\mu_{R}^{[1]}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\nabla_{{x}}\mathsf{h}d\mu_{R}^{[1]}

(4.37)

for all
\mathscr{F}_{R}^{[1]}
-measurable
\mathsf{h}=(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\hat{\mathsf{f}})\otimes\mathsf{g}\in\mathscr{D}_{\circ\mathrm{b}}^{[1]}
such that
\hat{\mathsf{f}}\in C_{0}^{\infty}(S_{R})
.

ii

\mathsf{d}_{R}
is the logarithmic derivative of
\mu_{R}=\mu\circ\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}}^{-1}
.

Proof.

From Lemma
4.6
,
d\mu_{R}^{[1]}=\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{[1]}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}
. Then, from (
4.32
) and Lemma
4.8
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}

\displaystyle|\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathsf{d}_{R}|^{2}d\mu_{R}^{[1]}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}|\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathsf{d}_{R}|^{2}\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{[1]}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}\quad\text{ by }d\mu_{R}^{[1]}=\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{[1]}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\lvert\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}\rvert^{2}\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}\quad\text{ by \eqref{:2)a}}

\displaystyle=\liminf_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}|\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}|^{2}d\mu_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}\leq c_{\small\ref{;22z}}\quad\text{ by $\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{[1]}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}=d\mu_{R}^{[1]}$ and \eqref{:2)z}}.

This implies (
4.35
). From Lemma
4.8
, we obtain (
4.36
). Indeed,

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{h}\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}d\mu_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}=\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{h}\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}(\hat{\mathsf{f}}\otimes\mathsf{g})\mathsf{f}_{Q}^{1/2}\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}(\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]})^{1/2}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]})^{1/2}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}(\hat{\mathsf{f}}\otimes\mathsf{g})\mathsf{f}_{Q}^{1/2}\mathsf{d}_{R}(\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{[1]})^{1/2}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{[1]})^{1/2}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}\quad\text{ by \eqref{:2)a}, \eqref{:2)b}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{h}\mathsf{d}_{R}\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{[1]}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{h}\mathsf{d}_{R}d\mu_{R}^{[1]}\quad\text{ by }\mathsf{h}=(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\hat{\mathsf{f}})\otimes\mathsf{g}.

This implies (
4.36
).
From (
4.36
), (
4.11
), and
(
A2
)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{h}\mathsf{d}_{R}d\mu_{R}^{[1]}=

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{h}\mathsf{d}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}d\mu_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}\quad\text{ by \eqref{:26b}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\nabla_{{x}}\mathsf{h}d\mu_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}\quad\text{ by \eqref{:23s}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\nabla_{{x}}\mathsf{h}d\mu_{R}^{[1]}\quad\text{ by $(${A2}$)$}.

(4.38)

Here, for the last line, we have used the weak convergence of
\mu^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}
to
\mu
, which imposed by
(
A2
)
ii.
From (
4.38
), we deduce (
4.37
). Thus, we have obtained i.

We easily extend (
4.37
) to all
\mathsf{h}=\hat{\mathsf{f}}\otimes\mathsf{g}
such that
\hat{\mathsf{f}}\in C_{0}^{\infty}(S_{R})
.
Hence,
\mathsf{d}_{R}
is the logarithmic derivative of
\mu_{R}
. This implies ii.
∎

We now construct the logarithmic derivative
\mathsf{d}^{\mu}
of
\mu
using the consistency of
\mathsf{d}_{R}
.

Theorem 4.3
.

The logarithmic derivative
\mathsf{d}^{\mu}
of
\mu
exists.
For
\mu^{[1]}
-a.e. and in
L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{2}(\mu^{[1]})
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}^{\mu}({x},\mathsf{s})=\lim_{R\to\infty}\mathsf{d}_{R}({x},\mathsf{s}).

(4.39)

In particular,
\mathsf{d}^{\mu}\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{2}(\mu^{[1]})
and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{h}\mathsf{d}^{\mu}d\mu^{[1]}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\nabla_{{x}}\mathsf{h}d\mu^{[1]}\quad\text{ for each $\mathsf{h}\in\mathscr{D}_{\circ\mathrm{b}}$},

(4.40)

\displaystyle E^{\mu_{{x}}}[\mathsf{d}^{\mu}({x},\mathsf{s})|\mathscr{F}_{R}]=\mathsf{d}_{R}({x},\mathsf{s})\quad\text{ for }{x}\in\overline{S}_{R},

(4.41)

where
\mathscr{F}_{R}=\sigma[\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}}]
and
\mu_{x}
is the reduced Palm measure of
\mu
conditioned at
{x}
.

Proof.

Using (
4.37
) and noting
\mu_{R}=\mu\circ\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}}^{-1}
,
we deduce that, for each
\mathscr{F}_{R}^{[1]}
-measurable
\mathsf{h}=\mathsf{f}\otimes\mathsf{g}\in\mathscr{D}_{\circ\mathrm{b}}^{[1]}
such that
\mathsf{f}\in C_{0}^{\infty}(S_{R})
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{h}\mathsf{d}_{R}d\mu^{[1]}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\nabla_{{x}}\mathsf{h}d\mu^{[1]}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{h}\mathsf{d}_{R+1}d\mu^{[1]}.

(4.42)

For a non-negative
f
such that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathsf{f}d{x}=1
, we consider the probability measure
\mathsf{f}\otimes 1\mu^{[1]}
.
Then, from (
4.42
), we can easily show that
\mathsf{d}_{R}\mathsf{f}
is an
\{\mathscr{F}_{R}^{[1]}\}
-martingale under
\mathsf{f}\otimes 1\mu^{[1]}
.
Furthermore,
\mathsf{d}^{\mu}\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{2}(\mu^{[1]})
from (
4.35
).
Hence, (
4.39
) follows from the martingale convergence theorems.
Equation (
4.40
) follows immediately from (
4.39
) and (
4.42
). Thus,
\mathsf{d}^{\mu}
is the logarithmic derivative of
\mu
.
From (
4.40
) and (
4.42
),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{h}\mathsf{d}^{\mu}d\mu^{[1]}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{h}\mathsf{d}_{R}d\mu^{[1]}.

(4.43)

Recall that
\mathsf{d}_{R}
is
\mathscr{F}_{R}^{[1]}
-measurable. Then, from (
4.43
), we obtain (
4.41
).
∎

5.
Explicit formulas for the logarithmic derivative
\mathsf{d}^{\mu}
: Proof of Theorem
2.1

In this section, we present explicit formulas for the logarithmic derivative
\mathsf{d}^{\mu}
of
\mu
and complete the proof of Theorem
2.1
. Although Theorem
4.3
established the existence of a logarithmic derivative
\mathsf{d}^{\mu}
of
\mu
, this result only confirms its existence and does not address the uniqueness of solutions to the associated ISDE or the existence of strong solutions.

This section assumes
(
A1
)
–
(
A3
)
. These assumptions are omitted from the statements of the Lemmas and Theorems for brevity.

5.1.
Construction of the logarithmic derivative
\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}
of
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}

The goal of this subsection is to construct the logarithmic derivative of
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}
, where
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}
is the regular conditional probability defined by
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}=\mu(\cdot\mid\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}^{c}}(\mathsf{s})=\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}^{c}}(\mathsf{y}))
.

Let
\mu^{N}
be the distribution of the
N
-particle system described in Section
1
.
For
\mu^{N}
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}\in\mathsf{S}
, let
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{N}
be the regular conditional probability of
\mu^{N}
given by

\displaystyle\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{N}=\mu^{N}(\cdot|\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}^{c}}(\mathsf{s})=\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}^{c}}(\mathsf{y})).

(5.1)

Let
\rho_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{N,1}
be the one-point correlation function of
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{N}
(on
\overline{S}_{R}
).
Let
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y},{x}}^{N}
be the reduced Palm measure of
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{N}
conditioned at
\mathsf{x}
.
Let

\displaystyle\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{N,[1]}(d{x}d\mathsf{s})=\rho_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{N,1}(x)\mu_{R,\mathsf{y},{x}}^{N}(d\mathsf{s})d{x}

(5.2)

be the one-reduced Campbell measure of
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{N}
.
Then, from
(
A1
)
and
(
A2
)
,
\rho_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{N,1}({x})
is continuous in
{x}
on
\overline{S}_{R}\cap O_{N}
.
We denote by
\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{N}
the logarithmic derivative of
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{N}
for
\mu^{N}
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}
, where

\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{N}
is defined by (
4.10
) by replacing
\mu_{R}^{N,[1]}
by
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{N,[1]}
.
Because
\rho_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{N,1}
does not exist on
S_{R}^{c}
,
\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{N}
is only defined on
\overline{S}_{R}\times\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
,
where
\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
is the configuration space over
\overline{S}_{R}
.

Let
\mathfrak{l}=(\mathfrak{l}^{i})
be the label in (
2.43
). We set
\mathsf{y}^{N}=\mathsf{0}
for
\mathsf{y}=\mathsf{0}
and

\displaystyle\mathsf{y}^{N}=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{y}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\wedge N}\delta_{\mathfrak{l}^{i}(\mathsf{y})}\quad\text{ for $\mathsf{y}\neq\mathsf{0}$}.

(5.3)

Because
\lvert\mathfrak{l}^{i}(\mathsf{y}^{N})\rvert\leq\lvert\mathfrak{l}^{i+1}(\mathsf{y}^{N})\rvert
by (
2.43
), the following holds for each
R\in\mathbb{N}
:

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}^{c}}(\mathsf{y}^{N})=\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}^{c}}(\mathsf{y})\quad\text{ for all $\mathsf{y}\in\mathsf{S}$}.

From
(
A1
)
and
(
A2
)
, the labeled density
\mathfrak{m}^{N}
is a continuous function on
O_{N}^{N}
.
Hence,
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N}}^{N}
can be defined for all
\mathsf{y}\in\mathsf{S}
such that
\mathsf{y}(O_{N})=N
and
\mathsf{y}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash O_{N})=0
.

Using (
2.18
), we obtain the following for
\mu^{N}
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}
and
({x},\mathsf{s})\in\overline{S}_{R}\times\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R}

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{N}({x},\mathsf{s})=

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}^{N}(x,\mathsf{s}+\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}^{c}}(\mathsf{y}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\beta\Big\{\nabla\Phi^{N}({x})+\sum_{s^{i}\in\overline{S}_{R}}\nabla_{{x}}\Psi^{N}({x},s^{i})+\sum_{i=1,\,{y}^{i}\not\in\overline{S}_{R}}^{N}\nabla_{{x}}\Psi^{N}({x},{y}^{i})\Big\},

(5.4)

where we label
\mathsf{y}=\sum_{i}\delta_{{y}^{i}}
such that
{y}^{i}=\mathfrak{l}^{i}(\mathsf{y})
.
Then, from (
2.43
), we have
\lvert{y}^{i}\rvert\leq\lvert{y}^{i+1}\rvert
for all
i
.
For
\mathsf{y}
satisfying
\mathsf{y}(\overline{S}_{R})\geq 1
, (
5.3
) and (
5.4
) imply that,
for
({x},\mathsf{s})\in\overline{S}_{R}\times\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N}}^{N}({x},\mathsf{s})

\displaystyle=-\beta\Big\{\nabla\Phi^{N}({x})+\sum_{s^{i}\in\overline{S}_{R}}\nabla_{{x}}\Psi^{N}({x},s^{i})+\sum_{i=1,\,{y}^{i}\not\in\overline{S}_{R}}^{N}\nabla_{{x}}\Psi^{N}({x},{y}^{i})\Big\}.

(5.5)

Let
a_{\mathsf{r}}=\{a_{\mathsf{r}}(R)\}_{R\in\mathbb{N}}
be an increasing sequence of increasing sequences of natural numbers.
Let
\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}]
be the set defined by (
2.44
).
Then,
\{\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}]\}_{\mathsf{r}\in\mathbb{N}}
is an increasing sequence of compact sets.
From (
2.45
),
\mu^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}(\cup_{\mathsf{r}\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}])=1
.
Let

\displaystyle\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}},\mathsf{r}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}=\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}(\cdot\cap\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}]).

(5.6)

Let
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}},\mathsf{r}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}
be the one-reduced Campbell measure of
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}},\mathsf{r}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}
.
Let
\mathsf{f}_{Q}
be as in (
4.5
) and
Q<R\in\mathbb{N}
.
For
\mathsf{y}\in\cup_{\mathsf{r}=1}^{\infty}\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}]
, let
\mathsf{r}\in\mathbb{N}
be such that
\mathsf{y}\in\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}]\backslash\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}-1}]
.
Then, for
\mathsf{y}\in\cup_{\mathsf{r}=1}^{\infty}\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}]
and
Q<R,\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{N}
, we define the constants

c_{5.1}
,
c_{5.2}
, and
c_{5.3}
by

\displaystyle c_{\small\ref{;42a}}(Q,\mathsf{y},R,\mathfrak{n})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{f}_{Q}\lvert\nabla\log\mathsf{f}_{Q}\rvert^{2}\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}},\mathsf{r}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}(d{x}d\mathsf{s}),

\displaystyle c_{\small\ref{;42b}}(Q,\mathsf{y},R,\mathfrak{n})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{f}_{Q}\Big(\Delta\Phi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}+\sum_{i=1}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}-1}\psi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},s^{i})\Big)\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}},\mathsf{r}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}(d{x}d\mathsf{s}),

\displaystyle c_{\small\ref{;42c}}(Q,\mathsf{y})=\frac{1}{4}\sup_{R>Q}\sup_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{N}}\Big\{\sqrt{c_{\small\ref{;42a}}}+\sqrt{c_{\small\ref{;42a}}+4\beta c_{\small\ref{;42b}}}\Big\}^{2}.

(5.7)

We note
\psi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}\geq 0
from (
2.17
). Hence,
c_{\small\ref{;42a}}+4\beta c_{\small\ref{;42b}}\geq 0
.
We can replace the assumption
\psi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}\geq 0
by
\liminf_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}c_{\small\ref{;42a}}+4\beta c_{\small\ref{;42b}}\geq 0
because it is used only for this.

Lemma 5.1
.

For each
Q\in\mathbb{N}
and all
\mathsf{y}\in\cup_{\mathsf{r}=1}^{\infty}\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}]
, the constant
c_{\small\ref{;42c}}(Q,\mathsf{y})
is finite.

Proof.

From (
2.44
) and (
5.6
), we have, for all
Q<R,\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{N}
, and
\mathsf{y}\in\mathsf{S}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{s}(S_{Q+1})\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}},\mathsf{r}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}(d\mathsf{s})\leq a_{\mathsf{r}}(Q+1).

(5.8)

Hence, from (
5.7
) and (
5.8
), we obtain, for
\mathsf{y}\in\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}]\backslash\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}-1}]
,

\displaystyle c_{\small\ref{;42a}}\leq\big\{\sup_{{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lvert\mathsf{f}_{Q}({x})\rvert\lvert\nabla\log\mathsf{f}_{Q}({x})\rvert^{2}\big\}a_{\mathsf{r}}(Q+1),

\displaystyle c_{\small\ref{;42b}}\leq\big\{\sup_{{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lvert\mathsf{f}_{Q}({x})\rvert\lvert\Delta\Phi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x})\rvert+\sup_{{x},{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lvert\psi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},{y})\rvert N_{\mathfrak{n}}\big\}a_{\mathsf{r}}(Q+1).

(5.9)

Hence, from
(
A1
)
, (
4.5
), (
5.7
), and (
5.9
), we obtain Lemma
5.1
.
∎

The following result is a local, quench version of Lemma
4.3
and Lemma
4.5
.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
\mathsf{f}_{Q}
be as in (
4.5
).
Let
R\geq Q+1
. Then, for
\mathsf{y}\in\cup_{\mathsf{r}\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}]
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{f}_{Q}({x})\big\lvert\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},\mathsf{s})\big\rvert^{2}\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}(d{x}d\mathsf{s})\leq c_{\small\ref{;42c}}(Q,\mathsf{y}).

Proof.

(
5.7
) corresponds to (
4.6
) used in the proof of Lemma
4.3
.
The remainder of the proof of Lemma
5.2
is similar to that of Lemma
4.3
, and is therefore omitted.
∎

We prove the strong convergence of the density function of
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}
, where
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}
is defined by (
5.1
) for
N_{\mathfrak{n}}
and
\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}
. Let
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}
be the one-reduced Campbell measure of
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}
.
Let
\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}
be the Radon–Nikodym density of
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}
with respect to
\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}
, where
\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}
was defined as in Section
4
.
Such densities
\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}
exist from (
2.18
).

Let
\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R,k}^{[1]}
be as in (
4.16
). We set

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}},k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}=\big(\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}1_{\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R,k}^{[1]}}\big)\wedge l.

(5.10)

By definition,
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}=\mu(\cdot|\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}^{c}}(\mathsf{s})=\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}^{c}}(\mathsf{y}))
is a random point field on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
concentrated at
\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}^{c}}(\mathsf{y})
on
\overline{S}_{R}^{c}
.
We regard
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}
as a random point field on
\overline{S}_{R}
as well as that on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Let
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}
be the one-reduced Campbell measure of
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}
.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
R\geq Q+1
,
k\in\{0\}\cup\mathbb{N}
, and
l\in\mathbb{N}
.
For
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}
, the following hold.

i

\{\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}},k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}\}_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{N}}
is relatively compact in
L^{2}(\Lambda_{R}^{[1]})
.

ii

\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}
has a Radon-Nikodym density
\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}
with respect to
\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}

satisfying

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}},k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}=\mathsf{f}_{Q}\big(\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}1_{\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R,k}^{[1]}})\wedge l\text{ in }L^{2}(\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}).

Proof.

Lemma
5.3
follows from Lemma
5.2
using similar arguments as for the proof of Lemma
4.6
and Theorem
4.1
. Hence, the proof is omitted here.
∎

Let
\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R,k}^{[1]}
,
\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{N,[1]}
, and
\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y},k,l}^{N,[1]}
be as in (
5.10
). Let

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}_{R,\mathsf{y},k,l}^{N,[1]}

\displaystyle=\{({x},\mathsf{s})\in\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R,k}^{[1]};0<\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{N,[1]}({x},\mathsf{s})<l\},

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y},k,l}^{N}({x},\mathsf{s})

\displaystyle=1_{\mathsf{M}_{R,\mathsf{y},k,l}^{N,[1]}}({x},\mathsf{s})\nabla_{{x}}\log\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y},k,l}^{N,[1]}({x},\mathsf{s}).

Let
\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y},k,l}^{[1]}=\big(\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}1_{\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R,k}^{[1]}})\wedge l
.
We define
\mathsf{M}_{R,\mathsf{y},k,l}^{[1]}
and
{\mathsf{d}}_{R,\mathsf{y},k,l}
by

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}_{R,\mathsf{y},k,l}^{[1]}

\displaystyle=\{({x},\mathsf{s})\in\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R,k}^{[1]};0<\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}({x},\mathsf{s})<l\},

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}_{R,\mathsf{y},k,l}({x},\mathsf{s})

\displaystyle=1_{\mathsf{M}_{R,\mathsf{y},k,l}^{[1]}}({x},\mathsf{s})\nabla_{{x}}\log\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y},k,l}^{[1]}({x},\mathsf{s}).

(5.11)

Lemma 5.4
.

For
R\geq Q+1
,
k\in\{0\}\cup\mathbb{N}
,
l\in\mathbb{N}
, and
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}},k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}},k,l}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]})^{1/2}

\displaystyle={\mathsf{d}}_{R,\mathsf{y},k,l}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y},k,l}^{[1]})^{1/2}\quad\text{ weakly in $L^{2}(\Lambda_{R}^{[1]})$},

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}_{R,\mathsf{y},k,l}({x},\mathsf{s})

\displaystyle={\mathsf{d}}_{R,\mathsf{y},k,l+1}({x},\mathsf{s})\quad\text{ for }({x},\mathsf{s})\in\mathsf{M}_{R,\mathsf{y},k,l}^{[1]}.

Proof.

Lemma
5.4
follows from Lemma
5.2
and Lemma
5.3
using similar arguments as for the proof of Lemma
4.7
. Hence, the proof is omitted here.
∎

Let
\mathsf{M}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}=\{({x},\mathsf{s})|0<\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}({x},\mathsf{s})<\infty\}
and

\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})=1_{\mathsf{M}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}}({x},\mathsf{s})\nabla_{{x}}\log\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}({x},\mathsf{s})
.
From (
5.11
), we derive the following:

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s}):=\lim_{l\to\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y},k,l}({x},\mathsf{s}),\quad\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\lvert\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y},k,l}({x},\mathsf{s})\rvert\leq\lvert\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})\rvert.

(5.12)

Lemma 5.5
.

For all
R\geq Q+1
and
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\lvert\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}\rvert^{2}\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}\leq c_{\small\ref{;42c}}(Q,\mathsf{y}),

(5.13)

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]})^{1/2}=\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]})^{1/2}\quad\text{ weakly in $L^{2}(\Lambda_{R}^{[1]})$},

(5.14)

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]})^{1/2}=(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]})^{1/2}\quad\text{ in $L^{2}(\Lambda_{R}^{[1]})$}.

(5.15)

Proof.

Lemma
5.5
corresponds to Lemma
4.8
and follows from Lemma
5.2
, Lemma
5.3
, and Lemma
5.4
using the same argument as for the derivation of Lemma
4.8
from Lemma
4.5
, Lemma
4.6
, and Lemma
4.7
. Hence, the proof is omitted here.
∎

We now construct the logarithmic derivative of
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}
.

Theorem 5.1
.

Assume
(
A1
)
–
(
A3
)
. Then, for
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}
, the following hold.

i

Let
R\geq Q+1
.
For all
\mathscr{F}_{R}^{[1]}
-measurable
\mathsf{h}=(\mathsf{f}_{Q}\hat{\mathsf{f}})\otimes\mathsf{g}\in\mathscr{D}_{\circ\mathrm{b}}^{[1]}
such that
\hat{\mathsf{f}}\in C_{0}^{\infty}(S_{R})
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\lvert\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}\rvert^{2}d\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}\leq c_{\small\ref{;42c}}(Q,\mathsf{y}),

(5.16)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{h}\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}d\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}=\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{h}\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}d\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]},

(5.17)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{h}\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}d\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\nabla_{{x}}\mathsf{h}d\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}.

(5.18)

ii

For each
R\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}
is the logarithmic derivative of
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}
.

Proof.

Note that Lemma
5.2
–Lemma
5.5
correspond to Lemma
4.5
–Lemma
4.8
, respectively.
Hence, Theorem
5.1
follows from Lemma
5.2
–Lemma
5.5
using similar arguments as for the proof of Theorem
4.2
. Hence, the proof is omitted here.
∎

Remark 5.1
.

In the definition of the logarithmic derivative, the space of test functions is limited to
C_{0}^{\infty}(S_{R})\otimes\mathscr{D}_{\circ\mathrm{b}}
, which is a subset of
\mathscr{D}_{\circ\mathrm{b}}^{[1]}=C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\otimes\mathscr{D}_{\circ\mathrm{b}}
. If we use
\mathscr{D}_{\circ\mathrm{b}}^{[1]}
as the space of test functions, a distribution emerges from the boundary condition.

5.2.
Explicit formulae of the logarithmic derivative
\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}
.

Let
\mathbf{I}(k)
be as in (
2.24
).
For
\mathbf{i}\in\mathbf{I}(k)
and
{x}=({x}_{1},\ldots,{x}_{d})
, we set

{x}^{\mathbf{i}}={x}_{1}^{i_{1}}\cdots{x}_{d}^{i_{d}}
.
Let
f^{(\mathbf{i})}=\partial^{k}f/\partial{x}^{\mathbf{i}}
,
where
\partial{x}^{\mathbf{i}}=(\partial x_{1})^{i_{1}}\cdots(\partial x_{d})^{i_{d}}
.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
\ell
be as in
(
A3
)
. We set
\mathsf{y}=\sum_{i}\delta_{{y}^{i}}
. Then,

\displaystyle\max_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbf{I}(\ell+1)}\int_{\mathsf{S}}\sum_{{y}^{i}\notin S_{R+\epsilon}}^{\infty}\big\lVert(-\nabla\Psi)^{(\mathbf{i})}({x}-{y}^{i})\big\rVert_{C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R})}d\mu(\mathsf{y})<\infty.

(5.19)

Proof.

According to (
1.1
) and
\mathbf{i}\in\mathbf{I}(\ell+1)
, (
5.19
) follows from

\displaystyle\int_{\mathsf{S}}\sum_{{y}^{i}\notin S_{R+\epsilon}}^{\infty}\sup_{{x}\in\overline{S}_{R}}\frac{1}{\lvert{x}-{y}^{i}\rvert^{d+\ell}}d\mu(\mathsf{y})<\infty.

(5.20)

By
(
A3
)
,
\ell>\ell_{0}-d
.
Hence, from (
2.22
) and (
5.20
), we obtain (
5.19
).
∎

Let
\mathbb{I}(\ell)=\sqcup_{k=0}^{\ell}\mathbf{I}(k)
as in Theorem
2.1
.
Recall that, from (
2.14
),
-\nabla_{{x}}\Psi^{N}({x},{y})=0
for
({x},{y})\notin\overline{O_{N}\times O_{N}}
.
Using Taylor’s expansion of
-\nabla_{{x}}\Psi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},{y})
and
-\nabla\Psi({x}-{y})
in
{x}

at the origin
{x}=0
for each
|{y}|>R
and
({x},{y})\in O_{N}\times O_{N}
, we obtain, for
|{x}|\leq R
,

\displaystyle-\nabla_{{x}}\Psi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},{y})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)}\frac{{x}^{\mathbf{i}}}{i_{1}!\cdots i_{d}!}(-\nabla_{{x}}\Psi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}})^{(\mathbf{i})}(0,{y})+\mathscr{Q}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n}}({x},{y}),

\displaystyle-\nabla\Psi({x}-{y})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)}\frac{{x}^{\mathbf{i}}}{i_{1}!\cdots i_{d}!}(-\nabla\Psi)^{(\mathbf{i})}(0-{y})+\mathscr{Q}^{\ell}({x},{y}),

(5.21)

where
\mathscr{Q}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n}}
and
\mathscr{Q}^{\ell}
are the residual terms of Taylor’s expansion defined by

\displaystyle\mathscr{Q}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n}}({x},{y})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbf{I}(\ell+1)}\frac{{x}^{\mathbf{i}}}{i_{1}!\cdots i_{d}!}(-\nabla_{{x}}\Psi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}})^{(\mathbf{i})}(\xi_{{x}}^{\mathfrak{n}},{y}),

\displaystyle\mathscr{Q}^{\ell}({x},{y})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbf{I}(\ell+1)}\frac{{x}^{\mathbf{i}}}{i_{1}!\cdots i_{d}!}(-\nabla\Psi)^{(\mathbf{i})}(\xi_{{x}}-{y}),

(5.22)

where
\xi_{{x}}^{\mathfrak{n}}
and
\xi_{{x}}\in S_{R}
are such that
\lvert\xi_{{x}}^{\mathfrak{n}}\rvert,\lvert\xi_{{x}}\rvert<\lvert{x}\rvert
.
Using (
2.26
), we can and do take
\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\xi_{{x}}^{\mathfrak{n}}=\xi_{{x}}
in
C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R})
and

\mathscr{Q}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n}}({x},{y}),\ \mathscr{Q}^{\ell}({x},{y})\in C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R})
for each
|{y}|>R
.

Let
\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}
,
\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n}}
,
\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell}
, and
\mathscr{R}_{R+\epsilon,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell}
be such that, for
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}=\sum_{i}\delta_{{y}^{i}}
,

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}({x})

\displaystyle=\sum_{{y}^{i}\not\in\overline{S}_{R}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}\mathscr{Q}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n}}({x},{y}^{i}),\quad\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n}}({x})=\sum_{{y}^{i}\not\in\overline{S}_{R}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}\mathscr{Q}^{\ell}({x},{y}^{i}),

\displaystyle\quad\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell}({x})

\displaystyle=\sum_{{y}^{i}\not\in\overline{S}_{R}}^{\infty}\mathscr{Q}^{\ell}({x},{y}^{i}),\quad\mathscr{R}_{R+\epsilon,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell}({x})=\sum_{{y}^{i}\notin\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R+\epsilon}}^{\infty}\mathscr{Q}^{\ell}({x},{y}^{i}).

(5.23)

Proposition 5.1
.

i

For each
R\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}=\sum_{i}\delta_{{y}^{i}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell}=\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n}}=\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}\quad\text{ in }C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R}),

(5.24)

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell}=\mathscr{R}_{R,\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}^{c}}(\mathsf{y})}^{\ell},

(5.25)

\displaystyle\lim_{R\to\infty}\lVert\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell}\rVert_{C^{1}(\overline{S}_{Q})}=0\quad\text{ for each $Q\in\mathbb{N}$}.

(5.26)

ii

For each
R\in\mathbb{N}
and
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathsf{S}}\lVert\mathscr{R}_{R+\epsilon,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell}({x})\rVert_{C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R})}d\mu(\mathsf{y})<\infty.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{y}=\sum_{i}\delta_{{y}^{i}}
. From (
5.22
) and (
2.27
), we have, for each
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathsf{S}}\sum_{{y}^{i}\notin S_{R+\epsilon}}^{\infty}\sup_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{N}}\lVert\mathscr{Q}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n}}({x},{y}^{i})\rVert_{C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R})}d\mu(\mathsf{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathsf{S}}\sum_{{y}^{i}\notin S_{R+\epsilon}}^{\infty}\sup_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{N}}\big\lVert\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbf{I}(\ell+1)}\frac{{x}^{\mathbf{i}}}{i_{1}!\cdots i_{d}!}(-\nabla_{{x}}\Psi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}})^{(\mathbf{i})}(\xi_{{x}}^{\mathfrak{n}},{y}^{i})\big\rVert_{C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R})}d\mu(\mathsf{y})\quad\text{ by \eqref{:35r}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbf{I}(\ell+1)}\frac{R^{\ell+1}}{i_{1}!\cdots i_{d}!}\int_{\mathsf{S}}\sum_{{y}^{i}\notin S_{R+\epsilon}}^{\infty}\sup_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{N}}\big\lVert(-\nabla_{{x}}\Psi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}})^{(\mathbf{i})}(\xi_{{x}}^{\mathfrak{n}},{y}^{i})\big\rVert_{C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R})}d\mu(\mathsf{y})<\infty\quad\text{by \eqref{:11j}}.

Hence, for
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}=\sum_{i}\delta_{{y}^{i}}
and each
\epsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{{y}^{i}\notin S_{R+\epsilon}}^{\infty}\sup_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{N}}\lVert\mathscr{Q}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n}}({x},{y}^{i})\rVert_{C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R})}<\infty.

(5.27)

Because (
5.27
) holds for all
\epsilon>0
, we deduce that, for
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}=\sum_{i}\delta_{{y}^{i}}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{{y}^{i}\not\in\overline{S}_{R}}^{\infty}\sup_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{N}}\lVert\mathscr{Q}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n}}({x},{y}^{i})\rVert_{C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R})}<\infty.

(5.28)

From (
5.28
), the infinite sum
\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}
converges absolutely in
C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R})
.

From (
5.22
) and (
5.19
), we obtain, for each
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathsf{S}}\sum_{{y}^{i}\notin S_{R+\epsilon}}^{\infty}\lVert\mathscr{Q}^{\ell}({x},{y}^{i})\rVert_{C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R})}d\mu(\mathsf{y})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{S}}\sum_{{y}^{i}\notin S_{R+\epsilon}}^{\infty}\Big\lVert\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbf{I}(\ell+1)}\frac{{x}^{\mathbf{i}}}{i_{1}!\cdots i_{d}!}(-\nabla\Psi)^{(\mathbf{i})}(\xi_{{x}}-{y}^{i}){x}^{\mathbf{i}}\Big\rVert_{C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R})}d\mu(\mathsf{y})\quad\text{ by \eqref{:35r}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbf{I}(\ell+1)}\frac{R^{\ell+1}}{i_{1}!\cdots i_{d}!}\int_{\mathsf{S}}\sum_{{y}^{i}\notin S_{R+\epsilon}}^{\infty}\big\lVert(-\nabla\Psi)^{(\mathbf{i})}({x}-{y}^{i})\big\rVert_{C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R})}d\mu(\mathsf{y})

\displaystyle<\infty\quad\text{by \eqref{:11k}}.

(5.29)

Hence, similarly as (
5.28
), we deduce, for
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}=\sum_{i}\delta_{{y}^{i}}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{{y}^{i}\not\in\overline{S}_{R}}^{\infty}\lVert\mathscr{Q}^{\ell}({x},{y}^{i})\rVert_{C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R})}<\infty,

(5.30)

which yields, for
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}=\sum_{i}\delta_{{y}^{i}}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{R\to\infty}\sum_{{y}^{i}\not\in\overline{S}_{R}}^{\infty}\lVert\mathscr{Q}^{\ell}({x},{y}^{i})\rVert_{C^{1}(\overline{S}_{Q})}=0.

This implies (
5.26
).
From (
5.30
), the infinite sums
\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell}
and
\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n}}
converge absolutely in
C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R})
, which implies the first equality in (
5.24
).

Using (
5.23
), (
5.22
), (
2.29
), and
\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\xi_{{x}}^{\mathfrak{n}}=\xi_{{x}}
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\lVert\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}({x})-\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n}}({x})\rVert_{C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R})}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\sum_{{y}^{i}\not\in\overline{S}_{R}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}\lVert\mathscr{Q}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n}}({x},{y}^{i})-\mathscr{Q}^{\ell}({x},{y}^{i})\rVert_{C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R})}\quad\text{ by \eqref{:35z}}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbf{I}(\ell+1)}\frac{{x}^{\mathbf{i}}}{i_{1}!\cdots i_{d}!}\sum_{{y}^{i}\not\in\overline{S}_{R}}^{\infty}\lVert(-\nabla\Psi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}})^{(\mathbf{i})}(\xi_{{x}}^{\mathfrak{n}},{y}^{i})-(-\nabla\Psi)^{(\mathbf{i})}(\xi_{{x}}-{y}^{i})\rVert_{C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R})}

\displaystyle=0\quad\quad\quad\text{ by \eqref{:11p}, }\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\xi_{{x}}^{\mathfrak{n}}=\xi_{{x}}\text{ in }C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R}).

(5.31)

Here, we used (
5.22
) for the third line. (
5.31
) yields the second equality in (
5.24
).

From (
5.23
),
\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n}}
is independent of
\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}}(\mathsf{y})
. Hence, (
5.24
) yields (
5.25
).
Thus, we have obtained i.

From (
5.23
) and (
5.29
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathsf{S}}\lVert\mathscr{R}_{R+\epsilon,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell}({x})\rVert_{C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R})}d\mu(\mathsf{y})\leq\int_{\mathsf{S}}\sum_{{y}^{i}\notin S_{R+\epsilon}}^{\infty}\lVert\mathscr{Q}^{\ell}({x},{y}^{i})\rVert_{C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R})}d\mu(\mathsf{y})<\infty.

This implies ii.
∎

For
\mathbf{i}=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{d})\in\sqcup_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{I}(k)

and
\mathsf{y}=\sum_{i}\delta_{{y}^{i}}
, we set

\displaystyle\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}=\Big(\frac{1}{i_{1}!\cdots i_{d}!}\Big)\sum_{{y}^{i}\not\in\overline{S}_{R}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}(-\nabla_{{x}}\Psi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}})^{(\mathbf{i})}(0,{y}^{i}),

\displaystyle\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n}}=\Big(\frac{1}{i_{1}!\cdots i_{d}!}\Big)\sum_{{y}^{i}\not\in\overline{S}_{R}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}(-\nabla\Psi)^{(\mathbf{i})}(0-{y}^{i}).

(5.32)

Let
\mathbb{I}(\ell)=\sqcup_{k=0}^{\ell}\mathbf{I}(k)
as in Theorem
2.1
.
From (
5.21
)–(
5.23
) and (
5.32
),

\displaystyle\sum_{{y}^{i}\not\in\overline{S}_{R}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}-\nabla_{{x}}\Psi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},{y}^{i})=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)}\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}{x}^{\mathbf{i}}+\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}({x}),

(5.33)

\displaystyle\sum_{{y}^{i}\not\in\overline{S}_{R}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}-\nabla\Psi({x}-{y}^{i})=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)}\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n}}{x}^{\mathbf{i}}+\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n}}({x}).

(5.34)

Proposition 5.2
.

For each
\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)
,
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}=\sum_{i}\delta_{{y}^{i}}
such that
\mathsf{y}(\overline{S}_{R})\geq 1
, and each
R\in\mathbb{N}
, we have a finite limit
\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i}}
such that

\displaystyle\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i}}=\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}=\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n}},

(5.35)

\displaystyle\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i}}=\eta_{R,\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}^{c}}(\mathsf{y})}^{\mathbf{i}},

(5.36)

\displaystyle\lim_{R\to\infty}\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i}}=0.

(5.37)

Proof.

We first prove the existence of a finite
\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i}}
. From (
5.33
) and (
5.5
),

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\Big(\beta\Big(\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)}\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}{x}^{\mathbf{i}}\Big)-\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},\mathsf{s})\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\Big(\beta\Big(-\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}({x})+\sum_{{y}^{i}\not\in\overline{S}_{R}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}-\nabla_{{x}}\Psi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},{y}^{i})\Big)-\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},\mathsf{s})\Big)\quad\text{ by \eqref{:35v} }

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\beta\Big(-\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}({x})+\nabla\Phi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x})+\sum_{s^{i}\in\overline{S}_{R}}\nabla_{{x}}\Psi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},s^{i})\Big)\quad\text{ by \eqref{:32t} }

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta\Big(-\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell}({x})+\nabla\Phi({x})+\sum_{s^{i}\in\overline{S}_{R}}\nabla_{{x}}\Psi({x},s^{i})\Big)\quad\text{by \eqref{:35c}, \eqref{:11e}, \eqref{:11f}}.

(5.38)

The convergence in (
5.38
) holds in
C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R,\epsilon}(\mathsf{s}))
for each
\epsilon>0
and all
\mathsf{s}
such that
\mathsf{s}=\sum_{s^{i}\in\overline{S}_{R}}\delta_{s^{i}}
, where
\overline{S}_{R,\epsilon}(\mathsf{s})=\{{x}\in\overline{S}_{R};\lvert{x}-s^{i}\rvert\geq\epsilon\text{ for all }i\}
.

From Lemma
5.5
and (
5.38
), we obtain weakly in
L^{1}(\Lambda_{R}^{[1]})

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\beta\Big(\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)}\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}{x}^{\mathbf{i}}\Big)\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}=\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\beta\Big(\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)}\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}{x}^{\mathbf{i}}\Big)\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}\quad\text{ by \eqref{:3)b}}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\Big(\beta\Big(\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)}\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}{x}^{\mathbf{i}}\Big)-\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},\mathsf{s})+\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},\mathsf{s})\Big)\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}

\displaystyle=\Big(\beta\Big(-\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell}({x})+\nabla\Phi({x})+\sum_{s^{i}\in\overline{S}_{R}}\nabla_{{x}}\Psi({x},s^{i})\Big)+\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})\Big)\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}.

(5.39)

Here, we used (
5.14
) and (
5.38
) for the last line.

From
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}(\overline{S}_{R}\times\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R})>0
, we have
\int_{\overline{S}_{R}\times\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R}}\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}d\Lambda_{R}^{[1]}>0
. Hence, the elements
\{\beta{x}^{\mathbf{i}}\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]};\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)\}

are linearly independent in
L^{1}(\Lambda_{R}^{[1]})
.
From this and (
5.39
), each numerical sequence
\{\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}\}_{\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{N}}
has a finite limit
\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i}}
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)}\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}\big(\beta{x}^{\mathbf{i}}\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}\big)=\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)}\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i}}\big(\beta{x}^{\mathbf{i}}\mathsf{f}_{Q}\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}\big),

which implies the first equality in (
5.35
), that is,

\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}=\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i}}
for each
\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)
.

Let
\mathscr{X}_{R}(\ell)
and
{x}_{\mathbf{j}}^{\mathbf{i}}
for
{x}\in\mathscr{X}_{R}(\ell)

be as in (
2.25
).
Then, for each
\mathbf{j}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)}(\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}-\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n}}){x}_{\mathbf{j}}^{\mathbf{i}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\Big(\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)}\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}{x}_{\mathbf{j}}^{\mathbf{i}}+\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}({x})\Big)-\Big(\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)}\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n}}{x}_{\mathbf{j}}^{\mathbf{i}}+\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n}}({x})\Big)\quad\text{ by \eqref{:35c}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\sum_{{y}^{i}\not\in\overline{S}_{R}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}\Big(-\nabla_{{x}}\Psi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},{y}^{i})+\nabla\Psi({x}-{y}^{i})\Big)\quad\text{ by \eqref{:35v}, \eqref{:35y}}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle 0\quad\text{by \eqref{:11o}}.

(5.40)

Let
\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{j}}=({x}_{\mathbf{j}}^{\mathbf{i}})_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)}
for
\mathbf{j}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)
.
Then the vectors
\{\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{j}}\}_{\mathbf{j}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)}
are linearly independent in
\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{I}(\ell)}

for each
{x}\in\mathscr{X}_{R}(\ell)
by (
2.25
).
Hence, using (
5.40
), we find that, for each
\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)
, the numerical sequence
\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n}}-\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}
converges to zero as
\mathfrak{n}\to\infty
for
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}
. From this and
\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}=\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i}}<\infty
for each
\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)
, we obtain (
5.35
).

Clearly,
\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n}}
and
\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}
are independent of
\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}}(\mathsf{y})
.
Hence, (
5.35
) yields (
5.36
).

From (
5.32
) and (
5.35
), we have

\displaystyle\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i}}=\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n}}=\Big(\frac{1}{i_{1}!\cdots i_{d}!}\Big)\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\sum_{{y}^{i}\not\in\overline{S}_{R}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}(-\nabla\Psi)^{(\mathbf{i})}(0-{y}^{i}).

(5.41)

Because the sum in (
5.41
) converges, we deduce

\displaystyle\lim_{R\to\infty}\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i}}=\Big(\frac{1}{i_{1}!\cdots i_{d}!}\Big)\lim_{R\to\infty}\Big(\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\sum_{{y}^{i}\not\in\overline{S}_{R}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}(-\nabla\Psi)^{(\mathbf{i})}(0-{y}^{i})\Big)=0.

This implies (
5.37
).
∎

Lemma 5.7
.

For
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}=\sum_{i}\delta_{{y}^{i}}
and each
R\in\mathbb{N}
, we have the following:

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\sum_{{y}^{i}\not\in\overline{S}_{R}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}-\nabla_{{x}}\Psi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},{y}^{i})=\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)}\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i}}{x}^{\mathbf{i}}+\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell}({x})\quad\text{in $C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R})$}.

(5.42)

Proof.

From (
5.33
), (
5.35
), and (
5.24
), we have (
5.42
).
∎

Let
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}=\mu(\cdot|\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}^{c}}(\mathsf{s})=\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}^{c}}(\mathsf{y}))
.
Let
\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}
be the logarithmic derivative of
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}
.
Let
\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
be the configuration space over
\overline{S}_{R}
.
We write
\mathsf{s}=\sum_{i}\delta_{s^{i}}
.

Theorem 5.2
.

For each
R\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}
, the following hold.

i

For all
({x},\mathsf{s})\in\overline{S}_{R}\times\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
such that
{x}\neq s^{i}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})=\beta\Big\{-\nabla\Phi({x})-\sum_{s^{i}\in\overline{S}_{R}}\nabla\Psi({x}-s^{i})+\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)}\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i}}{x}^{\mathbf{i}}+\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell}({x})\Big\}.

(5.43)

ii

Let
\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R,\epsilon}^{[1]}=\{({x},\mathsf{s})\in\overline{S}_{R}\times\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R};\lvert{x}-s^{i}\rvert\geq\epsilon\}
.
Then, for each
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})=\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},\mathsf{s})\quad\text{ in $C^{1}(\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R,\epsilon}^{[1]})$}.

(5.44)

Here,
\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R,\epsilon}^{[1]}
is regarded as a subset of
\overline{S}_{R,\epsilon}^{1+m}=\{(t^{i})_{i=1}^{1+m};\lvert t^{i}-t^{j}\rvert\geq\epsilon,i\neq j\}
, where
m=\mathsf{s}(\overline{S}_{R})
, and
C^{1}(\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R,\epsilon}^{[1]})
is imposed the norm given by the norm of
C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R,\epsilon}^{1+m})
.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{h}=\mathsf{f}\otimes\mathsf{g}\in C_{0}^{\infty}(S_{R})\otimes\mathscr{D}_{\circ\mathrm{b}}
be an
\mathscr{F}_{R}^{[1]}
-measurable function such that

\mathsf{g}(\mathsf{s})=0
on
\bigsqcup_{m=M}^{\infty}\mathsf{S}_{R}^{m}
for some
M\in\mathbb{N}
and that

\mathsf{h}({x},\mathsf{s})=0
for
({x},\mathsf{s})
satisfying
\lvert{x}-s^{i}\rvert\leq\epsilon
for some
i
, where

\mathsf{s}=\sum_{i}\delta_{s^{i}}
,
\epsilon>0
, and
\mathsf{S}=\bigsqcup_{m=0}^{\infty}\mathsf{S}_{R}^{m}
from (
2.2
).
Then, for
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}=\sum_{i}\delta_{{y}^{i}}
,

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{s^{i}\in\overline{S}_{R}}-\nabla\Psi({x}-s^{i})\Big)\mathsf{h}({x},\mathsf{s})\in C_{0}^{\infty}(S_{R})\otimes\mathscr{D}_{\circ\mathrm{b}}.

(5.45)

Let
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}
and
\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}
be as in (
5.2
) and (
5.3
) with
N=N_{\mathfrak{n}}
, respectively.
Then, from (
2.12
), (
2.15
), (
5.15
), and (
5.45
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\Big(\nabla\Phi({x})+\sum_{s^{i}\in\overline{S}_{R}}\nabla\Psi({x}-s^{i})\Big)\mathsf{h}({x},\mathsf{s})d\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\Big(\nabla\Phi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x})+\sum_{s^{i}\in\overline{S}_{R}}\nabla_{{x}}\Psi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},s^{i})\Big)\mathsf{h}({x},\mathsf{s})d\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}.

(5.46)

Hence, using (
5.17
) and (
5.46
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\Big(\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})+\beta\Big\{\nabla\Phi({x})+\sum_{s^{i}\in\overline{S}_{R}}\nabla\Psi({x}-s^{i})\Big\}\Big)\mathsf{h}({x},\mathsf{s})d\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\Big(\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},\mathsf{s})+\beta\Big\{\nabla\Phi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x})+\sum_{s^{i}\in\overline{S}_{R}}\nabla_{{x}}\Psi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},s^{i})\Big\}\Big)\mathsf{h}({x},\mathsf{s})d\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\beta\Big(-\sum_{{y}^{i}\not\in\overline{S}_{R}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}\nabla_{{x}}\Psi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},{y}^{i})\Big)\mathsf{h}({x},\mathsf{s})d\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},[1]}\quad\text{ by \eqref{:32t}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\beta\Big(\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)}\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i}}{x}^{\mathbf{i}}+\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell}({x})\Big)\mathsf{h}({x},\mathsf{s})d\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}\Big\}\quad\text{by \eqref{:3)b}, Lemma~\ref{l:3'}}.

Hence, we obtain (
5.43
) for
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}
-a.s.
({x},\mathsf{s})
. The right-hand side of (
5.43
) is continuous on

\{({x},\mathsf{s})\in\overline{S}_{R}\times\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R};{x}\neq s^{i}\}
, where
\mathsf{s}=\sum_{i}\delta_{s^{i}}
.
Hence, there exists a
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}
-version of
\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})
such that
(
5.43
) holds for all
\{({x},\mathsf{s})\in\overline{S}_{R}\times\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R};{x}\neq s^{i}\}
. This yields i.

We set
\mathsf{s}=\sum_{i}\delta_{s^{i}}
.
For
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}
, from (
5.5
) and (
5.33
), we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},\mathsf{s})

\displaystyle=\beta\Big\{-\nabla\Phi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x})-\sum_{s^{i}\in\overline{S}_{R}}\nabla_{{x}}\Psi^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},s^{i})+\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)}\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i},\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}{x}^{\mathbf{i}}+\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell,\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}}({x})\Big\}.

Hence, using (
2.12
), (
2.15
), Proposition
5.1
, and Proposition
5.2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathfrak{n}\to\infty}\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},\mathsf{s})

\displaystyle=\beta\Big\{-\nabla\Phi({x})-\sum_{s^{i}\in\overline{S}_{R}}\nabla\Psi({x}-s^{i})+\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)}\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i}}{x}^{\mathbf{i}}+\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell}({x})\Big\}

in
C^{1}(\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R,\epsilon}^{[1]})
for each
\epsilon>0
.
From this and (
5.43
), we obtain (
5.44
). This yields ii.
∎

5.3.
Explicit formula of
\mathsf{d}^{\mu_{\mathsf{y}}}
and
\mathsf{d}^{\mu}
: Proof of Theorem
2.1
.

Let
\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}
be as in (
5.12
). Then, from Theorem
5.1
,
\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}
is the logarithmic derivative of
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}
.
The consistency of
\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}
in the following lemma is crucial for demonstrating the explicit expression of the logarithmic derivatives of
\mu
in Theorem
2.1
.

Lemma 5.8
.

For
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}
and each
R\in\mathbb{N}
, we have the following:

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})=\mathsf{d}_{R+1,\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s}+\pi_{\overline{S}_{R+1}\backslash\overline{S}_{R}}(\mathsf{y}))

(5.47)

for all
({x},\mathsf{s})\in\overline{S}_{R}\times\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
such that
{x}\neq s^{i}
for each
i
, where
\mathsf{s}=\sum_{s^{i}\in\overline{S}_{R}}\delta_{s^{i}}
.

Proof.

From (
5.5
), we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},\mathsf{s})=\mathsf{d}_{R+1,\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}({x},\mathsf{s}+\pi_{\overline{S}_{R+1}\backslash\overline{S}_{R}}(\mathsf{y}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}})).

(5.48)

Hence, applying (
5.44
) to (
5.48
), we obtain (
5.47
).
∎

Let
\mu_{\mathsf{y}}=\mu(\,\cdot\,|\mathscr{T}(\mathsf{S}))(\mathsf{y})
be the tail decomposition of
\mu
as in Definition
2.3
.
Let
\mu^{[1]}_{\mathsf{y}}
be the one-reduced Campbell measure of
\mu_{\mathsf{y}}
.

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
\|h\|:=\|h\|_{L^{1}(S_{Q}\times\mathsf{S},\mu_{\mathsf{y}}^{[1]})}+\|\nabla_{{x}}h\|_{L^{1}(S_{Q}\times\mathsf{S},\mu_{\mathsf{y}}^{[1]})}
.
Then, the set
\mathscr{D}_{\circ\mathrm{b}}^{[1]}=C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\otimes\mathscr{D}_{\circ\mathrm{b}}
is dense in
\mathscr{D}_{\bullet\mathrm{b}}^{[1]}=C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\otimes\mathscr{D}_{\bullet\mathrm{b}}
with respect to
\|\cdot\|
for each
Q\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

From the proof of Lemma 2.4 in
[
22
]
, we easily see that both
\mathscr{D}_{\circ\mathrm{b}}
and
\mathscr{D}_{\bullet\mathrm{b}}
are dense in
L^{2}(\mu_{\mathsf{y}})
.
Hence, we can prove Lemma
5.9
from this.
∎

From (
5.47
),
\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})
has a limit as
R\to\infty
, where

\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})
is regarded as a function on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}
by
\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s}):=\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}({x},\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}}(\mathsf{s}))
.
For
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}
and
\mu^{[1]}_{\mathsf{y}}
-a.e.
({x},\mathsf{s})
, we define the limit
\mathfrak{d}_{\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})
by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}_{\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{s}}({x},\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}}(\mathsf{s})).

(5.49)

According to (
5.47
),
\mathfrak{d}_{\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})
is well defined. Indeed, from (
5.47
), we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{s}}({x},\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}}(\mathsf{s}))=\mathsf{d}_{R+1,\mathsf{s}}({x},\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}}(\mathsf{s})+\pi_{\overline{S}_{R+1}\backslash\overline{S}_{R}}(\mathsf{s}))=\mathsf{d}_{R+1,\mathsf{s}}({x},\pi_{\overline{S}_{R+1}}(\mathsf{s})).

We introduce the equivalence relation in
\mathsf{S}_{0}
such that

\mathsf{a}\sim_{\mathscr{T}}\mathsf{b}
if and only if (
2.51
) holds.
Let
[\mathsf{y}]=\{\mathsf{z}\in\mathsf{S}_{0};\mathsf{y}\sim_{\mathscr{T}}\mathsf{z}\}
for
\mathsf{y}\in\mathsf{S}_{0}
and

\mathsf{S}^{[1]}(\mathsf{y})=\{({x},\mathsf{s});\delta_{x}+\mathsf{s}\in[\mathsf{y}]\}
.

We take a version of
\mathfrak{d}_{\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})
such that, for any
\mathsf{y}\in\mathsf{S}_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}_{\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})=\mathfrak{d}_{\mathsf{z}}({x},\mathsf{s})\quad\text{ for all }\mathsf{z}\in[\mathsf{y}],({x},\mathsf{s})\in\mathsf{S}^{[1]}(\mathsf{y}).

We also set
\mathfrak{d}_{\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})=0
on
(\cup_{\mathsf{y}\in\mathsf{S}_{0}}\mathsf{S}^{[1]}(\mathsf{y}))^{c}
.
Because the equivalence relation
\sim_{\mathscr{T}}
gives the partition of
\mathsf{S}_{0}
and
\mu(\mathsf{S}_{0})=1
, such a version of
\mathfrak{d}_{\mathsf{y}}
is well defined for all
({x},\mathsf{s})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}
. Thus, we set for any
\mathsf{y}\in\mathsf{S}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}({x},\mathsf{s})=\mathfrak{d}_{\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})\quad\text{ for }({x},\mathsf{s})\in[\mathsf{y}].

(5.50)

We now provide an explicit expression for the logarithmic derivative of
\mu
.

Theorem 5.3
.

Let
\mathsf{d}^{\mu}
be the logarithmic derivative of
\mu
given by Theorem
4.3
.

i

\mathsf{d}^{\mu}
becomes the
\bullet
-logarithmic derivative of
\mu
.

ii
The
\bullet
-logarithmic derivatives
\mathsf{d}^{\mu_{\mathsf{y}}}
and
\mathsf{d}^{\mu}
of
\mu_{\mathsf{y}}
and
\mu
are given by

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}^{\mu_{\mathsf{y}}}({x},\mathsf{s})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{d}_{\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})\quad\text{for $\mu$-a.e.\,$\mathsf{y}$ and $\mu_{\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}$-a.e.}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{d}({x},\mathsf{s})\quad\text{ for $\mu$-a.e.\,$\mathsf{y}$ and }({x},\mathsf{s})\in\mathsf{S}^{[1]}(\mathsf{y}),

(5.51)

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}^{\mu}({x},\mathsf{s})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{d}({x},\mathsf{s})\quad\text{for $\mu^{[1]}$-a.e.}

(5.52)

iii

For
\mu^{[1]}
-a.e.
({x},\mathsf{s})
, where
\mathsf{s}=\sum_{i}\delta_{s^{i}}
, and for each
R\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}^{\mu}({x},\mathsf{s})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{s}}({x},\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}}(\mathsf{s}))

\displaystyle=\beta\Big(-\nabla\Phi({x})-\sum_{s^{i}\in\overline{S}_{R}}\nabla\Psi({x}-s^{i})+\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)}\eta_{R,\mathsf{s}}^{\mathbf{i}}{x}^{\mathbf{i}}+\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{s}}^{\ell}({x})\Big).

(5.53)

Proof.

From (
5.18
) and (
5.49
), we have that, for all
\mathsf{h}\in\mathscr{D}_{\circ\mathrm{b}}^{[1]}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{h}\mathfrak{d}_{\mathsf{y}}d\mu_{\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\nabla_{{x}}\mathsf{h}d\mu_{\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}.

(5.54)

Then, from Lemma
5.9
, (
5.54
) holds for all
\mathsf{h}\in\mathscr{D}_{\bullet\mathrm{b}}^{[1]}
, which implies
\mathfrak{d}_{\mathsf{y}}
is a
\bullet
-logarithmic derivative of
\mu_{\mathsf{y}}
. Thus, we have the first equality of (
5.51
).
The second equality of (
5.51
) follows from (
5.50
).
From
\mu^{[1]}=\int_{\mathsf{S}}\mu_{\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}\mu(d\mathsf{y})
and (
5.54
), we have that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{h}\mathfrak{d}d\mu^{[1]}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\nabla_{{x}}\mathsf{h}d\mu^{[1]}\quad\text{ for all }\mathsf{h}\in\mathscr{D}_{\bullet\mathrm{b}}^{[1]}.

(5.55)

Thus,
\mathfrak{d}
is the
\bullet
-logarithmic derivative of
\mu
from (
5.55
).
From (
4.40
) and (
5.55
),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{h}\mathsf{d}^{\mu}d\mu^{[1]}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathsf{h}\mathfrak{d}d\mu^{[1]}\quad\text{ for all }\mathsf{h}\in\mathscr{D}_{\circ\mathrm{b}}^{[1]}.

(5.56)

From Lemma
5.9
, (
5.55
) and (
5.56
), we have
\mathsf{d}^{\mu}=\mathfrak{d}
for
\mu^{[1]}
-a.e.
({x},\mathsf{s})
, which implies (
5.52
).
Hence,
\mathsf{d}^{\mu}
is the
\bullet
-logarithmic derivative of
\mu
according to
\mathsf{d}^{\mu}=\mathfrak{d}
.
We have thus proved i and ii.
From (
5.49
) and (
5.52
), we have, for
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}
and
\mu^{[1]}_{\mathsf{y}}
-a.e.
({x},\mathsf{s})
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}^{\mu}({x},\mathsf{s})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{s}}({x},\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}}(\mathsf{s})).

(5.57)

From (
5.43
) and (
5.57
), we obtain (
5.53
).
This completes the proof of iii.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.1
.

We obtain the
\bullet
-logarithmic derivative
\mathsf{d}^{\mu}\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{2}(\mu^{[1]})
given by (
2.33
) from Theorem
4.3
and Theorem
5.3
.
