\overline{\mathbf{X}}=(\overline{X}^{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
under
\overline{P}_{\varphi\mu\circ\mathfrak{l}^{-1}}^{\mathbb{N}}
is a weak solution of (
2.41
) with initial distribution
\varphi\mu\circ\mathfrak{l}^{-1}
that satisfies
(
SIN
)
,
(
NBJ
)
, and
(
AC
)
μ
.

Proof.

From (
9.16
), the family of distributions
\overline{P}_{\mathfrak{l}^{[m]}(\mathsf{s})}^{\mu^{[m]}}
is consistent.
Hence, using the Kolmogorov construction theorem, we obtain i.

Let
\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{[m]}
be as in Theorem
9.1
. From Theorem
9.1
, the first
m
-component
\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{m}
of the
m
-labeled process
\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{[m]}
is a weak solution of (
2.41
) for
1\leq i\leq m
.
From (
9.17
),
\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{[m]}
are consistent in
m\in\{0\}\cup\mathbb{N}
.
Hence,
\overline{\mathbf{X}}
is a weak solution of ISDE (
2.41
).

From (
9.3
), for
\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{[m]}
, none of
m
-labeled particles do not explode or collide with other labeled particles for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
.
Hence, combining this with the consistency (
9.17
), each tagged particle
\overline{X}^{i}
of
\overline{\mathbf{X}}=(\overline{X}^{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
neither explodes nor collides with the others.
We have thus obtained
(
SIN
)
.

We obtain
(
NBJ
)
from the martingale decomposition (
9.4
).
The detail of the proof of
(
NBJ
)
is similar to that of Theorem
8.2
. Hence, it is omitted here.

Finally,
(
AC
)
μ
is obvious because the unlabeled dynamics
\overline{\mathsf{X}}=\mathfrak{u}_{\mathrm{path}}^{[0]}(\overline{\mathbf{X}})
are associated with the Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(\mu)
, where
\overline{\mathsf{X}}_{t}=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\delta_{\overline{X}_{t}^{i}}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.2
.

From Theorem
9.1
,
\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{[m]}
is a
\mu^{[m]}
-symmetric, conservative diffusion for each
m\in\{0\}\cup\mathbb{N}
.
From Theorem
9.2
, we have a fully labeled process
\overline{\mathbf{X}}
associated with
\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{[m]}
and
\overline{\mathbf{X}}
is a weak solution of (
2.41
) with initial distribution
\varphi\mu\circ\mathfrak{l}^{-1}
. Here, we can take any function
\varphi
satisfying (
2.61
). Then, using Fubini’s theorem, we obtain
\overline{\mathbf{X}}
such that
\overline{\mathbf{X}}
is a weak solution of (
2.41
) starting at
\mathfrak{l}(\mathsf{s})
for
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{s}
. This completes the proof.
∎

10.
Unique strong solutions of ISDEs: Proofs of Theorem
2.3

To prove Theorem
2.3
, we utilize a general result developed in
[
34
,
16
]
.
A key assumption for the uniqueness of solutions is the tail triviality of the associated random point fields.
Starting from the tail decomposition of
\mu
, we introduce the tail decomposition of Dirichlet forms in Section
10.1
.
Using this, we complete the proof of Theorem
2.3
in Section
10.2
.

10.1.
Tail decomposition of Dirichlet forms and weak solution.

Let
\mu_{\mathsf{a}}

(\mathsf{a}\in\mathsf{S}_{0})
be the tail decomposition given in Definition
2.3
.
Then
\mu=\int\mu_{\mathsf{a}}d\mu(\mathsf{a})
,
\mathsf{S}_{0}=\sum_{\mathsf{a}}[\mathsf{a}]
, and
\mu(\mathsf{S}\backslash\mathsf{S}_{0})=0
.
For all
R\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{a}
, and
\mu_{\mathsf{a}}
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}
, we define

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}=\mu_{\mathsf{a}}(\cdot|\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}^{c}}(\mathsf{s})=\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}^{c}}(\mathsf{y})).

Let
\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R}^{k}=\{\mathsf{s}\in\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R};\mathsf{s}(\overline{S}_{R})=k\}
, where
\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R}
is the configuration space over
\overline{S}_{R}
.

Theorem 10.1
.

For
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{a}
,
\mu_{\mathsf{a}}
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}
, and each
R\in\mathbb{N}
, the following hold.

i

Let
m\in\{0\}\cup\mathbb{N}
. Then,
\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}
has a density
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}
with respect to
\Lambda_{R}^{[m]}
such that
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}
is bounded and continuous on
\overline{S}_{R}^{m}\times\overline{\mathsf{S}}_{R}^{k}
for each
k\in\{0\}\cup\mathbb{N}
.

ii

\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}
has a one-point correlation function
\rho_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{1}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}
and
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}
be the logarithmic derivatives of
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}
and
\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}
, respectively.
From Theorem
5.3
,
\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})=\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})
for
\mu_{\mathsf{a}}
-a.s.
({x},\mathsf{s})
for
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{a}
.
Hence, from Theorem
5.2
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})=\beta\Big(-\nabla\Phi({x})-\sum_{s^{i}\in\overline{S}_{R}}\nabla\Psi({{x}-s^{i}})+\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)}\eta_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\mathbf{i}}{x}^{\mathbf{i}}+\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{\ell}({x})\Big).

(10.1)

In Theorem
7.1
, we derived the existence of bounded continuous density
\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}
of
\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}
from the explicit representation (
7.2
) to
\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}
.
Hence, from (
10.1
), we obtain i similarly.
We can prove ii in a similar fashion as Theorem
7.1
ii.
∎

Lemma
10.1
–Lemma
10.6
below correspond to Lemma
7.1
–Lemma
7.6
, respectively.
The proof of Lemma 8.
k
is similar to that of Lemma 5.
k
for
2\leq k\leq 7
; therefore, we omit those proofs.
In Lemma
7.1
–Lemma
7.6
, we had common assumptions
(
A1
)
–
(
A3
)
.
We will impose these assumptions again in Lemma
10.1
–Lemma
10.6
, but we will not include them in the statements of these lemmas.

Let
\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}
and
\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}
be the
m
-reduced Campbell measures of
\mu_{\mathsf{a}}
and
\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}
, respectively.
Replacing
\mu^{[m]}
by
\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}
and
\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}
, we set

(\mathscr{E}_{R}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\mathscr{D}_{R\bullet}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
,
(\mathscr{E}_{R}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}},\mathscr{D}_{R\bullet}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}})
, and other quantities for
\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}
and
\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}
. For example, we have the following.

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{R}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}}(f,g)=\int_{\mathsf{S}_{R}^{[m]}}\mathbb{D}_{\mathfrak{a},R}^{[m]}[f,g]d\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]},

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{R\bullet}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}}=\{f\in\mathscr{D}_{\bullet}^{[m]};\mathscr{E}_{R}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}}(f,f)<\infty,\,f\in L^{2}(\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]})\}.

Lemma 10.1
.

For
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{a}
,
R\in\mathbb{N}
, and

, the following hold.

(\mathscr{E}_{R}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}},\mathscr{D}_{R\bullet}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}})

(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}}_{R},\mathscr{D}_{R\circ}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})

.

.

Lemma 10.4
.

For

Let
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
and

.

Lemma 10.5
.

For
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{a}
and each
m\in\{0\}\cup\mathbb{N}
, the following hold.

i

(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}}_{\bullet})=(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
.

ii

(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}}_{\circ})=(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
.

Lemma 10.6
.

For
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{a}
and
m\in\{0\}\cup\mathbb{N}
, the following hold.

i

(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
is a strongly local Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]})
.

ii

(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
is a strongly local, quasi-regular Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]})
.

Similarly as Theorem
5.1
, we obtain the logarithmic derivative
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}
of
\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}
.
In Lemma
10.7
, we prove that
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}
coincides with
\mathsf{d}^{\mu}
.
This implies that the stochastic dynamics given by the Dirichlet forms in Lemma
10.6
are described by ISDE (
2.41
).

Lemma 10.7
.

For
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{a}
, all
R\in\mathbb{N}
, and
\mu_{\mathsf{a}}
-a.s.
\mathsf{y}
,
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}
satisfies the following:

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})=\mathsf{d}^{\mu}({x},\mathsf{s}+\pi_{\overline{S}_{R}^{c}}(\mathsf{y}))\quad\text{ for $\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}$-a.s.\,$({x},\mathsf{s})$}.

(10.2)

Proof.

From
\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}(\cdot)\mu_{\mathsf{a}}(d\mathsf{y})\mu(d\mathsf{a})=\mu^{[1]}(\cdot)
, we obtain, for
h\in C_{0}^{\infty}(S_{R})\otimes\mathscr{D}_{\circ\mathrm{b}}
,

\displaystyle\int h({x},\mathsf{s})\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})d\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}({x},\mathsf{s})\mu_{\mathsf{a}}(d\mathsf{y})\mu(d\mathsf{a})

\displaystyle=-\int\nabla_{{x}}h({x},\mathsf{s})d\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}({x},\mathsf{s})\mu_{\mathsf{a}}(d\mathsf{y})\mu(d\mathsf{a})\quad\text{ by definition}

\displaystyle=-\int\nabla_{{x}}h({x},\mathsf{s})d\mu^{[1]}({x},\mathsf{s})\quad\text{ by Fubini's theorem}

\displaystyle=\int h({x},\mathsf{s})\mathsf{d}^{\mu}({x},\mathsf{s})d\mu^{[1]}({x},\mathsf{s})\quad\text{ by definition}

\displaystyle=\int h({x},\mathsf{s})\mathsf{d}^{\mu}({x},\mathsf{s})d\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}({x},\mathsf{s})\mu_{\mathsf{a}}(d\mathsf{y})\mu(d\mathsf{a})\quad\text{ by Fubini's theorem}.

This implies (
10.2
).
∎

Let
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}}_{R},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
be the strongly local, quasi-regular Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]})
given by Lemma

.
Let
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{\mathsf{a},R}
be the diffusion properly associated with
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}}_{R},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
on
L^{2}(\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]})
.
Let
W^{[m]}=C([0,\infty);(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\times\mathsf{S})

(m\geq 1)
and
W^{[0]}=C([0,\infty);\mathsf{S})
.

.

) with initial distribution

, which satisfy

)
,
(

)
, and
(

)
{}_{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}}
.

\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}
and

(

)
.
To this end, we apply Theorem

. Specifically, we verify assumptions
\{

\}
and
\{

\}
of Theorem

for

\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}
and

. See Subsection

for details regarding
\{

\}
and

\}
.

Let
\mathbf{a}=\{a_{\mathsf{q}}\}_{\mathsf{q}\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of sequences, where each
a_{\mathsf{q}}=\{a_{\mathsf{q}}(R)\}_{R\in\mathbb{N}}
consists of natural numbers satisfying
a_{\mathsf{q}}(R)<a_{\mathsf{q}}(R+1)
and
a_{\mathsf{q}}(R)<a_{\mathsf{q}+1}(R)
for all
\mathsf{q},R\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}^{\circ}
,
\mathsf{m}=(\mathsf{p},\mathsf{q},\mathsf{r})
, be the set defined in (
13.20
).
Define

\displaystyle\varsigma_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathbf{w}^{[m]})=\inf\{t>0;\mathbf{w}^{[m]}_{t}\not\in\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}^{\circ}\}.

Let
\lim_{\mathsf{m}}
be as given in (
13.25
). We now make an assumption.

\{
B1
\}

\mathbf{X}^{[m]}=(\mathbf{X}^{m},\mathsf{X}^{m*})
does not exit from
\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]=\bigcup_{\mathsf{m}\in\mathbf{N}}\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}^{\circ}
, that is,

\displaystyle P(\lim_{\mathsf{m}}\varsigma_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathbf{X}^{[m]})=\infty)=1.

(10.3)

Lemma 10.8
.

Assume
(
A1
)
–
(
A3
)
. Then, for
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{a}
,
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}
and
\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}

\{
B1
\}
.

Proof.

Throughout this proof,
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}
and
\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}
will be denoted by the same symbol
\mathbf{X}=(X^{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\mathsf{X}
will denote the unlabeled dynamics given by
\mathbf{X}
.
Let
\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{q}}]
be the compact set defined by (
13.16
):

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{q}}]=\bigcap_{R=1}^{\infty}\{\mathsf{s}\in\mathsf{S}\,;\,\mathsf{s}(S_{R})\leq a_{\mathsf{q}}(R)\}.

Note that
\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{q}}]
is increasing concerning
\mathsf{q}
from (
13.17
).
Let

\displaystyle\kappa_{\mathsf{q}}=\inf\{t>0;\mathsf{X}_{t}\notin\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{q}}]\}.

Let
\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s}}=\{\mathsf{s}\in\mathsf{S}\,;\,\mathsf{s}(\{{x}\})\in\{0,1\}\text{ for all }{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
.
Then, according to (
13.18
)–(
13.20
), (
10.3
) follows from the following:

\displaystyle P(\mathsf{X}_{t}\in\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s}}\text{ for all }0\leq t<\infty)=1,

(10.4)

\displaystyle P(\lim_{\mathsf{q}\to\infty}\kappa_{\mathsf{q}}=\infty)=1.

(10.5)

We have already proved (
10.4
) in Theorem
6.1
. Thus, it only remains to prove (
10.5
).
To verify (
10.5
), we use Lemma
13.8
.
We check all assumptions of Lemma
13.8
for
\mu_{\mathsf{a}}
:

\{
UB
\}
,
\{
MF
\}
,
\{
BX
\}
,
\{\mathbf{S}_{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}}\}
, and (
13.30
). See Subsection
13.7
.

\{

\}
is the uniform ellipticity and boundedness of
\mathfrak{a}
, which follows from (
2.39
).

\{
MF
\}
is clear because
\mathsf{X}
is associated with a symmetric Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(\mu_{\mathsf{a}})
.

\{\mathbf{S}_{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}}\}
is also clear because the
m
-labeled process
\mathsf{X}^{[m]}
given by
\mathbf{X}
is associated with a symmetric Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]})
.

Let
\nu=\varphi\mu
and
Q_{\nu}
be the probability such that
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{B})
is defined on
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},Q_{\nu},\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\})
.
Then,
\{
BX
\}
makes the following statement: for almost surely
Q_{\nu}

\displaystyle\sigma[\mathbf{B}_{s};s\leq t]\subset\sigma[\mathbf{X}_{s};s\leq t],\quad t\geq 0.

(10.6)

Let
\mathbf{M}=(M^{i})_{i=1}^{\infty}
such that
M_{t}^{i}=\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(X_{u}^{i})dB_{u}^{i}
.
Then, because
\mathfrak{a}=\sigma^{t}\sigma
,
\sigma\in C_{b}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and
\mathfrak{a}
is uniformly elliptic, we can represent
\mathbf{B}=(B^{i})_{i=1}^{\infty}
as a function of
\mathbf{M}
. Thus,
\mathbf{B}
is a
\sigma[\mathbf{M}_{s};s\leq t]
-adapted process. Because
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{B})
is a solution of ISDE (
2.41
),

\displaystyle M_{t}^{i}=\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(X_{u}^{i})dB_{u}^{i}=X_{t}^{i}-X_{0}^{i}-\int_{0}^{t}\mathfrak{b}(X_{u}^{i},\sum_{j\neq i}^{\infty}\delta_{X_{u}^{j}})du.

(10.7)

From (
10.7
),
\mathbf{M}
is a
\sigma[\mathbf{X}_{s};s\leq t]
-adapted process.
Hence, we obtain (
10.6
).

We can easily find
\mathbf{a}=\{a_{\mathsf{q}}\}_{\mathsf{q}\in\mathbb{N}}
in (
13.16
) that satisfies (
13.17
) and (
13.31
). Therefore, we can use (
13.30
) with reference to Lemma
13.7
.
Thus, we have verified all assumptions of Lemma
13.8
, which yields (
10.5
).
We have thus completed the proof.
∎

Lemma 10.9
.

Assume
(
A1
)
–
(
A3
)
. Then, for

-a.s.
\mathsf{a}

\}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{u}^{[m,0]}\!:\!(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\times\mathsf{S}\!\to\!\mathsf{S}
be the map such that

\mathfrak{u}^{[m,0]}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})=\mathfrak{u}(\mathbf{x})+\mathsf{s}
.

Let
\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}}
be the set introduced before (

).
Then,
\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}}
is the set where the coefficient of ISDE (
13.14
) is defined on

.

Let
(\sigma,\mathfrak{b})
be the coefficient of ISDE (
2.41
).
We define
\sigma^{m}
and
\mathsf{b}^{m}
by

\displaystyle\sigma^{m}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})=(\sigma({x}^{i}))_{i=1}^{m},\quad\mathsf{b}^{m}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})=\Big(\mathfrak{b}({x}^{i},\sum_{j\neq i}^{m}\delta_{{x}^{j}}+\mathsf{s})\Big)_{i=1}^{m}

(10.8)

for
\mathbf{x}=(x^{1},\ldots,x^{m})
and
\mathsf{s}\in\mathsf{S}
.
Let
\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]
be as given by (
13.20
)–(

).

Assumption
\{
B2
\}
is defined as follows:

\{
B2
\}

i
The inclusion
\mathfrak{u}^{[m,0]}(\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}])\subset\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}}
holds.

ii For each
\mathsf{m}=(\mathsf{p},\mathsf{q},\mathsf{r})\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
, there exists a constant
\mathsf{F}_{\mathsf{m}}
such that

\displaystyle|\sigma^{m}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})-\sigma^{m}(\mathbf{y},\mathsf{s})|\leq\mathsf{F}_{\mathsf{m}}|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}|,

(
13.27
)

\displaystyle|\mathsf{b}^{m}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})-\mathsf{b}^{m}(\mathbf{y},\mathsf{s})|\leq\mathsf{F}_{\mathsf{m}}|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}|

(
13.28
)

for all
(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s}),(\mathbf{y},\mathsf{s})\in\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}
such that

(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})\sim_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathbf{y},\mathsf{s})
, where
\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}=\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{p},\mathsf{q},\mathsf{r}}
is the set defined by (
13.21
) and

is the equivalence relation defined after (
13.26
).

Suppose (
13.27
) and (
13.28
). Then,
(\sigma^{m},\mathsf{b}^{m})
is Lipschitz continuous on
\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}
for each
\mathsf{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
.
From (

),
\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]=\bigcup_{\mathsf{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{3}}\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}
.
Thus,

\{

\}

For
\{
B2
\}

\sigma^{m}
and

in (

) and (
13.28
).

Note that
\sigma\in C_{b}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and that

is independent of

by assumption.
Hence,
\sigma^{m}
is Lipschitz continuous, which yields (
13.27
).

From Proposition
5.1
, we have
\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{s}}^{\ell}\in C^{1}(\overline{S}_{R})
. From (

), we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}^{\mu}({x},\mathsf{s})=\beta\Big(-\nabla\Phi({x})-\sum_{s^{i}\in\overline{S}_{R}}\nabla\Psi({x}-s^{i})+\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathbb{I}(\ell)}\eta_{R,\mathsf{s}}^{\mathbf{i}}{x}^{\mathbf{i}}+\mathscr{R}_{R,\mathsf{s}}^{\ell}({x})\Big).

(10.9)

Hence,
\mathsf{d}^{\mu}
is Lipschitz continuous on
\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}
from (
10.9
), (
13.19
), and (
13.21
).
From
\mathfrak{a}\in C_{b}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
\mathfrak{a}
and
\nabla\mathfrak{a}
are Lipschitz continuous.
Then,
\mathfrak{b}=\frac{1}{2}\{\nabla\mathfrak{a}+\mathsf{d}^{\mu}\mathfrak{a}\}
is Lipschitz continuous on
\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}
.
Hence, from (
10.8
),
\mathsf{b}^{m}
is Lipschitz continuous on
\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}
, which yields (

).
Thus, we obtain
\{
B2
\}
ii.
∎

\mu_{\mathsf{a}}

)

, and

)
.

\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}
and
\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}
are indistinguishable.
This implies i.

Let
\underline{\mathbf{X}}^{[m]}_{\mathsf{a}}
and
\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{[m]}_{\mathsf{a}}

-labeled processes of

\underline{\mathbf{X}}^{[m]}_{\mathsf{a}}
and

From Theorem
10.2
and Theorem
