,
\underline{\mathbf{X}}^{[m]}_{\mathsf{a}}
and
\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{[m]}_{\mathsf{a}}
are associated with the
m
-labeled Dirichlet forms
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
and
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
on
L^{2}(\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]})
, respectively.
Because
\underline{\mathbf{X}}^{[m]}_{\mathsf{a}}
and
\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{[m]}_{\mathsf{a}}
are indistinguishable, we obtain (
10.10
).

From Theorem
10.3
,
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
is a strongly local, conservative, and quasi-regular Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]})
. Hence, from (
10.10
),
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
is also a strongly local, conservative, and quasi-regular Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]})
. This implies ii.
∎

Remark 10.1
.

The method to use the uniqueness of strong solutions of ISDEs to prove the uniqueness of the extension of Dirichlet forms on interacting Brownian motions is due to Tanemura
[
43
]
. This method was followed by
[
15
]
.

Proof of Theorem
2.3
.

Let
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}
and
\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}
be as in Theorem
10.6
.
Then, they are indistinguishable from Theorem
10.6
. Hence, we write
\mathbf{X}:=\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}=\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}
.

From Theorem
10.6
, for
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{a}
, (
2.41
) has a unique strong solution
\mathbf{X}
starting at
\mathbf{s}=\mathfrak{l}(\mathsf{s})
for
\mu_{\mathsf{a}}
-a.s.
\mathsf{s}
under constraints
(
SIN
)
,
(
NBJ
)
,
(
AC
)
{}_{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}}
,
(
MF
)
, and
(
IFC
)
.
For each
m\in\{0\}\cup\mathbb{N}
, the
m
-labeled process
\mathbf{X}^{[m]}
given by
\mathbf{X}
is properly associated with the strongly local, quasi-regular Dirichlet form in (
10.10
) on
L^{2}(\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]})
.
Hence,
\mathbf{X}^{[m]}
is a
\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}
-symmetric diffusion.
From Theorem
10.2
,
\mathbf{X}^{[m]}
is conservative.
We have thus completed the proof of Theorem
2.3
.
∎

11.
Uniqueness of the extension of Dirichlet forms: Proof of Theorem
2.4
.

This section aims to prove the uniqueness of the extensions of Dirichlet forms and, using this, to establish the fully labeled process
\mathbf{X}=(X^{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
in Theorem
2.3
is a conservative diffusion on
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}}
and, thus, complete the proof of Theorem
2.4
.

11.1.
Uniqueness of extension of Dirichlet forms.

We now analyze the Dirichlet forms directly related to the solution of ISDE (
2.41
) to prove the uniqueness of extension of Dirichlet forms in Theorem
11.1
.

Let
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{R},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R}^{\mu^{[m]}})
and
(\mathscr{E}_{R}^{\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R}^{\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}})
be as in Lemma
7.2
.
Recall that

\displaystyle\mu^{[m]}=\int_{\mathsf{S}}\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}\mu(d\mathsf{y}).

Let
(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{R\star}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R\star}^{\mu^{[m]}})
be the superposition
of
(\mathscr{E}_{R}^{\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R}^{\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}})
on
L^{2}(\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]})

[
4
]
.
By definition,

(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{R\star}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R\star}^{\mu^{[m]}})

is the closed form on
L^{2}(\mu^{[m]})
given by

\displaystyle\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{R\star}^{\mu^{[m]}}(f,g)=\int_{\mathsf{S}}\mathscr{E}_{R}^{\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}}(f,g)\mu(d\mathsf{y}),

\displaystyle\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R\star}^{\mu^{[m]}}=\{f\in\bigcap_{\text{$\mu$-a.s.\,$\mathsf{y}$}}\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R}^{\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}};\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{R\star}^{\mu^{[m]}}(f,f)<\infty,f\in L^{2}(\mu^{[m]})\}.

(11.1)

Lemma 11.1
.

Assume
(
A1
)
–
(
A3
)
. Let
m\in\{0\}\cup\mathbb{N}
. Then,

\displaystyle(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{R},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R}^{\mu^{[m]}})=(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{R\star}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R\star}^{\mu^{[m]}})\quad\text{ for all }R\in\mathbb{N}.

(11.2)

Proof.

Let
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{R}^{[m]}
and
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}
be the
m
-labeled diffusions given by the Dirichlet forms
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{R},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R}^{\mu^{[m]}})
on
L^{2}(\mu^{[m]})
and
(\mathscr{E}_{R}^{\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R}^{\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}})
on
L^{2}(\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]})
, respectively.
Let
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{R}
and
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{R,\mathsf{y}}
be the fully labeled processes given by the diffusions

\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{R}^{[m]}
and
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}
, respectively.
From the argument before Lemma
8.1
, such processes exist.
According to Lemma
8.1
, both fully labeled processes are weak solutions of the pair of SDEs (
2.58
) and (
2.59
).

From the representation of the logarithmic derivative
\mathsf{d}_{R,\mathsf{y}}({x},\mathsf{s})
given by (
5.5
), the pair of SDEs (
2.58
) and (
2.59
) has a unique strong solution.
Indeed, the coefficients given by the logarithmic derivative are smooth outside

\mathscr{C}^{[1]}=\{({x},\mathsf{s})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S};{x}=s^{i}\text{ for some }i\}
, where
\mathsf{s}=\sum_{i}\delta_{s^{i}}
, and each particle is posed the reflecting boundary condition on
\partial S_{R}
.
Hence, (
2.58
) has a unique strong solution until the solution hits
\mathscr{C}^{[1]}
, which never happens because of Lemma
8.1
iv.
Here, Lemma
8.1
iv is clearly valid for
(\mathscr{E}_{R}^{\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R}^{\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]}})
on
L^{2}(\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[1]})
similarly as for
(\mathscr{E}_{R}^{\mu^{[1]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R}^{\mu^{[1]}})
on
L^{2}(\mu^{[1]})
.
Thus, (
2.58
) has a unique strong solution.
SDE (
2.59
) means that all particles outside
\overline{S}_{R}
are frozen.
Hence, it is clear that (
2.59
) has a trivial unique strong solution.

Because
(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{R\star}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R\star}^{\mu^{[m]}})
is the superposition of
(\mathscr{E}_{R}^{\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R}^{\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}})
, the collection of diffusions
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}
associated with
(\mathscr{E}_{R}^{\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R}^{\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}})
on
L^{2}(\mu_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]})
gives the diffusion associated with
(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{R\star}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R\star}^{\mu^{[m]}})
on
L^{2}(\mu^{[m]})
.
As we saw at the beginning of the proof, the diffusion
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}

gives the unique solution of (
2.58
) and (
2.59
). Hence, the diffusion
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}
,
\mathsf{y}\in\mathsf{S}
, given by the superposition
(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{R\star}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R\star}^{\mu^{[m]}})
is the unique solution of (
2.58
) and (
2.59
).

Recall that
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{R}
is a weak solution of SDEs (
2.58
) and (
2.59
).
Hence, from Corollary
2
, the diffusion
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}
,
\mathsf{y}\in\mathsf{S}
, given by
(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{R\star}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R\star}^{\mu^{[m]}})
and
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{R}^{[m]}
given by
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{R},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R}^{\mu^{[m]}})
coincide.
This implies the identity of the Dirichlet forms in (
11.2
).
∎

Let
\mathscr{D}_{\bullet}^{\mu^{[m]}}=\{f\in\mathscr{D}_{\bullet}^{[m]};\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}}(f,f)<\infty,f\in L^{2}(\mu^{[m]})\}
.
Let
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{\bullet})
be the closure of
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}},\mathscr{D}_{\bullet}^{\mu^{[m]}})
on
L^{2}(\mu^{[m]})
given by Lemma
7.4
.

From (
7.11
),
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{R},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R}^{\mu^{[m]}})
is increasing.
Hence,
(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{R\star}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R\star}^{\mu^{[m]}})
is increasing from Lemma
11.1
.
Let
(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{\star}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{\star}^{\mu^{[m]}})
be
the increasing limit of
(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{R\star}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R\star}^{\mu^{[m]}})
.

Lemma 11.2
.

Assume
(
A1
)
–
(
A3
)
. Then, for each
m\in\{0\}\cup\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu^{[m]}})=(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{\star}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{\star}^{\mu^{[m]}}).

(11.3)

Proof.

The first equality in (
11.3
) follows from Lemma
7.5
i. The second follows from

\displaystyle(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu^{[m]}})=\lim_{R\to\infty}(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{R},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R}^{\mu^{[m]}})=\lim_{R\to\infty}(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{R\star}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R\star}^{\mu^{[m]}})=(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{\star}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{\star}^{\mu^{[m]}}).

Here, we used Lemma
7.3
i and Lemma
11.1
.
∎

Remark 11.1
.

It is plausible that the following identity holds:

\displaystyle(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu^{[m]}})=(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu^{[m]}}).

(11.4)

Kawamoto proved (
11.4
) for Dyson’s model
[
14
]
.

Let
(\mathscr{E}_{R}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}})
be the Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]})
given in Lemma
10.2
.
Let
(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{R\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
be the superposition
of
(\mathscr{E}_{R}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}})
in
\mathsf{y}
for
\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}
, that is,

\displaystyle\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{R\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}}(f,g)=\int_{\mathsf{S}}\mathscr{E}_{R}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}}(f,g)\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}(d\mathsf{y}),

\displaystyle\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}}=\{f\in\bigcap_{\mathsf{y}\in\mathsf{S}_{0}}\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a},R,\mathsf{y}}^{[m]}};\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{R\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}}(f,f)<\infty,f\in L^{2}(\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]})\},

where
\mathsf{S}_{0}
is the set in Definition
2.3
. We recall that
\mu(\mathsf{S}_{0})=1
.
Applying the general theorem on the superposition of closed forms
[
4
]
, we see that
(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{R\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
is a closed form on
L^{2}(\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]})
.
Let
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}}_{R},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
be as in Lemma
10.2
.

Lemma 11.3
.

Assume
(
A1
)
–
(
A3
)
. Let
m\in\{0\}\cup\mathbb{N}
. Then, for
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{a}

\displaystyle(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}}_{R},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})=(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{R\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})\quad\text{ for all }R\in\mathbb{N}.

Proof.

The proof of Lemma
11.3
is identical to that of Lemma
11.1
. Hence, it is omitted here.
∎

We easily show that
(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{R\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
,
R\in\mathbb{N}
, is increasing. Let
(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
be
the increasing limit of
(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{R\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{R\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
in the sense of (
13.2
).
Then,
(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
is a closed form on
L^{2}(\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]})
from Lemma
13.5
i.

Replacing
\mu
by
\mu_{\mathsf{a}}
, we set
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}}_{\bullet})
similarly as
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{\bullet})
.
Let
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
be the closed form on
L^{2}(\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]})
in Lemma
10.6
.

Lemma 11.4
.

Assume
(
A1
)
–
(
A3
)
. Then, for each
m\in\{0\}\cup\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}}_{\bullet})

\displaystyle=(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})=(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}}).

(11.5)

Proof.

Equation (
11.5
) follows from Lemma
10.5
, Lemma
10.3
i, and Lemma
11.3
.
The detail of the proof is similar to that of Lemma
11.2
.
Hence, it is omitted here.
∎

Let
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{\star},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{\star})
,

(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{\star}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{\star}^{\mu^{[m]}})
, and
(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{\star\star}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{\star\star}^{\mu^{[m]}})
be the superpositions of
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
,
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
, and
(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
in
\mathsf{a}
, respectively.
Here,
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
is the closed form in Lemma
10.6
.
The following theorem asserts that these Dirichlet forms coincide and are strongly local and quasi-regular.

Theorem 11.1

(Uniqueness of extension of Dirichlet forms)
.

Assume
(
A1
)
–
(
A3
)
.

i

Let
\mathsf{S}_{0}
be the set in Definition
2.3
. Then, for all
\mathsf{a}\in\mathsf{S}_{0}
,

\displaystyle(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})

\displaystyle=(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})=(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}}),

(11.6)

\displaystyle(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{\star},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{\star})

\displaystyle=(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{\star}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{\star}^{\mu^{[m]}})=(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{\star\star}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{\star\star}^{\mu^{[m]}}).

(11.7)

ii

All Dirichlet forms in (
11.6
) and (
11.7
) are strongly local and quasi-regular.

Proof.

From Theorem
10.6
and Lemma
11.4
, we obtain (
11.6
).
Taking the superposition of (
11.6
) in
\mathsf{a}
, we have (
11.7
), which yields i.

From Lemma
10.6
ii,
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
is strongly local and quasi-regular on
L^{2}(\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]})
.
Hence, all Dirichlet forms in (
11.6
) are strongly local and quasi-regular.

Let
[\mathsf{a}]=\{\mathsf{b}\in\mathsf{S}_{0};\mu_{\mathsf{a}}=\mu_{\mathsf{b}}\}
be as in Definition
2.3
.
Then,
\mu_{\mathsf{a}}([\mathsf{a}])=1
and
[\mathsf{a}]\cap[\mathsf{b}]=\emptyset
if
[\mathsf{a}]\neq[\mathsf{b}]
.
Note that
[\mathsf{a}]
gives a partition of
\mathsf{S}_{0}
and
\mu(\mathsf{S}_{0})=1
by construction.

Let
\mathbf{S}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}=(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\times[\mathsf{a}]
. Then,
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
is a strongly local, quasi-regular Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(\mathbf{S}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]},\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]})
.
Let
\mathrm{Cap}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}
be the capacity given by the Dirichlet form
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
on
L^{2}(\mathbf{S}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]},\mu_{\mathsf{a}})
. Then, there exists an increasing sequence of compact sets
\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{n}
such that
\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{n}\subset[\mathsf{a}]
satisfying

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathrm{Cap}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}(\{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\times\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{n}\}^{c})=0.

(11.8)

Indeed, (
11.8
) follows from
(
QR1
)
in Definition
13.4
.
Here, we take, for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{n}=\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{b}}^{n}\quad\text{ if and only if }[\mathsf{a}]=[\mathsf{b}].

(11.9)

Let
\mathsf{P}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}}
be the diffusion properly associated with the strongly local, quasi-regular Dirichlet form

(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
on
L^{2}(\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]})
.
From (
11.8
), the state space of
\mathsf{P}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}}
can be taken to be disjoint for
[\mathsf{a}]\neq[\mathsf{b}]
.
Thus, the collection of diffusions
\mathsf{P}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}}
,
\mathsf{a}\in\mathsf{S}_{0}
, gives the diffusion associated with

(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{\star},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{\star})
on
L^{2}(\mu^{[m]})
.
Hence, from
[
5
, Th. 1.5.3]
,
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{\star},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{\star})
on
L^{2}(\mu^{[m]})
is quasi-regular.
The strong locality of
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{\star},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{\star})
is clear by construction.
Hence, all Dirichlet forms in (
11.7
) are strongly local and quasi-regular on
L^{2}(\mu^{[m]})
.
Thus, we complete the proof of ii.
∎

At first glance, Theorem
11.1
seems abstract, and it may not be easy to understand how it plays a role in the stochastic dynamics of an infinite-particle system. Therefore, to explain its importance, we describe some applications in the following remark.

Remark 11.2
.

i
The quasi-regularity of Dirichlet forms is a critical property for applying stochastic analysis to these objects.
From Theorem
11.1
and the general theory of quasi-regular Dirichlet forms (cf.
[
5
]
), we have diffusions that are properly associated with Dirichlet forms in (
11.6
) and (
11.7
).

ii
The proof of the sub-diffusivity of the tagged particles of the Ginibre interacting Brownian motion (the two-dimensional Coulomb interacting Brownian motion with
\beta=2
) relies on (
11.6
)
[
28
,
29
]
.
This result reflects the effect of the long-range nature of the two-dimensional Coulomb potential.
Indeed, the tagged particles of interacting Brownian motion in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
under Ruelle’s class potential with a convex hardcore are always diffusive if
d\geq 2

[
23
]
.

iii
Suzuki proved that the unlabeled Markov processes associated with a Dirichlet form are ergodic if the reference measure
\mu
is number rigid and the Dirichlet form is locally ergodic
[
40
]
.
This theorem can be applied to the Ginibre random point field.
In
[
40
]
, Suzuki considered the Dirichlet form that corresponds to
(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{\star\star}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{\star\star}^{\mu^{[m]}})
.
Hence, from Theorem
11.1
, we see that the diffusion associated with the Dirichlet form for the Ginibre random point field is ergodic and the Markov process in
[
40
]
is a diffusion.

iv Suzuki
[
41
]
studied curvature bound of Dyson’s Brownian motion, which is an infinite particle system of interacting Brownian particles in
\mathbb{R}
with the two-dimensional Coulomb potential with the inverse temperature
\beta=2
. Thus, the dynamics have a Riesz potential.
He studied Dirichlet form of the type
(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{\star\star}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{\star\star}^{\mu^{[m]}})
. Our argument for Theorem
11.1
is valid for the Riesz potential. Thus, his result is valid for the dynamics of Dyson’s model studied by
[
25
,
34
]
because of the uniqueness of Dirichlet forms.

iv In
[
36
,
30
]
, the irreducibility and ergodicity of Dyson’s model were proved.
The proof is also valid for the Coulomb interacting Brownian motions in the present article. .

For nonnegative, symmetric bilinear forms, we write
(\mathscr{E}_{1},\mathscr{D}_{1})\prec(\mathscr{E}_{2},\mathscr{D}_{2})
if

(\mathscr{E}_{2},\mathscr{D}_{2})
is an extension of
(\mathscr{E}_{1},\mathscr{D}_{1})
, that is,

\mathscr{D}_{1}\subset\mathscr{D}_{2}
and
\mathscr{E}_{1}(f,g)=\mathscr{E}_{2}(f,g)
for all
f,g\in\mathscr{D}_{1}
.
Then, by construction, we can easily deduce that, for all
\mathsf{a}\in\mathsf{S}_{0}
,

\displaystyle(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})\prec

\displaystyle(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})\prec(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{\star}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}}),

\displaystyle(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{\star},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{\star})\prec

\displaystyle(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{\star}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{\star}^{\mu^{[m]}})\prec(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{\star\star}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{\star\star}^{\mu^{[m]}}).

(11.10)

Thus, we have proved in Theorem
11.1
that the relations in (
11.10
) are indeed equalities.

11.2.
Proof of Theorem
2.4
.

Utilizing the uniqueness results of solutions of ISDEs (Theorem
10.6
) and Dirichlet forms (Theorem
11.1
), we establish Theorem
2.4
in this subsection. The goal is to prove that the fully labeled process
\mathbf{X}
in Theorem
2.3
is an
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}}
-valued diffusion.

Let
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}
and
\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}
be as in Theorem
10.6
.
In the proof of Theorem
2.3
, we constructed
\mathbf{X}
as
\mathbf{X}:=\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}=\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}
by proving
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}
and
\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}
are indistinguishable.
Let
\mathbf{X}^{[m]}
be the
m
-labeled process of
\mathbf{X}
defined by (
2.42
).
In Theorem
2.3
, we have already proved that
\mathbf{X}^{[m]}
is a
\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}
-symmetric diffusion for
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{a}
, but this does not necessarily mean the collection over
\mathsf{a}\in\mathsf{S}
is a
\mu^{[m]}
-symmetric diffusion.
Indeed, denoting by
\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}
the state space of the
\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}
-symmetric diffusion
\mathbf{X}^{[m]}
, we have

\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}((\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\times\mathsf{S}\backslash\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]})=0
but it does not necessarily hold

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}\cap\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}_{\mathsf{b}}^{[m]}=\emptyset\quad\text{ for }[\mathsf{a}]\neq[\mathsf{b}],

(11.11)

where
[\mathsf{a}]
is the equivalent class in (
2.51
).
If (
11.11
) holds, then the collection
\mathbf{X}^{[m]}
over
\mathsf{a}
is a diffusion with state space
\cup_{\mathsf{a}}\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}
because each
\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}
is the invariant set of the conservative diffusion
\mathbf{X}^{[m]}
.
In the following theorem, we prove (
11.11
) and that the
m
-labeled process
\mathbf{X}^{[m]}
is a diffusion with state space
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\times\mathsf{S}
.

Theorem 11.2
.

Assume
(
A1
)
–
(
A3
)
. Let
\mathbf{X}
and
\mathbf{X}^{[m]}
be as above.

i
For
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{a}
,

\mathbf{X}^{[m]}
is a
\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}
-symmetric, conservative diffusion properly associated with the Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]})
in (
11.6
) for each
m\in\{0\}\cup\mathbb{N}
.

ii

\mathbf{X}^{[m]}
is a
\mu^{[m]}
-symmetric, conservative diffusion properly associated with the Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(\mu^{[m]})
in (
11.7
) for each
m\in\{0\}\cup\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

From the proof of Theorem
2.3
, we have already proved that
\mathbf{X}^{[m]}
is a
\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}
-symmetric, conservative diffusion for
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{a}
.
From Theorem
10.6
,
\mathbf{X}^{[m]}
is properly associated with the Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(\mu^{[m]})
in (
11.6
).
Thus, we obtain i.

Let
\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{n}
be the compact set given in the proof of Theorem
11.1
. Let
[\mathsf{a}]
be as in (
2.51
).
Recall that
\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{n}
is increasing in
n
for each
\mathsf{a}
and that
\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{n}\subset[\mathsf{a}]
.
Then,

\displaystyle(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\times\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{n}\subset(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\times[\mathsf{a}]\quad\text{ for all }n\in\mathbb{N}.

(11.12)

Let
\mathbf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}=(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\times\cup_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{n}
and
\mathbf{S}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}=(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\times[\mathsf{a}]
.
From (
11.9
),
\mathbf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}=\mathbf{K}_{\mathsf{b}}^{[m]}
if and only if
[\mathsf{a}]=[\mathsf{b}]
.
From (
11.12
),
\mathbf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}\cap\mathbf{K}_{\mathsf{b}}^{[m]}=\emptyset
if
[\mathsf{a}]\neq[\mathsf{b}]
.
Indeed, this follows from
\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{n}\cap\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{b}}^{o}=\emptyset
for any
[\mathsf{a}]\neq[\mathsf{b}]
and
all
n,o\in\mathbb{N}
, which follows from
\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{n}\subset[\mathsf{a}]
.

From the proof of Theorem
11.1
,
\mathbf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}
is the state space of the diffusion
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}
properly associated with

(\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}})
on
L^{2}(\mathbf{S}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]},\mu_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]})
. Thus,
\mathbf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}
is an invariant set of the diffusion
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}
for each
\mathsf{a}
.
Because
\underline{\mathbf{X}}^{[m]}
coincides with
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}
on
\mathbf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}
,
\underline{\mathbf{X}}^{[m]}
is a
\mu^{[m]}
-symmetric, conservative diffusion properly associated with the superposition
(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{\star\star}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{\star\star}^{\mu^{[m]}})
on
L^{2}(\mu^{[m]})
with the state space
\cup_{[\mathsf{a}]}\mathbf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}
such that
\{\mathbf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}\}_{\mathsf{a}}
gives a partition of the state space into
\mathbf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[m]}
that are invariant sets of
\underline{\mathbf{X}}^{[m]}
. We have thus proved ii.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.4
.

Let
\mathbf{X}
be the fully labeled process in Theorem
2.3
.
Let
\mathsf{X}=\mathbf{X}^{[0]}
be the zero-labeled process given by
\mathbf{X}
.
Recall that
\mu=\mu^{[0]}
.
Then, from Theorem
11.2
ii,
\mathsf{X}
is a
\mu
-reversible diffusion with state space
\mathsf{S}_{\star\star}
properly associated with the Dirichlet form

(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{\star\star}^{\mu},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{\star\star}^{\mu})
on
L^{2}(\mu)
.
The state space
\mathsf{S}_{\star\star}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}_{\star\star}=\cup_{\mathsf{a}\in\mathsf{S}_{0}}\cup_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{n}=\cup_{\mathsf{a}\in\mathsf{S}_{0}}\mathbf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[0]},

where
\mathsf{S}_{0}
is as in Definition
2.3
and
\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{n}
is given by (
11.8
) and

\cup_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{n}=\mathbf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[0]}
.

From Theorem
11.1
ii,
(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{\star\star}^{\mu},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{\star\star}^{\mu})
on
L^{2}(\mu)
is strongly local and quasi-regular.
The state space
\mathsf{S}_{\star\star}
satisfies
\mathrm{Cap}^{\mu}(\mathsf{S}_{\star\star}^{c})=0
,
where
\mathrm{Cap}^{\mu}
is the capacity associated with
(\underline{\mathscr{E}}_{\star\star}^{\mu^{[m]}},\underline{\mathscr{D}}_{\star\star}^{\mu^{[m]}})
on
L^{2}(\mu)
.
This follows from that
\mathbf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[0]}
is an invariant set of the diffusion
\underline{\mathsf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}=\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[0]}
for each
\mathsf{a}\in\mathsf{S}_{0}
.
Because
\mathsf{X}=\underline{\mathsf{X}}_{\mathsf{a}}
,
\mathbf{K}_{\mathsf{a}}^{[0]}
is the invariant set of the diffusion
\mathsf{X}
for each
\mathsf{a}\in\mathsf{S}_{0}
.

We can take a quasi-continuous version of the diffusion measure
P_{\mathsf{x}}
of
\mathsf{X}
such that
P_{\mathsf{x}}
is defined for all
\mathsf{x}\in\mathsf{S}_{\star\star}
. Note that
P_{\mathsf{x}}(\mathsf{X}\in C([0,\infty);\mathsf{S}_{\star\star}))=1
because
\mathsf{S}_{\star\star}
is the state space of the conservative diffusion
\mathsf{X}
.

Let
\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}\!:\!W_{\mathrm{NE}}(\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s,i}})\!\to\!C([0,\infty);\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}}
be as in Lemma
13.2
.
Let
\mathfrak{u}_{\mathrm{path}}
be the map defined by
\mathfrak{u}_{\mathrm{path}}(\mathbf{w})=\mathsf{w}
, where
\mathsf{w}(t)=\sum_{i}\delta_{w^{i}(t)}
for
\mathbf{w}=(w^{i})_{i}
.
Then,
\mathfrak{u}_{\mathrm{path}}\circ\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}
is the identity map. Hence,
\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}
is injective. We note that
\mathbf{X}=\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}(\mathsf{X})
.

The value
\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}(\mathsf{w})(t)\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}}
is determined by
\mathfrak{l}(\mathsf{w}(0))
and
\mathsf{w}(u)
,
0\leq u\leq t
.
Hence,
\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}(\mathsf{w})(t)
is
\mathscr{F}_{t}=\sigma[\mathfrak{l}(\mathsf{w}(0))]\vee\sigma[\mathsf{w}(u),0\leq u\leq t]
-measurable.
Thus, the time-shifted path
\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}(\mathsf{w})(\cdot+a)
is determined by
\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}(\mathsf{w})(a)
.

Let
\mathbf{S}_{\star\star}\subset(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}}
be the set defined by

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{\star\star}=\{\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}(\mathsf{w})(t);\mathsf{w}\in C([0,\infty);\mathsf{S}_{\star\star}),0\leq t<\infty\}.

(11.13)

We note that
\mathfrak{u}(\mathbf{S}_{\star\star})=\mathsf{S}_{\star\star}
.

Let
P_{\mathbf{x}}=P(\mathbf{X}\in\cdot|\mathbf{X}_{0}=\mathbf{x})
be the distribution of
\mathbf{X}
starting at
\mathbf{x}\in\mathfrak{u}^{-1}(\mathsf{S}_{\star\star})
. As before, let
\mathsf{P}_{\mathsf{x}}
be the distribution of
\mathsf{X}
starting at
\mathsf{x}
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle P_{\mathbf{x}}=\mathsf{P}_{\mathsf{x}}\circ\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}^{-1},\quad\mathbf{x}=\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}(\mathsf{X}_{0})=\mathfrak{l}(\mathsf{x}),\quad\mathfrak{u}(\mathbf{x})=\mathsf{x}.

(11.14)

Let
\sigma
be an arbitrary
\{\mathscr{F}_{t}\}
-stopping time such that
\sigma<\infty
a.s.
Let
\tau=\sigma\circ\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}
and
\mathscr{G}_{t}=\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}^{-1}(\mathscr{F}_{t})
.
Then, we have
\mathscr{G}_{\tau}=\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}^{-1}(\mathscr{F}_{\sigma})
.
Recall that
\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}
is injective and
\mathsf{X}
is a diffusion with state space
\mathsf{S}_{\star\star}
.
Then, for each
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{S}_{\star\star}
such that
\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{X}_{\sigma(\mathbf{X})}
,

\displaystyle P_{\mathbf{x}}(\mathbf{X}_{t+\sigma(\mathbf{X})}\in\cdot|\mathscr{F}_{\sigma})|_{\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{X}_{\sigma(\mathbf{X})}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{P}_{\mathfrak{u}(\mathbf{x})}(\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}(\mathsf{X}_{t+\sigma(\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}(\mathsf{X}))})\in\cdot|\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}^{-1}(\mathscr{F}_{\sigma}))|_{\mathfrak{u}(\mathbf{x})=\mathsf{X}_{\sigma(\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}(\mathsf{X}))}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{P}_{\mathsf{x}}(\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}(\mathsf{X}_{t+\tau(\mathsf{X})})\in\cdot|\mathscr{G}_{\tau})|_{\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{X}_{\tau(\mathsf{X})}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{P}_{\mathsf{x}}(\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}(\mathsf{X}_{t})\in\cdot)|_{\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{X}_{\tau(\mathsf{X})}}

\displaystyle=P_{\mathbf{x}}(\mathbf{X}_{t}\in\cdot|\mathscr{F}_{\sigma})|_{\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{X}_{\sigma(\mathbf{X})}}.

Here, we used (
11.14
) for the second line, the strong Markov property of
\mathsf{X}
for the forth line, and the injectivity of
\mathfrak{l}
and
\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}
for the last line. Because
\mathsf{X}
is
\mu
-reversible and satisfies
(
SIN
)
,
\mathsf{X}
has infinite lifetime. Hence, so is
\mathbf{X}=\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}(\mathsf{X})
.
Thus, we conclude that
\mathbf{X}
is a conservative diffusion with state space
\mathbf{S}_{\star\star}
such that

\mu(\mathfrak{u}(\mathbf{S}_{\star\star}))=1
.
∎

12.
Dynamical finite-volume approximations: Proof of Theorem
2.5
.

In this section, we complete the proof of Theorem
2.5
. Let
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}
be as in Theorem
2.5
.

Lemma 12.1
.

Assume
(
A1
)
–
(
A3
)
and (
2.60
).
Then, for
\mu
-a.s.
\mathsf{s}
, the sequence
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}
converges in finitely dimensional distributions to
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{R}
as
\mathfrak{n}\to\infty
.

Proof.

Let
k=\mathsf{s}(\overline{S}_{R})
. Suppose that
\mathfrak{n}
satisfies
N_{\mathfrak{n}}\geq k
.
We regard
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}
as the
\overline{S}_{R}^{k}
-valued diffusion with the reflecting boundary condition.
To prove Lemma
12.1
, it is sufficient to show the convergence of finitely dimensional distributions for each
k
.

Let
\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{s}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},k}
and
\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{s}}^{k}
be the
k
-density functions of
\mu_{R,\mathsf{s}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}
and

\mu_{R,\mathsf{s}}
on
\overline{S}_{R}^{k}
, respectively.
Recall that
\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{s}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},k}(\mathbf{x})
and
\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{s}}^{k}(\mathbf{x})
are symmetric in
\mathbf{x}
.
Hence, according to Lemma
5.3
, we deduce that, for each
l
,
\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{s}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},k}\wedge l
converges to
\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{s}}^{k}\wedge l
in
L^{2}(\overline{S}_{R}^{k},d\mathbf{x})
as
\mathfrak{n}\to\infty
.

Let
p_{R,\mathsf{s}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},k}(t,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{s}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},k}(\mathbf{y})d\mathbf{y}
be the transition probability of
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}
.
Similarly, let
p_{R,\mathsf{s}}^{k}(t,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\mathfrak{m}_{R,\mathsf{s}}^{k}(\mathbf{y})d\mathbf{y}
be the transition probability of
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{R}
.

Let
\mathscr{N}=\{\mathbf{x}=({x}^{1},\ldots,{x}^{k})\in\overline{S}_{R}^{k};x^{i}\neq x^{j}\text{ for }i\neq j\}
and

\mathscr{O}=(0,\infty)\times(\overline{S}_{R}^{k}\backslash\mathscr{N})^{2}
.
Then, using the classical theory of heat kernels with divergence form, we obtain that
p_{R,\mathsf{s}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},k}(t,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})
and
p_{R,\mathsf{s}}^{k}(t,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})
are locally Hölder continuous on
\mathscr{O}

and that
p_{R,\mathsf{s}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},k}(t,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})
converges to
p_{R,\mathsf{s}}^{k}(t,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})
uniformly on each compact set
K
in
\mathscr{O}
.
Hence, we obtain the convergence of the finitely dimensional distributions of
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}
to those of
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{R}
.
∎

Lemma 12.2
.

Assume
(
A1
)
–
(
A3
)
, (
2.61
), and (
2.62
).
Then, the sequence
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}
is tight in
C([0,\infty);\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}}
as
\mathfrak{n}\to\infty
.

Proof.

We suppose
N_{\mathfrak{n}}\geq\mathsf{s}(\overline{S}_{R})
.
We only consider the case
i\leq\mathsf{s}(\overline{S}_{R})
because
\underline{X}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},i}(t)
is frozen for
i>\mathsf{s}(\overline{S}_{R})
.
From (
2.56
), we have that, for
i\leq\mathsf{s}(\overline{S}_{R})
,

\displaystyle\underline{X}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},i}(t)-\underline{X}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},i}(0)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(\underline{X}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},i}(u))dB_{u}^{i}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathfrak{b}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}(\underline{X}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},i}(u),\sum_{j\neq i}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}\delta_{\underline{X}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},j}(u)})du

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{n}_{R}\mathfrak{a}(\underline{X}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},i}(u))dL_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},i}(u),

\displaystyle L_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},i}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}1_{\partial S_{R}}(\underline{X}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},i}(u))dL_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},i}(u).

Note that the unlabeled dynamics
\underline{\mathsf{X}}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}
are
\mu_{R,\mathsf{s}}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}
-reversible.
Suppose that
\varphi=\mathrm{const}
.
Then, from the Lyons-Zheng decomposition
[
5
, p.284]
, for
1\leq i\leq\mathsf{s}(\overline{S}_{R})
,

\displaystyle\underline{X}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},i}(t)-\underline{X}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},i}(0)=\frac{1}{2}\Big\{\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(\underline{X}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},i}(v))dB_{v}^{i}-\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(\underline{X}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},i}(v))dB_{v}^{i}\circ r_{t}\Big\}.

(12.1)

From (
12.1
) and (
2.39
), and the martingale inequality, we obtain a uniform bound

\displaystyle E_{\mu}[\lvert\underline{X}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},i}(t)-\underline{X}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},i}(u)\rvert^{4}]\leq c_{\small\ref{;X2k}}\lvert t-u\rvert^{2}.

Hence,
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}
is tight in
C([0,\infty);\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}}
as
\mathfrak{n}\to\infty
, where
c_{12.1}
is a constant independent of
\mathfrak{n}
.
For
\varphi
as in (
2.61
), the tightness of
\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}}}
follows from (
2.62
) and

\displaystyle E_{\varphi d\mu}[\lvert\underline{X}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},i}(t)-\underline{X}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},i}(u)\rvert^{4}]\leq c_{\small\ref{;14}}E_{\mu}[\lvert\underline{X}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},i}(t)-\underline{X}_{R}^{N_{\mathfrak{n}},i}(u)\rvert^{4}]\leq c_{\small\ref{;14}}c_{\small\ref{;X2k}}\lvert t-u\rvert^{2}.

We have thus completed the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.5
.

From Lemma
12.1
and Lemma
12.2
, we have (
2.64
).
From (
8.11
), we have (
2.65
). We have thus completed the proof.
∎

13.
Appendices

13.1.
Topology of the unlabeled and labeled path spaces.

Let
\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s,i}}=\{\mathsf{s}\in\mathsf{S}\,;\,\mathsf{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\infty,\mathsf{s}(\{s\})\in\{0,1\}\text{ for all }s\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
.
Let
W(\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s,i}})
be the set of all
\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s,i}}
-valued continuous paths defined on
[0,\infty)
.
We quote:

Lemma 13.1

(
[
28
, Lem. 7.1]
)
.

Each
\mathsf{w}=\{\mathsf{w}_{t}\}\in W(\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s,i}})
can be written as

\displaystyle\mathsf{w}_{t}=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\delta_{w^{i}(t)},\quad w^{i}=\{w^{i}(t)\}\in C(I_{i};\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Here,
I_{i}
is an interval of the form
[0,b_{i})
or
(a_{i},b_{i})
,
where
0\leq a_{i}<b_{i}\leq\infty
.
For a given
\mathsf{w}\in W(\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s,i}})
, the set
\{(w^{i},I_{i}),i\in\mathbb{N}\}

is uniquely determined (except labeling).

For
\mathbf{w}=(w^{i})_{i=1}^{\infty}
, we set
\mathfrak{u}_{\mathrm{path}}(\mathbf{w})=\mathsf{w}
, where

\mathsf{w}_{t}=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\delta_{w^{i}(t)}
.

Lemma 13.2

(
[
28
, Lem. 7.2]
)
.

For a label
\mathfrak{l}
, we have a unique
\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}\!:\!W_{\mathrm{NE}}(\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s,i}})\!\to\!C([0,\infty);\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}}

such that
\mathfrak{l}(\mathsf{w}_{0})=\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}(\mathsf{w})_{0}
and
\mathfrak{u}_{\mathrm{path}}\circ\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{path}}(\mathsf{w})=\mathsf{w}
.

Let
\|u-v\|_{T}=\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|u(t)-v(t)|
,

\mathbf{u}=(u^{i})_{i=1}^{\infty}
, and
\mathbf{v}=(v^{i})_{i=1}^{\infty}
. We set

\displaystyle\varrho_{\mathrm{path}}(u,v)=\sum_{T=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{T}}(1\wedge\|u-v\|_{T}),\quad\varrho_{\mathrm{lpath}}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{i}}(1\wedge\varrho_{\mathrm{path}}(u^{i},v^{i})).

We endow
C([0,\infty);\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}}
with
\varrho_{\mathrm{lpath}}
.
C([0,\infty);\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}}
is a complete separable metric space under
\varrho_{\mathrm{lpath}}
. Let
\varrho_{\mathrm{Proho}}
be the Proholov metric on
\mathsf{S}
:

\displaystyle\varrho_{\mathrm{Proho}}(\mathsf{s},\mathsf{s}^{\prime})=\inf\{\epsilon>0;\mathsf{s}(B)\leq\mathsf{s}^{\prime}(B^{\epsilon})+\epsilon,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}(B)\leq\mathsf{s}(B^{\epsilon})+\epsilon\}.

Here,
B^{\epsilon}
is the open
\epsilon
-neighborhood of
B
.
Let
f_{R}\in C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be such that
0\leq f_{R}\leq 1
,
f_{R}(x)=1
on
S_{R}
, and
f_{R+1}(x)=0

on
S_{R+1}^{c}
. Let
f\mathsf{s}=\sum_{i}f(s^{i})\delta_{s^{i}}
be the measure
f\mathsf{s}
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Then, the vague topology on
\mathsf{S}
is given by the separable, complete metric defined by

\displaystyle\varrho_{\mathrm{vague}}(\mathsf{s},\mathsf{s}^{\prime})=\sum_{R=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{R}}\Big(1\wedge\varrho_{\mathrm{Proho}}(f_{R}\mathsf{s},f_{R}\mathsf{s}^{\prime})\Big).

We refer to
[
12
]
for these metrics. We set

\displaystyle\varrho_{\mathrm{upath}}(\mathsf{w},\mathsf{w}^{\prime})=\sum_{T=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{T}}\Big(1\wedge\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\varrho_{\mathrm{vague}}(\mathsf{w}_{t},\mathsf{w}_{t}^{\prime})\Big).

Then,
C([0,\infty);\mathsf{S})
is a complete separable metric space under
\varrho_{\mathrm{upath}}
.
We set

\displaystyle\text{$\mathcal{NBJ}=\{\mathbf{w}\in C([0,\infty);\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}};\text{$I_{R,T}(\mathbf{w})<\infty$
for each $R,T\in\mathbb{N}$}\}$},

where
I_{R,T}
is defined by (
2.54
).

Lemma 13.3

(
[
28
, Lem. 7.3]
)
.

i

\mathfrak{u}_{\mathrm{path}}(\mathbf{w})\in C([0,\infty);\mathsf{S})
for
\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{NBJ}
.

ii

\mathfrak{u}_{\mathrm{path}}\!:\!\mathcal{NBJ}\!\to\!C([0,\infty);\mathsf{S})
is continuous.

13.2.
Closability and extension of Dirichlet forms and strongly local, quasi-regular Dirichlet forms.

In Subsection
13.2
, we quote the concepts of closability and extension of Dirichlet forms, strongly local, quasi-regular Dirichlet forms, and related quantities following
[
5
,
7
]
.
We repeatedly use them in the present paper.

Let
S
be a Polish space, that is, a topological space homeomorphic to a separable and complete metric space. Let
\nu
be a Radon measure on
S
.
We set
L^{2}(\nu)=L^{2}(S,\nu)
.

For non-negative symmetric bilinear forms
(\mathscr{E}_{1},\mathscr{D}_{1})
and
(\mathscr{E}_{2},\mathscr{D}_{2})
on
L^{2}(\nu)
, we say
(\mathscr{E}_{2},\mathscr{D}_{2})
is an extension of
(\mathscr{E}_{1},\mathscr{D}_{1})
if

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{1}\subset\mathscr{D}_{2},\quad\mathscr{E}_{1}(f,f)=\mathscr{E}_{2}(f,f)\quad\text{ for all }f\in\mathscr{D}_{1}.

(13.1)

We write
(\mathscr{E}_{1},\mathscr{D}_{1})\prec(\mathscr{E}_{2},\mathscr{D}_{2})
if

(\mathscr{E}_{2},\mathscr{D}_{2})
is an extension of
(\mathscr{E}_{1},\mathscr{D}_{1})
on
L^{2}(\nu)
.

A non-negative, closed form
(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{D})
on
L^{2}(\nu)
is a densely defined bilinear form defined on
\mathscr{D}
that is complete under the inner product
\mathscr{E}(f,g)+(f,g)_{L^{2}(\nu)}
.

Definition 13.1
.

[Closable and closure
[
5
,
7
]
] i
A non-negative symmetric bilinear form
(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{D}_{0})
densely defined on
L^{2}(\nu)
is called closable on
L^{2}(\nu)
if, for any
\mathscr{E}
-Cauchy sequence
f_{n}\in\mathscr{D}_{0}
such that
\lim\|f_{n}\|_{L^{2}(\nu)}=0
as
n\to\infty
,
it holds that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathscr{E}(f_{n},f_{n})=0
.

ii
If
(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{D}_{0})
is closable on
L^{2}(\nu)
, then there exists
a closed extension of
(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{D}_{0})
.
The smallest closed extension
(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{D})
of
(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{D}_{0})
is called the closure of
(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{D}_{0})
.

The following simple fact is used repeatedly in the present paper.

Lemma 13.4
.

Let
(\mathscr{E}_{1},\mathscr{D}_{1})
and
(\mathscr{E}_{2},\mathscr{D}_{2})
be non-negative symmetric bilinear forms on
L^{2}(\nu)
.
Let
(\mathscr{E}_{2},\mathscr{D}_{2})
be an extension of
(\mathscr{E}_{1},\mathscr{D}_{1})
. Let
(\mathscr{E}_{2},\mathscr{D}_{2})
be closable on
L^{2}(\nu)
. Then,
(\mathscr{E}_{1},\mathscr{D}_{1})
is closable on
L^{2}(\nu)
.

Proof.

If
\{f_{n}\}
is a Cauchy sequence of
(\mathscr{E}_{1},\mathscr{D}_{1})
, then
\{f_{n}\}
is a Cauchy sequence of
(\mathscr{E}_{2},\mathscr{D}_{2})
by (
13.1
).
Then,
\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathscr{E}_{2}(f_{n},f_{n})=0
because of the closability of
(\mathscr{E}_{2},\mathscr{D}_{2})
on
L^{2}(\nu)
.
Hence,
\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathscr{E}_{1}(f_{n},f_{n})=0
from (
13.1
). This completes the proof.
∎

A sequence of closed non-negative bilinear forms
(\mathscr{E}^{n},\mathscr{D}^{n})
on
L^{2}(\nu)
is said to converge to
(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{D})
on
L^{2}(\nu)
in the strong resolvent sense if their
\alpha
-resolvents
G_{\alpha}^{n}
converge strongly to the
\alpha
-resolvent
G_{\alpha}
of
(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{D})
in
L^{2}(\nu)
for each
\alpha>0
(cf.
[
38
]
). This implies that the
L^{2}(\nu)
-semi-groups
T_{t}^{n}
associated with
(\mathscr{E}^{n},\mathscr{D}^{n})
on
L^{2}(\nu)
converge strongly to the
L^{2}(\nu)
-semigroup
T_{t}
associated with
(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{D})
on
L^{2}(\nu)
for each
t\geq 0
.

For non-negative symmetric bilinear forms
(\mathscr{E}^{1},\mathscr{D}^{1})
and
(\mathscr{E}^{2},\mathscr{D}^{2})
, we write
(\mathscr{E}^{1},\mathscr{D}^{1})\leq(\mathscr{E}^{2},\mathscr{D}^{2})
if

\mathscr{D}^{1}\supset\mathscr{D}^{2}
and

\mathscr{E}^{1}(f,f)\leq\mathscr{E}^{2}(f,f)
for all
f\in\mathscr{D}^{2}
.
We write
(\mathscr{E}^{1},\mathscr{D}^{1})\geq(\mathscr{E}^{2},\mathscr{D}^{2})
if

(\mathscr{E}^{2},\mathscr{D}^{2})\leq(\mathscr{E}^{1},\mathscr{D}^{1})
.
A sequence of symmetric non-negative bilinear forms
\{(\mathscr{E}^{n},\mathscr{D}^{n})\}
on
L^{2}(\nu)
is said to be increasing if
(\mathscr{E}^{n},\mathscr{D}^{n})\leq(\mathscr{E}^{n+1},\mathscr{D}^{n+1})
for all
n
and decreasing if
(\mathscr{E}^{n},\mathscr{D}^{n})\geq(\mathscr{E}^{n+1},\mathscr{D}^{n+1})
for all
n
.

Lemma 13.5

(
[
38
, p.373, Th. S.16]
)
.

i

Let
\{(\mathscr{E}^{n},\mathscr{D}^{n})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be an increasing sequence of non-negative symmetric closed bilinear forms on
L^{2}(\nu)
.
Let
(\mathscr{E}^{\infty},\mathscr{D}^{\infty})
be the symmetric bilinear form defined by

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(f,f)=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathscr{E}^{n}(f,f),\quad\mathscr{D}^{\infty}=\{f\in\bigcap_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathscr{D}^{n}\,;\,\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathscr{E}^{n}(f,f)<\infty\}.

(13.2)

Then,
(\mathscr{E}^{\infty},\mathscr{D}^{\infty})
is a closed symmetric bilinear form on
L^{2}(\nu)
.

\{(\mathscr{E}^{n},\mathscr{D}^{n})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges to
(\mathscr{E}^{\infty},\mathscr{D}^{\infty})
in the strong resolvent sense on
L^{2}(\nu)
.

ii

Let
\{(\mathscr{E}^{n},\mathscr{D}^{n})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a decreasing sequence of non-negative symmetric closed bilinear forms on
L^{2}(\nu)
.
Let
(\mathscr{E}^{\infty},\mathscr{D}^{\infty})
be the symmetric bilinear form defined by

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(f,f)=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathscr{E}^{n}(f,f),\quad\mathscr{D}^{\infty}=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathscr{D}^{n}.

Then,

\{(\mathscr{E}^{n},\mathscr{D}^{n})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges to
(\mathscr{E}_{\mathrm{max}}^{\infty},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{max}}^{\infty})
in the strong resolvent sense on
L^{2}(\nu)
.
Here,
(\mathscr{E}_{\mathrm{max}}^{\infty},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{max}}^{\infty})
is the maximal closable part of
(\mathscr{E}_{\mathrm{max}}^{\infty},\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{max}}^{\infty})
on
L^{2}(\nu)
.

Definition 13.2

(Dirichlet form
[
7
]
)
.

We say a closed non-negative symmetric bilinear form
(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{D})
on
L^{2}(\nu)
is
a symmetric Dirichlet form if any
u\in\mathscr{D}
satisfies

\displaystyle v:=\min\{1,\max\{0,u\}\}\in\mathscr{D}\text{ and }\mathscr{E}(v,v)\leq\mathscr{E}(u,u).

(13.3)

For a symmetric Dirichlet form, there exists an associated symmetric Markovian
L^{2}
-semi-group
[
7
]
. We now recall the concept of capacity
[
7
,
5
]
.

Definition 13.3

(Capacity
[
5
,
7
]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{O}
be the family of all open subsets of
S
.
For
{A}\in\mathcal{O}
, let

\mathcal{L}_{{A},1}=\{f\in\mathscr{D};f\geq 1\ \mu\text{-a.e.\,on }{A}\}
.
We set
\mathcal{O}_{0}=\{{A}\in\mathcal{O};\mathcal{L}_{{A},1}\neq\emptyset\}
.
Let
\mathscr{E}_{1}=\mathscr{E}+(\cdot,*)_{L^{2}(\nu)}
.
For an open set
{A}\in\mathcal{O}
, we set

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cap}({A})=\begin{cases}\inf\{\mathscr{E}_{1}(f,f);f\in\mathcal{L}_{{A},1}\},&{A}\in\mathcal{O}_{0}\\
\infty,&{A}\notin\mathcal{O}_{0}.\end{cases}

For any set
{B}\subset S
, we set

\mathrm{Cap}({B})=\inf\{\mathrm{Cap}({A});{A}\in\mathcal{O},\,{A}\supset{B}\}
.

An increasing sequence of closed sets
\{F_{k}\}
is called an
\mathscr{E}
-nest if
\cup_{k\geq 1}\mathscr{D}(F_{k})
is
\mathscr{E}_{1}
-dense, where

\mathscr{D}(F_{k})=\{f\in\mathscr{D};f=0\text{ $\nu$-a.e.\,on }F_{k}^{c}\}
and

\mathscr{E}_{1}=\mathscr{E}+(\cdot,\cdot)_{L^{2}(\nu)}
.
A function
f
is called
\mathscr{E}
-quasi-continuous if for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists an open set
\mathcal{O}
with
\mathrm{Cap}(\mathcal{O})<\epsilon
such that
f|_{S\backslash\mathcal{O}}
is finite and continuous.
We call a subset
\mathfrak{N}\subset S
an
\mathscr{E}
-polar set if
there exists an
\mathscr{E}
-nest
\{F_{k}\}
such that

\mathfrak{N}\subset\cap_{k}(S\backslash F_{k})
.

Definition 13.4
.

A Dirichlet form
(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{D})
on
L^{2}(\nu)
is called quasi-regular if:

(
QR1
)
there exists an
\mathscr{E}
-nest
\{F_{k},k\geq 1\}
consisting of compact sets;

(
QR2
)

there exists
\mathscr{E}+(\cdot,*)_{L^{2}(\nu)}
-dense subset of
\mathscr{D}

whose elements have
\mathscr{E}
-quasi-continuous
\mu
-version;

(
QR3
)

there exists
\{f_{k},k\geq 1\}\subset\mathscr{D}
having
\mathscr{E}
-quasi-continuous
\mu
-versions
\{\tilde{f}_{k},k\geq 1\}\subset\mathscr{D}
and an
\mathscr{E}
-polar set
\mathfrak{N}\subset S
such that

\{\tilde{f}_{k},k\geq 1\}\subset\mathscr{D}
separates the points on
S\backslash\mathfrak{N}
.

A Dirichlet form
(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{D})
on
L^{2}(\nu)
is called strongly local if
\mathscr{E}(f,g)=0
for any
f,g\in\mathscr{D}
such that
f
is constant on a neighborhood of the support of
g

[
5
]
.
A diffusion process is a family of Markov processes with continuous sample path and has the strong Markov property. We say a diffusion is conservative if it has an infinite lifetime.
If the Dirichlet form is strongly local and quasi-regular and the state space is homeomorphic to a complete separable metric space, then there exists a properly associate diffusion exists
[
5
]
.
We refer to
[
5
]
for the concept of properly associated.

In general, a Dirichlet form is not necessarily symmetric.
In the present paper, a Dirichlet form means a symmetric Dirichlet form.

13.3.
Quasi-regularity of
m
-labeled Dirichlet forms.

Let
\mu
be a random point field on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Let
\mu^{[m]}
be the
m
-reduced Campbell measure. Let

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}}(f,g)=\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\times\mathsf{S}}\mathbb{D}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{[m]}[f,g]d\mu^{[m]},

where
\mathfrak{a}
satisfies (
2.39
) and
\mathbb{D}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{[m]}
is defined by (
7.5
).
Let
\mathscr{D}_{\circ}^{\mu^{[m]}}
be as in (
7.12
):

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{\circ}^{\mu^{[m]}}=\{f\in\mathscr{D}_{\circ}^{[m]};\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}}(f,f)<\infty,\,f\in L^{2}(\mu^{[m]})\}.

We make assumptions:

A.1

(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}},\mathscr{D}_{\circ}^{\mu^{[m]}})
is closable on
L^{2}(\mu^{[m]})
.

A.2

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}k\mu(S_{R}^{k})<\infty

A.3

\mu
has a bounded
k
-density function on
\overline{S}_{R}^{k}
for each
R,k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Let
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{\circ})
be the closure of
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}},\mathscr{D}_{\circ}^{\mu^{[m]}})
on
L^{2}(\mu^{[m]})
.
We write
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu},\overline{\mathscr{D}}_{\circ}^{\mu})
for
m=0
.

Theorem 13.1

(
[
22
, Th. 1]
)
.

Assume
A.1
for
m=0
,
A.2
, and
A.3
.
Then, the Dirichlet form
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu},\overline{\mathscr{D}}_{\circ}^{\mu})
on
L^{2}(\mu)
is quasi-regular.

Proof.

Theorem
13.1
was proved by
[
22
, Th. 1]
.
In
[
22
, Th. 1]
,
\mathfrak{a}
was supposed to be the unit matrix.
It is obvious that Theorem
13.1
follows from this case as well because, from (
2.39
),
\mathfrak{a}
is uniformly elliptic and bounded.
∎

In the following theorem, we prove the quasi-regularity without assumption A.3.

Theorem 13.2
.

Assume
A.1
for
m=0
and
A.2
.
Then, the Dirichlet form
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu},\overline{\mathscr{D}}_{\circ}^{\mu})
on
L^{2}(\mu)
is quasi-regular.

Proof.

(
QR1
)
and
(
QR3
)
follow from A.1 and A.2 in the exactly same fashion as the proof of
[
22
, Th. 1]
.

In the proof of
[
22
, Th. 1]
, A.3 was used only in the proof of
(
QR2
)
.
More specifically, the use of A.3 was limited to the proof of 2.2 found in
[
22
, Lem. 2.4]
.
Here, 2.2
[
22
, Lem. 2.4]
is the statement such that, for a
\sigma[\pi_{S_{R-\delta}}]
-measurable bounded function
f
,
0<\delta<R
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\lVert\mathcal{J}_{R,\varepsilon}f(\mathsf{s})-f\rVert_{L^{2}(\mu)}=0,

(13.4)

where
\mathcal{J}_{R,\varepsilon}
is defined by (
13.6
).
Additionally, (
13.4
) was used solely to establish Lemma 2.4 3 in the same work, which asserts that

\displaystyle\text{$\mathcal{D}_{\circ}^{\mu}$ is dense in $L^{2}(\mu)$}.

(13.5)

Therefore, our goal is to demonstrate (
13.5
) without using A.3. To accomplish this, we will recall the mollifier
\mathcal{J}_{R,\varepsilon}
on
\mathsf{S}
that was introduced in
[
22
]
.

Let
j\!:\!\mathbb{R}^{d}\!\to\!\mathbb{R}
be a non-negative, smooth function such that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}j(x)dx=1
,
j(x)=0
for
|x|\geq 1/2
.
Let
j_{\varepsilon}(x)=\varepsilon^{d}j(x/\varepsilon)
and
j_{\varepsilon}^{m}((x_{1},\ldots,x_{i}))=\prod_{j=1}^{m}j_{\varepsilon}(x_{j})
.

For a
\sigma[\pi_{S_{R}}]
-measurable function
f
, we set

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{R,\varepsilon}f(\mathsf{s})=\begin{cases}j_{\varepsilon}^{m}*f_{R}^{m}(\mathbf{x}_{R}^{m}(\mathsf{s}))&\text{ for }\mathsf{s}\in\mathsf{S}_{R}^{m}\quad(m\geq 1),\\
f(\mathsf{s})&\text{ for }\mathsf{s}\in\mathsf{S}_{R}^{0}.\end{cases}

(13.6)

Here,
f_{R}^{m}
is
S_{R}^{m}
-representation of
f
and

\mathbf{x}_{R}^{m}(\mathsf{s})
is the
S_{R}^{m}
-coordinate introduced in Section
1
.
Additionally,
*
denotes the convolution in
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}
, that is,

\displaystyle j_{\varepsilon}^{m}*f_{R}^{m}(\mathbf{x})=\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}}j_{\varepsilon}^{m}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})f_{R}^{m}(\mathbf{y})d\mathbf{y},

(13.7)

where we set
f_{R}^{m}(\mathbf{x})=0
for
\mathbf{x}\not\in S_{R}^{m}
and we assume
f_{R}^{m}\in L^{1}(S_{R}^{m},d\mathbf{x})
to make (
13.7
) meaningful.
We now prove that
\mathcal{D}_{\circ}^{\mu}
is dense in
L^{2}(\mu)
.

Let
0<\delta<R
(
\delta\in\mathbb{R}
) and
f\in C_{b}(\mathsf{S})\cap\sigma[\pi_{S_{R-\delta}}]
.
Then
f
is a bounded continuous,
\sigma[\pi_{S_{R-\delta}}]
-measurable function on
\mathsf{S}
by definition.
From
[
22
, Lem. 2.4 2.1]
we see
\mathcal{J}_{R,\varepsilon}f\in\mathcal{D}_{\circ}^{\mu}

for
0<\varepsilon<\delta
. Moreover, because
f\in C_{b}(\mathsf{S})
, we see from (
13.6
) that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathcal{J}_{R,\varepsilon}f(\mathsf{s})=f(\mathsf{s})\quad\text{ for each }\mathsf{s},

(13.8)

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathsf{s}\in\mathsf{S}}|\mathcal{J}_{R,\varepsilon}f(\mathsf{s})|\leq\sup_{\mathsf{s}\in\mathsf{S}}|f(\mathsf{s})|<\infty.

(13.9)

From (
13.8
)–(
13.9
) we can apply the Lebesgue convergence theorem to obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{\mathsf{S}}|\mathcal{J}_{R,\varepsilon}f(\mathsf{s})-f(\mathsf{s})|^{2}\mu(d\mathsf{s})=\int_{\mathsf{S}}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}|\mathcal{J}_{R,\varepsilon}f(\mathsf{s})-f(\mathsf{s})|^{2}\mu(d\mathsf{s})=0

(13.10)

for each
R\in\mathbb{N}
. Because

\cup_{0<\delta<R,\,\delta\in\mathbb{R},R\in\mathbb{N}}C_{b}(\mathsf{S})\cap\sigma[\pi_{S_{R-\delta}}]

is dense in
L^{2}(\mu)
, we see that
\mathcal{D}_{\circ}^{\mu}
is dense in
L^{2}(\mu)
from (
13.10
).
Thus, we have obtained (
13.5
) and completed the proof of Theorem
13.2
.
∎

In Theorem
13.2
and Theorem
13.3
, we significantly relax the assumptions introduced in Theorem
13.1
. Specifically, if
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}},\mathscr{D}_{\circ}^{\mu^{[m]}})
is found to be closable on
L^{2}(\mu^{[m]})
, then the existence of a density function is no longer necessary.
The random point field
\mu_{\mathrm{GAF}}
, defined by the zeros of the planar Gaussian analytic function, does not possess a density function due to its number and mean rigidities
[
8
,
9
]
.
Therefore, the stochastic dynamics that are reversible with respect to
\mu_{\mathrm{GAF}}
serve as a notable example where our results can be applied very effectively.

Theorem 13.3
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
.
Assume
A.1
for
m
and
A.2
. Then,
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{\circ})
is a quasi-regular Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(\mu^{[m]})
.

Proof.

Our goal is to prove
(
QR1
)
,
(
QR2
)
, and
(
QR3
)
for
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{\circ})
on
L^{2}(\mu^{[m]})
.

Assumptions
(
QR2
)
and
(
QR3
)
are obvious.
Indeed,
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}},\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{\circ})
is the closure of
(\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}},\mathscr{D}_{\circ}^{\mu^{[m]}})
on
L^{2}(\mu^{[m]})
and
\mathscr{D}_{\circ}^{\mu^{[m]}}\subset C((\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\times\mathsf{S})
.
Each element of
\mathscr{D}_{\circ}^{\mu^{[m]}}
is continuous, which yields
(
QR2
)
.
From
\mathscr{D}_{\circ}^{\mu^{[m]}}
, we can extract a countable dense subset, which yields
(
QR3
)
.
Thus, it only remains to prove
(
QR1
)
.

From Theorem
13.2
,
(
QR1
)
holds for
\mu
.
Hence, there exists an
\mathscr{E}^{\mu}
-nest
\{\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{r}}\}_{\mathsf{r}\in\mathbb{N}}
consisting of compact sets.
Then, using the characterization of compact sets in
\mathsf{S}
,
we have an increasing sequence of increasing sequences of natural numbers
a_{\mathsf{r}}=\{a_{\mathsf{r}}(Q)\}_{Q\in\mathbb{N}}
,
\mathsf{r}\in\mathbb{N}
, such that

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{r}}\subset\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}],

where
\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}]
be the set defined by (
2.44
).
Then, clearly
\{\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}]\}
is a compact
\mathscr{E}^{\mu}
-nest. Let
a_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}=\{a_{\mathsf{r}}(Q)-m\}_{Q\in\mathbb{N}}
and

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{r}}^{[m]}=\overline{S}_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}\times\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}].

Then,
\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{r}}^{[m]}
,
\mathsf{r}\in\mathbb{N}
, is an increasing sequence of compact sets in
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\times\mathsf{S}
.
Let
\mu_{\mathsf{r}}=\mu(\cdot\cap\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}])
.
Let
\mu^{[m]}_{\mathsf{r}}
be the
m
-reduced Campbell measure of
\mu_{\mathsf{r}}
.
Then, the
m
-reduced Campbell measure
\mu^{[m]}
is defined as the increasing limit of
\mu^{[m]}_{\mathsf{r}}
as
\mathsf{r}\to\infty
and

\displaystyle\mu^{[m]}_{\mathsf{r}}=\mu^{[m]}(\cdot\cap\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{r}}^{[m]}).

We want to prove
\{\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{r}}^{[m]}\}
is an
\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}}
-nest.

Let
\tilde{\mu}_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}=\sum_{n=m}^{\infty}n(n-1)\cdots(n-m+1)\mu(\cdot\cap\mathsf{S}_{\mathsf{r}}^{n}\cap\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}])
.
The number of particles in
S_{\mathsf{r}}
under
\mu^{[m]}(\cdot\cap\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{r}}^{[m]})
is bounded by
a_{\mathsf{r}}(\mathsf{r})
by
a_{\mathsf{r}}=\{a_{\mathsf{r}}(Q)\}_{Q\in\mathbb{N}}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mu}_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}=\sum_{n=m}^{a_{\mathsf{r}}(\mathsf{r})}n(n-1)\cdots(n-m+1)\mu(\cdot\cap\mathsf{S}_{\mathsf{r}}^{n}\cap\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}]).

(13.11)

Let

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\tilde{\mu}_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}}(f,g)=\int_{\mathsf{S}}\mathbb{D}_{\mathfrak{a}}[f,g]d\tilde{\mu}_{\mathsf{r}}^{m},

\displaystyle\overline{\mathscr{D}}_{\circ}^{\tilde{\mu}_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}}=\{f\in\overline{\mathscr{D}}_{\circ}^{\mu};\mathscr{E}^{\tilde{\mu}_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}}(f,f)<\infty,\,f\in L^{2}(\tilde{\mu}_{\mathsf{r}}^{m})\}.

(13.12)

For a closed set
A
, we set

\displaystyle\overline{\mathscr{D}}_{\circ}^{\tilde{\mu}_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}}(A)=\{f\in\overline{\mathscr{D}}_{\circ}^{\tilde{\mu}_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}};f(\mathsf{s})=0\text{ for $\tilde{\mu}_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}$-a.e.\,}\mathsf{s}\in A^{c}\},

\displaystyle\overline{\mathscr{D}}_{\circ}^{\mu^{[m]}_{\mathsf{r}}}(A)=\{f\in\overline{\mathscr{D}}_{\circ}^{\mu^{[m]}_{\mathsf{r}}};f(\mathsf{s})=0\text{ for $\mu^{[m]}_{\mathsf{r}}$-a.e.\,}\mathsf{s}\in A^{c}\}.

Let

\displaystyle\overline{\mathscr{D}}_{\circ}^{\tilde{\mu}_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}}(\{\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}]\})=\bigcup_{\mathsf{r}=1}^{\infty}\overline{\mathscr{D}}_{\circ}^{\tilde{\mu}_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}}(\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}]),

\displaystyle\overline{\mathscr{D}}_{\circ}^{\mu^{[m]}_{\mathsf{r}}}(\{\overline{S}_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}\times\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}]\})=\bigcup_{\mathsf{r}=1}^{\infty}\overline{\mathscr{D}}_{\circ}^{\mu^{[m]}_{\mathsf{r}}}(\overline{S}_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}\times\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}]).

From (
13.11
) and (
13.12
),
\overline{\mathscr{D}}_{\circ}^{\tilde{\mu}_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}}(\{\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}]\})
is dense in

\overline{\mathscr{D}}_{\circ}^{\tilde{\mu}_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}}
with respect to the norm

\displaystyle\lVert f\rVert_{\mathscr{E}_{1}^{\tilde{\mu}_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}}}=\{\mathscr{E}^{\tilde{\mu}_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}}(f,f)+\int_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert f\rvert^{2}d\tilde{\mu}_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}\}^{1/2}.

Let
f\otimes g\in\mathscr{D}_{\circ}^{\mu^{[m]}}
and
f\otimes h\in C_{0}^{\infty}((\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m})\otimes\overline{\mathscr{D}}_{\circ}^{\tilde{\mu}_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}}(\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}])
.
Then,
f\otimes h\in\mathscr{D}_{\circ}^{\mu^{[m]}}
.

Let
\mathsf{r}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
f({x})=0
for
{x}\notin S_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}
and that
g
is
\sigma[\pi_{S_{\mathsf{r}}}]
-measurable. Let
(f\otimes g)_{\mathrm{sym}}
be the symmetrization of
f\otimes g
defined by

(f\otimes g)_{\mathrm{sym}}(\mathsf{s})=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{m}}f(\sigma({x}))g(\mathsf{t})
, where
\mathsf{s}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\delta_{{x}_{i}}+\mathsf{t}
and
\mathfrak{S}_{m}
is the symmetric group of order
m
.
Then,
h_{\mathrm{sym}}\in\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}]
.

Let
c_{13.1}
and
c_{13.2}
be constants defined by

\displaystyle c_{\small\ref{;QR3b}}=\sup_{{x}\in S_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}}\lvert\nabla f({x})\rvert^{2}+\sup_{{x}\in S_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}}\lvert f({x})\rvert^{2},\quad c_{\small\ref{;QR3n}}=\sum_{n=m}^{a_{\mathsf{r}}(\mathsf{r})}n(n-1)\cdots(n-m+1).

By a straightforward calculation, we have, writing
\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{1}(f,f)=\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{1}(f)
,

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{1}(f\otimes g-f\otimes h)=

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{1}(f\otimes(g-h))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{\small\ref{;QR3b}}\mathscr{E}^{\mu^{[m]}_{\mathsf{r}}}_{1}(1\otimes(g-h))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{\small\ref{;QR3b}}\mathscr{E}_{1}^{\tilde{\mu}_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}}((g-h)_{\mathrm{sym}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{\small\ref{;QR3b}}c_{\small\ref{;QR3n}}\mathscr{E}^{\mu_{\mathsf{r}}}((g-h)_{\mathrm{sym}})\quad\text{ by }\eqref{:QR3i},\eqref{:QR3j}.

(13.13)

Let
\mathscr{D}^{[m]}=\cup_{\mathsf{r}}C_{0}^{\infty}(S_{\mathsf{r}}^{m})\otimes\mathscr{D}_{\circ}(\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}])
.
Recall that
a_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}=\{a_{\mathsf{r}}(Q)-m\}_{Q\in\mathbb{N}}
.
Note that
\mathscr{D}_{\circ}(\{\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}]\})
is dense in
\overline{\mathscr{D}}_{\circ}^{\mu}
because
\{\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{r}}]\}
is an
\mathscr{E}^{\mu}
-nest.
Then,
\mathscr{D}^{[m]}
is dense in
\mathscr{D}_{\circ}^{\mu^{[m]}}
from (
13.13
).
Hence,
\mathscr{D}^{[m]}
is dense in
\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu^{[m]}}_{\circ}
, which implies
(
QR1
)
.
∎

13.4.
Weak solution and the IFC condition to ISDEs.

In Subsection
13.4
, we quote the IFC condition from
[
34
]
. Let

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s,i}}=\{\mathsf{s}\in\mathsf{S}\,;\,\mathsf{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\infty,\mathsf{s}(\{s\})\in\{0,1\}\text{ for all }s\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}.

Let
\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}}
be a Borel subset of
\mathsf{S}
such that
\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}}\subset\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s,i}}
.
Let
\mathfrak{u}^{[1,0]}
be the map on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{S}
such that
\mathfrak{u}^{[1,0]}(x,\mathsf{s})=\delta_{x}+\mathsf{s}
.
Let
\mathbf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}}=\mathfrak{u}^{-1}(\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}})
and
\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}}^{[1]}=(\mathfrak{u}^{[1,0]})^{-1}(\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}})
.

Let

\sigma\!:\!\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}}^{[1]}\!\to\!\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}}
and
\mathsf{b}\!:\!\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}}^{[1]}\!\to\!\mathbb{R}^{d}
be Borel measurable functions.
We consider the ISDE of
\mathbf{X}=(X^{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
with state space
\mathbf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}}
such that

\displaystyle dX_{t}^{i}=\sigma(X_{t}^{i},\mathsf{X}_{t}^{i\diamond})dB_{t}^{i}+\mathsf{b}(X_{t}^{i},\mathsf{X}_{t}^{i\diamond})dt\ (i\in\mathbb{N}),\quad\mathbf{X}_{t}\in\mathbf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}},\forall t\in[0,\infty).

(13.14)

Here, we set
\mathsf{X}
and
\mathsf{X}^{i\diamond}
as

\mathsf{X}_{t}=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\delta_{X_{t}^{i}}
and

\mathsf{X}_{t}^{i\diamond}=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N},\ j\not=i}\delta_{X_{t}^{j}}
.
We take
\mathbf{B}=(B^{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
to be an
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}}
-valued Brownian motion.
That is,
\{B^{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
are independent copies of a
d
-dimensional Brownian motion starting at the origin.

Definition 13.5

(weak solution)
.

By a weak solution of ISDE (
13.14
), we mean an
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}}\times(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}}
-valued stochastic process
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{B})

defined on a probability space
(\Omega,\mathscr{F},P)

with a reference family
\{\mathscr{F}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
such that

i
\mathbf{X}=(X^{i})_{i=1}^{\infty}
is an
\{\mathscr{F}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
-adapted,
\mathbf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}}
-valued continuous process.

ii
\mathbf{B}=(B^{i})_{i=1}^{\infty}
is an
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}}
-valued

\{\mathscr{F}_{t}\}
-Brownian motion with
\mathbf{B}_{0}=\mathbf{0}
,

iii
the family of measurable
\{\mathscr{F}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
-adapted
processes
\Phi
and
\Psi
defined by

\displaystyle\Phi^{i}(t,\omega)=\sigma(X_{t}^{i}(\omega),\mathsf{X}_{t}^{i\diamond}(\omega)),\quad\Psi^{i}(t,\omega)=\mathsf{b}(X_{t}^{i}(\omega),\mathsf{X}_{t}^{i\diamond}(\omega))

belong to
\mathcal{L}^{2}
and
\mathcal{L}^{1}
, respectively.
Here,
\mathcal{L}^{p}
is the set of all measurable
\{\mathscr{F}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
-adapted
processes
\alpha
such that

E[\int_{0}^{T}|\alpha(t,\omega)|^{p}dt]<\infty
for all
T
.
Here, we can and do take a predictable version of
\Phi^{i}
and
\Psi^{i}
(see pp 45-46 in
[
11
]
).

iv with probability one, the process
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{B})
satisfies for all
t

\displaystyle X_{t}^{i}-X_{0}^{i}=\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(X_{u}^{i},\mathsf{X}_{u}^{i\diamond})dB_{u}^{i}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathsf{b}(X_{u}^{i},\mathsf{X}_{u}^{i\diamond})du\quad(i\in\mathbb{N}).

Let
\mathbf{X}=(X^{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
be a weak solution to (
13.14
) starting at
\mathbf{s}=\mathfrak{l}(\mathsf{s})
. Here,
\mathfrak{l}
is the label given in (
2.43
).
For
\mathbf{X}
, we introduce the family of finite-dimensional SDEs as follows.

Define
\sigma_{\mathsf{X}}^{m}\!:\![0,\infty)\times(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\!\to\!\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}}
and

\mathit{b}_{\mathsf{X}}^{m}\!:\![0,\infty)\times(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\!\to\!\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that, for
(u,\mathbf{v})\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}
and

\mathsf{v}=\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}\delta_{v_{i}}\in\mathsf{S}
, where

\mathbf{v}=(v_{1},\ldots,v_{m-1})\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m-1}
,

\displaystyle\sigma_{\mathsf{X}}^{m}(t,(u,\mathbf{v}))={\sigma}(u,\mathsf{v}+\mathsf{X}_{t}^{m*}),\quad\mathit{b}_{\mathsf{X}}^{m}(t,(u,\mathbf{v}))={\mathsf{b}}(u,\mathsf{v}+\mathsf{X}_{t}^{m*}).

Here,
\mathsf{X}_{t}^{m*}=\sum_{i=m+1}^{\infty}\delta_{X_{t}^{i}}
.
For
\mathbf{s}=(s^{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
, we set
\mathsf{s}_{m}^{*}=\sum_{i=m+1}^{\infty}\delta_{s^{i}}
.
Recall that
\mathbf{X}_{0}=\mathbf{s}=\mathfrak{l}(\mathsf{s})
.
We have
\mathsf{X}_{0}^{m*}=\mathsf{s}_{m}^{*}
by construction.
The coefficients
\sigma_{\mathsf{X}}^{m}
and
\mathit{b}_{\mathsf{X}}^{m}
depend on
both
\mathsf{X}^{m*}
and the label
\mathfrak{l}
.
In particular,
\mathsf{X}
gives a part of
\sigma_{\mathsf{X}}^{m}
and
\mathit{b}_{\mathsf{X}}^{m}
.
Let

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}}^{m}(t,\mathsf{w})=\{\mathbf{s}^{m}=(s^{1},\ldots,s^{m})\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m};\mathfrak{u}(\mathbf{s}^{m})+\mathsf{w}_{t}^{m*}\in\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}}\},

where
\mathsf{w}_{t}^{m*}=\sum_{i=m+1}^{\infty}\delta_{w_{t}^{i}}
for

\mathsf{w}_{t}=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\delta_{w_{t}^{i}}
.
By definition,
\mathbf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}}^{m}(t,\mathsf{w})
is a time-dependent domain in
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}
given by
\mathsf{w}_{t}^{m*}
.

We consider the SDE to
\mathbf{Y}^{m}=(Y^{m,i})_{i=1}^{m}
with random environment
\mathsf{X}
defined on
(\Omega,\mathscr{F},P_{\mathbf{s}},\{\mathscr{F}_{t}\})
such that

\displaystyle dY_{t}^{m,i}=\sigma_{\mathsf{X}}^{m}(t,(Y_{t}^{m,i},\mathbf{Y}_{t}^{m,i\diamond}))dB_{t}^{i}+\mathit{b}_{\mathsf{X}}^{m}(t,(Y_{t}^{m,i},\mathbf{Y}_{t}^{m,i\diamond}))dt,

\displaystyle\mathbf{Y}_{t}^{m}\in\mathbf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}}^{m}(t,\mathsf{X})\quad\text{ for all }t,

\displaystyle\mathbf{Y}_{0}^{m}=\mathbf{s}^{m},\quad\text{ where $\mathbf{s}^{m}=(s^{1},\ldots,s^{m})$ for $\mathbf{s}=(s^{i})\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}}$}.

(13.15)

Here,
\mathbf{Y}^{m,i\diamond}=(Y^{m,j})_{j\not=i}^{m}
and

\mathsf{Y}_{t}^{m,i\diamond}=\sum_{j\not=i}^{m}\delta_{Y_{t}^{m,j}}
.

For
\mathbf{X}=(X^{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
, let
\mathbf{X}^{m*}=(0,\ldots,0,X^{m+1},X^{m+2},...)
.
The first
m
components of
\mathbf{X}^{m*}
are constant paths
0
. A triplet
(\mathbf{Y}^{m},\mathbf{B}^{m},\mathbf{X}^{m*})
of continuous processes on
(\Omega,\mathscr{F},P_{\mathbf{s}},\{\mathscr{F}_{t}\})
satisfying (
13.15
) is called a weak solution.

We introduce the concept of a unique strong solution of (
13.15
).
Let
\widetilde{P}^{m}=P_{\mathbf{s}}\circ(\mathbf{B}^{m},\mathbf{X}^{m*})^{-1}
be the distribution of
(\mathbf{B}^{m},\mathbf{X}^{m*})
.
Let
W^{\mathbb{N}}=C([0,\infty);\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}}
,

W^{m}=C([0,\infty);\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}
, and
W_{\mathbf{0}}^{m}=\{\mathbf{w}\in W^{m};\mathbf{w}(0)=\mathbf{0}\}
.
We set
\mathscr{B}_{t}(W_{\mathbf{0}}^{m}\times W^{\mathbb{N}})=\sigma[(\mathbf{v}(s),\mathbf{w}(s));0\leq s\leq t]
,
\mathscr{C}^{m}_{t}=\overline{\mathscr{B}_{t}(W_{\mathbf{0}}^{m}\times W^{\mathbb{N}})}
, and
\mathscr{C}^{m}=\overline{\mathscr{B}(W_{\mathbf{0}}^{m}\times W^{\mathbb{N}})}
, where
\overline{\cdot}
denotes the completion with respect to
\widetilde{P}^{m}
.

Definition 13.6
.

i

\mathbf{Y}^{m}
is called a strong solution of (
13.15
) for
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{B})
under
P_{\mathbf{s}}
if

(\mathbf{Y}^{m},\mathbf{B}^{m},\mathbf{X}^{m*})
satisfies (
13.15
) and
there exists a
\mathscr{C}^{m}
-measurable function
F_{\mathbf{s}}^{m}\!:\!W_{\mathbf{0}}^{m}\times W^{\mathbb{N}}\!\to\!W^{m}

such that
F_{\mathbf{s}}^{m}
is
\mathscr{C}^{m}_{t}/\mathscr{B}_{t}^{m}(W^{m})
-measurable for each
t
, and
F_{\mathbf{s}}^{m}
satisfies
\mathbf{Y}^{m}=F_{\mathbf{s}}^{m}(\mathbf{B}^{m},\mathbf{X}^{m*})

P_{\mathbf{s}}
-a.s.,
where
\mathscr{B}_{t}^{m}(W^{m})=\sigma[\mathbf{w}(s);0\leq s\leq t]
.

ii
The SDE (
13.15
) is said to have a unique strong solution for
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{B})
under
P_{\mathbf{s}}
if there exists a function
F_{\mathbf{s}}^{m}
satisfying the conditions in Definition
13.6
i and,
for any weak solution
(\hat{\mathbf{Y}}^{m},\mathbf{B}^{m},\mathbf{X}^{m*})
of (
13.15
) under
P_{\mathbf{s}}
,

\hat{\mathbf{Y}}^{m}=F_{\mathbf{s}}^{m}(\mathbf{B}^{m},\mathbf{X}^{m*})
for
P_{\mathbf{s}}
-a.s.

Definition 13.7
.

For (
13.15
), the function
F_{\mathbf{s}}^{m}
in Definition
13.6
i is called a strong solution starting at
\mathbf{s}^{m}
.
(
13.15
) is said to have a unique strong solution
F_{\mathbf{s}}^{m}

if
F_{\mathbf{s}}^{m}
satisfies the condition in Definition
13.6
ii.

By construction, the function
F_{\mathbf{s}}^{m}
is unique for
\widetilde{P}^{m}
-a.s.

We introduce the IFC condition of
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{B})
defined on
(\Omega,\mathscr{F},P,\{\mathscr{F}_{t}\})
:

(
\mathbf{IFC}
)
The SDE (
13.15
) has a unique strong solution
F_{\mathbf{s}}^{m}(\mathbf{B}^{m},\mathbf{X}^{m*})

for
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{B})
under
P_{\mathbf{s}}
for
P\circ\mathbf{X}_{0}^{-1}
-a.s.
\mathbf{s}

for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
, where
P_{\mathbf{s}}=P(\cdot|\mathbf{X}_{0}=\mathbf{s})
.

13.5.
A unique strong solution of ISDEs.

In Subsection
13.5
, we quote a result on unique, strong solutions of ISDEs of (
13.14
) from
[
34
]
.
In
[
34
]
, we developed a theory of SDEs to infinite-particle systems based on the classical theory in
[
11
]
.

Definition 13.8

(uniqueness in law)
.

We say that the uniqueness in law of weak solutions for (
13.14
) with initial distribution
\nu
holds if
the laws of the processes
\mathbf{X}
and
\mathbf{X}^{\prime}
in
W^{\mathbb{N}}
coincide for any
weak solutions
\mathbf{X}
and
\mathbf{X}^{\prime}
with initial distribution
\nu
.

Definition 13.9

(pathwise uniqueness)
.

We say that the pathwise uniqueness of weak solutions of
(
13.14
) holds
if whenever
\mathbf{X}
and
\mathbf{X}^{\prime}
are two weak solutions
defined on the same probability space
(\Omega,\mathscr{F},P)
with the same reference family
\{\mathscr{F}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
and the same
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}}
-valued
\{\mathscr{F}_{t}\}
-Brownian motion
\mathbf{B}
such that
\mathbf{X}_{0}=\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\prime}
a.s.,
then

\displaystyle P(\text{$\mathbf{X}_{t}=\mathbf{X}_{t}^{\prime}$ for all $t\geq 0$})=1.

Definition 13.10

(pathwise uniqueness of weak solutions starting at
\mathbf{s}
)
.

We say that the pathwise uniqueness of weak solutions of
(
13.14
) starting at
\mathbf{s}
holds
if the condition of Definition
13.9
holds for

\mathbf{X}_{0}=\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\prime}=\mathbf{s}
a.s.

Let
P_{\mathrm{Br}}^{\infty}
be the distribution of an
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}}
-valued Brownian motion

\mathbf{B}
with
\mathbf{B}_{0}=\mathbf{0}
.
Let
W^{\mathbb{N}}=C([0,\infty);\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}}
.
Let
W_{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbb{N}}=\{\mathbf{w}\in W^{\mathbb{N}}\,;\mathbf{w}_{0}=\mathbf{0}\}
. Clearly,
P_{\mathrm{Br}}^{\infty}(W_{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbb{N}})=1
.
Let
\mathscr{B}_{t}(P_{\mathrm{Br}}^{\infty})
and
\mathscr{B}(P_{\mathrm{Br}}^{\infty})
be the completion of

\sigma[\mathbf{w}_{s};0\leq s\leq t,\,\mathbf{w}\in W_{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbb{N}}]
and
\mathscr{B}(W_{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbb{N}})

with respect to
P_{\mathrm{Br}}^{\infty}
, respectively.
Let
\mathscr{B}_{t}^{\infty}=\sigma[\mathbf{w}(s);0\leq s\leq t,\mathbf{w}\in W^{\mathbb{N}}]
.

Definition 13.11

(a strong solution starting at
\mathbf{s}
)
.

A weak solution
\mathbf{X}
of (
13.14
)
with an
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}}
-valued
\mathscr{F}_{t}
-Brownian motion

\mathbf{B}
is called a strong solution starting at
\mathbf{s}
defined on
(\Omega,\mathscr{F},P,\{\mathscr{F}_{t}\})

if
\mathbf{X}_{0}=\mathbf{s}
a.s.  and if there exists a function

F_{\mathbf{s}}\!:\!W_{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbb{N}}\!\to\!W^{\mathbb{N}}
such that

F_{\mathbf{s}}
is
\mathscr{B}(P_{\mathrm{Br}}^{\infty})/\mathscr{B}(W^{\mathbb{N}})
-measurable, and that

F_{\mathbf{s}}
is
\mathscr{B}_{t}(P_{\mathrm{Br}}^{\infty})/\mathscr{B}_{t}^{\infty}
-measurable for each
t
, and
that
F_{\mathbf{s}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}=F_{\mathbf{s}}(\mathbf{B})\quad\text{ a.s.}

Also we call
F_{\mathbf{s}}
itself a strong solution starting at
\mathbf{s}
.

Definition 13.12

(a unique strong solution starting at
\mathbf{s}
)
.

We say (
13.14
) has a unique strong solution starting at
\mathbf{s}
if
there exists a
\mathscr{B}(P_{\mathrm{Br}}^{\infty})/\mathscr{B}(W^{\mathbb{N}})
-measurable function

F_{\mathbf{s}}\!:\!W_{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbb{N}}\!\to\!W^{\mathbb{N}}
such that, for any weak solution
(\hat{\mathbf{X}},\hat{\mathbf{B}})

of (
13.14
) starting at
\mathbf{s}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{X}}=F_{\mathbf{s}}(\hat{\mathbf{B}})\quad\text{ a.s.}

and if, for any
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}}
-valued
\{\mathscr{F}_{t}\}
-Brownian motion
\mathbf{B}

defined on
(\Omega,\mathscr{F},P,\{\mathscr{F}_{t}\})
with
\mathbf{B}_{0}=\mathbf{0}
, the continuous process
F_{\mathbf{s}}(\mathbf{B})

is a strong solution of (
13.14
) starting at
\mathbf{s}
.
Also we call
F_{\mathbf{s}}
a unique strong solution starting at
\mathbf{s}
.

We next present a variant of the notion of a unique strong solution.

Definition 13.13

(a unique strong solution under constraint)
.

For a condition
(
\bullet
)
, we say (
13.14
) has a unique strong solution starting at
\mathbf{s}
under constraint
(
\bullet
)
if
there exists a
\mathscr{B}(P_{\mathrm{Br}}^{\infty})/\mathscr{B}(W^{\mathbb{N}})
-measurable function

F_{\mathbf{s}}\!:\!W_{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbb{N}}\!\to\!W^{\mathbb{N}}
such that for any weak solution

(\hat{\mathbf{X}},\hat{\mathbf{B}})
of (
13.14
) starting at
\mathbf{s}

satisfying
(
\bullet
)
, it holds that

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{X}}=F_{\mathbf{s}}(\hat{\mathbf{B}})\quad\text{ a.s.}

and if for any
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}}
-valued
\{\mathscr{F}_{t}\}
-Brownian motion
\mathbf{B}

defined on
(\Omega,\mathscr{F},P,\{\mathscr{F}_{t}\})
with
\mathbf{B}_{0}=\mathbf{0}
the continuous process
F_{\mathbf{s}}(\mathbf{B})

is a solution of (
13.14
) starting at
\mathbf{s}
satisfying
(
\bullet
)
.
Also we call
F_{\mathbf{s}}
a unique strong solution starting at
\mathbf{s}

under constraint
(
\bullet
)
.

Let
\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s,i}}=\{\mathsf{s}\in\mathsf{S}\,;\,\mathsf{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\infty,\mathsf{s}(\{s\})\in\{0,1\}\text{ for all }s\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
.
Let
W(\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s,i}})
be the set of all
\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s,i}}
-valued continuous paths defined on
[0,\infty)
.
Let

\displaystyle W_{\mathrm{NE}}(\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s,i}})=\{\mathsf{w}=\{(w^{i},I_{i})\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\in W(\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s,i}});I_{i}=[0,\infty)\text{ for all $i\in\mathbb{N}$}\},

\displaystyle I_{R,T}(\mathbf{w})=\sup\{i\in\mathbb{N};\min_{t\in[0,T]}\lvert w^{i}(t)\rvert\leq R\},\quad\text{where $\mathbf{w}\in W_{\mathrm{NE}}(\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s,i}})$}.

Let
\mathsf{X}
be the unlabeled path of

\mathbf{X}=(X^{i})
given by
\mathsf{X}_{t}=\sum_{i}\delta_{X_{t}^{i}}
, where
X^{i}=\{X_{t}^{i}\}
.
Recall the condition
(
IFC
)
introduced in Subsection
13.4
.
We make the assumptions.

(
SIN
)

P(\mathsf{X}\in W_{\mathrm{NE}}(\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s,i}}))=1
.

(
NBJ
)

P(I_{R,T}(\mathbf{X})<\infty)=1
for each
R,T\in\mathbb{N}
.

(
AC
)
μ

P\circ\mathsf{X}_{t}^{-1}
is absolutely continuous with respect to
\mu
for all
0<t<\infty
.

For a strong solution
F_{\mathbf{s}}
, we impose the following condition:

(
MF
)

P(F_{\mathbf{s}}(\mathbf{B})\in A)
is
\overline{\mathscr{B}((\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}})}^{P\circ\mathbf{X}_{0}^{-1}}
-measurable in
\mathbf{s}
for any
A\in\mathscr{B}(W^{\mathbb{N}})
.

Theorem 13.4

(
[
34
, Th. 3.1, p.1157]
)
.

Assume that
\mu
is tail trivial.
Assume that (

) has a weak solution
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{B})
under

)
,

)
,

, and

)
. Then, (

) has a family of unique strong solutions
\{F_{\mathbf{s}}\}
starting at
\mathbf{s}
for

-a.s.
\mathbf{s}
under constraints of

)
,
(

)
,

)

,
(

)
, and

)
.
