13.6.
Cut-off functions
\chi_{\mathsf{m}}
.

We will localize the coefficients of (
13.15
) to prove the IFC condition.
To do this, we introduce a sequence of subsets in
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\times\mathsf{S}
.

\sigma
-compact sets
\bigcup_{q=1}^{\infty}\mathsf{K}_{Q}[\mathit{a}_{q}]
in
\mathsf{S}
:

Let
\mathbf{a}=\{a_{\mathsf{q}}\}_{\mathsf{q}\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of
increasing sequences
a_{\mathsf{q}}=\{a_{\mathsf{q}}(R)\}_{R\in\mathbb{N}}
of natural numbers such that

a_{\mathsf{q}}(R)<a_{\mathsf{q}}(R+1)
and
a_{\mathsf{q}}(R)<a_{\mathsf{q}+1}(R)
for all
\mathsf{q},R\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{Q}[\mathit{a}_{q}]

\displaystyle=\bigcap_{R=1}^{Q}\{\mathsf{s}\in\mathsf{S}\,;\,\mathsf{s}(S_{R})\leq a_{\mathsf{q}}(R)\}\quad\text{for $Q\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}$}.

(13.16)

Then,
\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{q}}]\subset\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{q}+1}]
for all
\mathsf{q}\in\mathbb{N}
.
Note that
\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{q}}]
is a compact set in
\mathsf{S}
for each
\mathsf{q}\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\{a_{\mathsf{q}}^{+}(R)\}_{R\in\mathbb{N}}
be such that
a_{\mathsf{q}}^{+}(R)=1+a_{\mathsf{q}}(R+1)
.
We assume

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{Q}[\mathit{a}_{q}]\subset\mathsf{K}_{Q}[a_{\mathsf{q}}^{+}]\subset\mathsf{K}_{Q}[a_{\mathsf{q}+1}]\quad\text{ for all $Q\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}$}.

(13.17)

Remark 13.1
.

Note that
\mathsf{K}_{Q}[\mathit{a}_{q}]\subset\mathsf{K}_{Q}[a_{\mathsf{q}}^{+}]
is clear because
a_{\mathsf{q}}<a_{\mathsf{q}}^{+}
.
Let
C(q)
be an increasing function such that
C(q)\to\infty
.
Suppose
a_{\mathsf{q}}(R)=C(q)R^{\alpha}
for some
\alpha>0
.
Then, taking a new sequence from

\mathbf{a}=\{a_{\mathsf{q}}\}_{q\in\mathbb{N}}
more intermittently,
we can easily retake
\mathbf{a}
such that
a_{\mathsf{q}}^{+}(R)<a_{\mathsf{q}+1}(R)
for all
R\in\mathbb{N}
.
For such
\mathbf{a}
we obtain
\mathsf{K}_{Q}[a_{\mathsf{q}}^{+}]\subset\mathsf{K}_{Q}[a_{\mathsf{q}+1}]
.
Thus, (
13.17
) holds.

Cut-off compact sets
\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}
:

We introduce the approximation of
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\times\mathsf{S}
consisting of compact sets
\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}
.
Recall that
\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s,i}}
is the set consisting of infinite configurations with no multiple points.
Let
\mathbf{x}=({x}^{1},\ldots,{x}^{m})\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}
,
\mathfrak{u}(\mathbf{x})=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\delta_{{x}^{i}}
,
and
\mathsf{s}=\sum_{i}\delta_{s^{i}}
. We set

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s,i}}^{[m]}=\{(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\times\mathsf{S}\,;\,\mathfrak{u}(\mathbf{x})+\mathsf{s}\in\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s,i}}\}.

(13.18)

We set
\overline{S}^{m}_{\mathsf{r}}=\{{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d};|{x}|\leq\mathsf{r}\}^{m}
. Let
j,k,l=1,\ldots,m
and

\displaystyle S_{\mathsf{p},\mathsf{r}}^{m}(\mathsf{s})=\big\{\mathbf{x}\in S_{\mathsf{r}}^{m}\,;\,\inf_{j\not=k}|{x}^{j}-{x}^{k}|>2^{-\mathsf{p}},\inf_{l,i}|{x}^{l}-s^{i}|>2^{-\mathsf{p}}\big\},

\displaystyle\overline{S}^{m}_{\mathsf{p},\mathsf{r}}(\mathsf{s})=\big\{\mathbf{x}\in\overline{S}^{m}_{\mathsf{r}}\,;\,\min_{j\not=k}|{x}^{j}-{x}^{k}|\geq 2^{-\mathsf{p}},\ \inf_{l,i}|{x}^{l}-s^{i}|\geq 2^{-\mathsf{p}}\big\}.

(13.19)

Then,
S_{\mathsf{p},\mathsf{r}}^{m}(\mathsf{s})
is an open set and
\overline{S}^{m}_{\mathsf{p},\mathsf{r}}(\mathsf{s})
is its closure in
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}
.

Let
\mathbf{a}=\{a_{\mathsf{q}}\}_{\mathsf{q}\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of increasing sequences introduced before (
13.16
). For
\mathsf{m}=(\mathsf{p},\mathsf{q},\mathsf{r})
, we set

\displaystyle\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}^{\circ}=\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{p},\mathsf{q},\mathsf{r}}^{\circ}=\big\{(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})\in\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s,i}}^{[m]}\,;\,\ \mathbf{x}\in S_{\mathsf{p},\mathsf{r}}^{m}(\mathsf{s}),\,\mathsf{s}\in\mathsf{K}[\mathit{a}_{\mathsf{q}}^{+}]\big\},

(13.20)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}=\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{p},\mathsf{q},\mathsf{r}}=\big\{(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})\in\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s,i}}^{[m]}\,;\,\ \mathbf{x}\in\overline{S}^{m}_{\mathsf{p},\mathsf{r}}(\mathsf{s}),\ \mathsf{s}\in\mathsf{K}[\mathit{a}_{\mathsf{q}}^{+}]\big\}.

(13.21)

Then,
\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}^{\circ}
is an open set and
\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}
is the closure of
\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}^{\circ}
.
We easily see that
\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}
is a compact set.
Although
\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}^{\circ}
and
\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}
depend on
m\in\mathbb{N}
, we omit
m
from the notation.
Let

\displaystyle\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]=\bigcup_{\mathsf{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{3}}\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}^{\circ}=\bigcup_{\mathsf{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{3}}\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}.

(13.22)

We set
\mathbf{N}=\mathbb{N}\cup\mathbb{N}^{2}\cup\mathbb{N}^{3}
.
For
\mathsf{m}\in\mathbf{N}
, we define
\mathsf{m}+1\in\mathbf{N}
as

\displaystyle\mathsf{m}+1=\begin{cases}(\mathsf{p}+1,\mathsf{q},\mathsf{r})&\text{ for $\mathsf{m}=(\mathsf{p},\mathsf{q},\mathsf{r})\in\mathbb{N}^{3}$, }\\
(\mathsf{q}+1,\mathsf{r})&\text{ for $\mathsf{m}=(\mathsf{q},\mathsf{r})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}$, }\\
\mathsf{r}+1&\text{ for $\mathsf{m}=\mathsf{r}\in\mathbb{N}$.}\end{cases}

(13.23)

We set

\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}=\cup_{\mathsf{p}=1}^{\infty}\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{p},\mathsf{q},\mathsf{r}}
for
\mathsf{m}=(\mathsf{q},\mathsf{r})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
and

\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}=\cup_{\mathsf{q}=1}^{\infty}\cup_{\mathsf{p}=1}^{\infty}\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{p},\mathsf{q},\mathsf{r}}
for
\mathsf{m}=(\mathsf{r})\in\mathbb{N}
. We set
\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}^{\circ}
analogously. Clearly, for all
\mathsf{m}\in\mathbf{N}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}^{\circ}\subset\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}},\quad\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}\subset\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}+1}.

Cut-off functions
\chi_{\mathsf{m}}
on
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\times\mathsf{S}
:

Let
\tilde{\varphi}_{\mathsf{r}}\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be such that

\displaystyle\tilde{\varphi}_{\mathsf{r}}(t)=\begin{cases}1&\text{for $|t|\leq\mathsf{r}$}\\
0&\text{for $|t|\geq\mathsf{r}+1$},\end{cases}\quad 0\leq\tilde{\varphi}_{\mathsf{r}}(t)\leq 1,\quad\lvert\tilde{\varphi}_{\mathsf{r}}^{\prime}(t)\rvert\leq 2\quad\text{ for all $t\in\mathbb{R}$}.

We set
\varphi_{\mathsf{r}}(\mathbf{x})=\tilde{\varphi}_{\mathsf{r}}(\lvert\mathbf{x}\rvert)
.
Then,
\varphi_{\mathsf{r}}\in C_{0}^{\infty}((\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m})
,
0\leq\varphi_{\mathsf{r}}(\mathbf{x})\leq 1
, and
|\nabla\varphi_{\mathsf{r}}(\mathbf{x})|\leq 2
.
Let

h_{\mathsf{p}}\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})

(\mathsf{p}\in\{0\}\cup\mathbb{N})
such that

\displaystyle h_{\mathsf{p}}(t)=\begin{cases}1&(t\leq 2^{-\mathsf{p}-2}),\\
0&(2^{-\mathsf{p}-1}\leq t)\end{cases},\quad 0\leq h_{\mathsf{p}}(t)\leq 1,\quad|h_{\mathsf{p}}^{\prime}(t)|\leq 2^{\mathsf{p}+3}\quad\text{ for all }t.

We write
\mathbf{x}=({x}^{k})_{k=1}^{m}\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}
and
\mathsf{s}=\sum_{i}\delta_{s^{i}}\in\mathsf{S}
.
Let
h_{\mathsf{p}}^{[m]}\!:\!(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\times\mathsf{S}\!\to\!\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad h_{\mathsf{p}}^{[m]}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})=\prod_{k=1}^{m}\Big\{\prod_{j\not=k}^{m}\{1-h_{\mathsf{p}}(|{x}^{k}-{x}^{j}|)\}\Big\}\Big\{\prod_{i}\{1-h_{\mathsf{p}}(|{x}^{k}-s^{i}|)\}\Big\}.

We label
\mathsf{s}=\sum_{i}\delta_{s^{i}}
in such a way that
|s^{i}|\leq|s^{i+1}|
for all
i
.
Let
a_{\mathsf{q}}=\{a_{\mathsf{q}}(\mathsf{r})\}_{\mathsf{r}\in\mathbb{N}}
be the increasing sequences in (
13.16
).
We set for
Q\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}

\displaystyle\mathit{d}_{\mathsf{q}}^{Q}(\mathsf{s})=\Big\{\sum_{R=1}^{Q}\sum_{s^{i}\in\overline{S}_{R},i>a_{\mathsf{q}}(R)}(R-|s^{i}|)^{2}\Big\}^{1/2}.

(13.24)

We introduce the cut-off functions defined as

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathsf{r}}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\mathsf{r}}(\mathbf{x}),

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathsf{q},\mathsf{r}}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\mathsf{r}}(\mathbf{x})(h_{0}\circ\mathit{d}_{\mathsf{q}}^{\infty})(\mathsf{s}),

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathsf{p},\mathsf{q},\mathsf{r}}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\mathsf{r}}(\mathbf{x})(h_{0}\circ\mathit{d}_{\mathsf{q}}^{\infty})(\mathsf{s})h_{\mathsf{p}}^{[m]}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s}).

We set
\chi_{\mathsf{m}}=\chi_{\mathsf{p},\mathsf{q},\mathsf{r}}
. Then,
\{\chi_{\mathsf{m}}\}
are consistent in the sense that

\chi_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})=\chi_{\mathsf{m}+1}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})
for
(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})\in\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}
.
We suppress
m
from the notation of
\chi_{\mathsf{m}}
although
\chi_{\mathsf{m}}
depends on
m\in\mathbb{N}
.
We take the limit in
\mathsf{m}=(\mathsf{p},\mathsf{q},\mathsf{r})
along with the order
\mathsf{m}\mapsto\mathsf{m}+1
in (
13.23
).
Let

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathsf{m}}:=\lim_{\mathsf{r}\to\infty}\lim_{\mathsf{q}\to\infty}\lim_{\mathsf{p}\to\infty}.

(13.25)

Then, we easily see that

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathsf{m}}\chi_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})=1\quad\text{ for all $(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})\in\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]$}.

By a direct calculation similar to that in
[
22
, Lem. 2.5]
, we obtain the following.

Lemma 13.6
.

For each
m\in\mathbb{N}
, the functions
\chi_{\mathsf{m}}
,
\mathsf{m}\in\mathbf{N}
, satisfy the following.

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})=\begin{cases}0&\text{ for }(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})\not\in\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}+1}\\
1&\text{ for }(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})\in\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}\end{cases},\quad\chi_{\mathsf{m}}\in C_{0}((\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m})\otimes\mathscr{D}_{\bullet\mathrm{b}}^{[1]},

\displaystyle 0\leq\chi_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})\leq 1,\quad|\nabla_{\mathbf{x}}\chi_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})|^{2}\leq c_{\small\ref{;83a}},\quad\mathbb{D}[\chi_{\mathsf{m}},\chi_{\mathsf{m}}](\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})\leq c_{\small\ref{;83b}}.

Here,

c_{13.3}=c_{\small\ref{;83a}}(\mathsf{m})
and
c_{13.4}=c_{\small\ref{;83b}}(\mathsf{m})

are positive finite constants.

13.7.
A sufficient condition for
(
IFC
)
.

In Subsection
13.7
, we present a sufficient condition for the IFC condition.

Let
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{B})
be a weak solution of (
13.14
) defined on
(\Omega,\mathscr{F},P,\{\mathscr{F}_{t}\})
.
For
\mathbf{X}=(X^{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
, we set the
m
-labeled process
\mathbf{X}^{[m]}=(\mathbf{X}^{m},\mathsf{X}^{m*})
such that
\mathbf{X}^{m}=(X^{1},\ldots,X^{m})
and
\mathsf{X}_{t}^{m*}=\sum_{j=m+1}^{\infty}\delta_{X_{t}^{j}}
.
Let
\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}^{\circ}
be as in (
13.20
).
Let
\lim_{\mathsf{m}}
be as in (
13.25
).

We make an assumption.

\{
B1
\}

\mathbf{X}^{[m]}=(\mathbf{X}^{m},\mathsf{X}^{m*})
does not exit from

\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]=\bigcup_{\mathsf{m}\in\mathbf{N}}\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}^{\circ}
, that is,

\displaystyle P(\lim_{\mathsf{m}}\varsigma_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathbf{X}^{[m]})=\infty)=1,

where
\varsigma_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathbf{w}^{[m]})=\inf\{t>0;\mathbf{w}^{[m]}_{t}\not\in\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}^{\circ}\}
is the exit time from
\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}^{\circ}
.

Let
\sigma
and
\mathsf{b}
be the coefficients in (
13.14
).
We set

\displaystyle\sigma^{m}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})=(\sigma(x^{i},\mathsf{x}^{i\diamond}+\mathsf{s}))_{i=1}^{m},\quad\mathsf{b}^{m}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})=(\mathsf{b}(x^{i},\mathsf{x}^{i\diamond}+\mathsf{s}))_{i=1}^{m},

(13.26)

where

\mathbf{x}=(x^{i})_{i=1}^{m}\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}
and
\mathsf{x}^{i\diamond}=\sum_{j\neq i}\delta_{x^{j}}
.

Let
S_{\mathsf{p},\mathsf{r}}^{m}(\mathsf{s})
be as in (
13.19
).
For
\mathsf{m}=(\mathsf{p},\mathsf{q},\mathsf{r})\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
and

, we set

if

and

are in the same connected component of

and
\mathsf{s}\in\Pi_{2}(\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}})
.
Here,

is a projection
\Pi_{2}\!:\!(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\times\mathsf{S}\!\to\!\mathsf{S}
given by
\Pi_{2}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})=\mathsf{s}
and
\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}=\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{p},\mathsf{q},\mathsf{r}}
is denoted by (
13.21
).
We extend the domain of
\mathfrak{u}
from
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}
to
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\times\mathsf{S}
such that

\mathfrak{u}^{[m,0]}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})=\mathfrak{u}(\mathbf{x})+\mathsf{s}
.

Let
\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}}
be the set defined before (
13.14
).
By definition,
\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}}
is the set such that the coefficient of (
13.14
) is defined on

\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}}^{[1]}=(\mathfrak{u}^{[1,0]})^{-1}(\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}})
.

With these preparation, we then make the assumptions:

\{
B2
\}

i
The inclusion
\mathfrak{u}^{[m,0]}(\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}])\subset\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}}
holds.

ii For each
\mathsf{m}=(\mathsf{p},\mathsf{q},\mathsf{r})\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
, there exists a constant
\mathsf{F}_{\mathsf{m}}
such that

\displaystyle|\sigma^{m}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})-\sigma^{m}(\mathbf{y},\mathsf{s})|\leq\mathsf{F}_{\mathsf{m}}|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}|,

(13.27)

\displaystyle|\mathsf{b}^{m}(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})-\mathsf{b}^{m}(\mathbf{y},\mathsf{s})|\leq\mathsf{F}_{\mathsf{m}}|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}|

(13.28)

for all
(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s}),(\mathbf{y},\mathsf{s})\in\mathfrak{H}[\mathbf{a}]_{\mathsf{m}}
such that
(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})\sim_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathbf{y},\mathsf{s})
.

Theorem 13.6
.

Suppose that (
13.14
) has a weak solution
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{B})
satisfying
\{\mathbf{B1}\}
and that
\{\mathbf{B2}\}
holds.
Then,
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{B})
satisfies
(
IFC
)
.

Proof.

Theorem
13.6
was proved in
[
16
, Th. 3.2]
under more mild assumptions.
From
\{
B1
\}
and
\{
B2
\}
in the present paper, we can deduce the assumptions
[
16
, Th. 3.2]
.
Hence, the proof is omitted here.
∎

The next step is to provide a sufficient condition for
\{
B1
\}
.

We set
a=\sigma{}^{t}\sigma
, where
\sigma
is the coefficient in
(
13.14
)
.
We make an assumption.

\{
UB
\}

a=(a_{kl}(x,\mathsf{s}))_{k,l=1}^{d}
is uniformly elliptic with upper bound
c_{13.5}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{k,l=1}^{d}a_{kl}(x,\mathsf{s})\xi_{k}\xi_{l}\leq c_{\small\ref{;34}}|\xi|^{2}\quad\text{ for all }\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d},(x,\mathsf{s})\in\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}}^{[1]}.

Let
\nu
be a random point field such that
\nu(\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{sde}})=1
and let

\mathfrak{l}\!:\!\mathsf{S}_{\mathrm{s,i}}\!\to\!(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{N}}
be a label.
We shall consider ISDE (
13.14
) with the initial distribution
\nu\circ\mathfrak{l}^{-1}
.
Let
\{Q_{\mathsf{s}}\}
be a family of probability measures on
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\})
such that
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{B})
defined on
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},Q_{\mathsf{s}},\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\})

is a weak solution of (
13.14
) starting at

\mathbf{s}=\mathfrak{l}(\mathsf{s})
for
\nu
-a.s.
\mathsf{s}
.
We assume
\{Q_{\mathsf{s}}\}
is a measurable family in the following sense.

\{
MF
\}

Q_{\mathsf{s}}(A)
is

\overline{\mathscr{B}(\mathsf{S})}^{\nu}
-measurable in
\mathsf{s}
for each
A\in\mathcal{F}
.

We note that, if
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{B})
is a strong solution, then
\{
MF
\}
coincides with
(
MF
)
.

For
\{Q_{\mathsf{s}}\}
satisfying
\{
MF
\}
, we set
Q_{\nu}=\int_{\mathsf{S}}Q_{\mathsf{s}}d\nu
.
Then,
(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{B})
under
Q_{\nu}
is a solution of (
13.14
) with the initial distribution
\nu\circ\mathfrak{l}^{-1}
.
We make an assumption.

\{
BX
\}

\sigma[\mathbf{B}_{s};s\leq t]\subset\sigma[\mathbf{X}_{s};s\leq t]
for all
t
under
Q_{\nu}
.

Let
Q_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s}}^{[m]}
be the distribution of
\mathbf{X}^{[m]}=(\mathbf{X}^{m},\mathsf{X}^{m*})
under

Q_{\mathfrak{u}(\mathbf{x})+\mathsf{s}}
.
Then,

\displaystyle\int_{C([0,\infty);(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\times\mathsf{S})}f(\mathbf{w}_{t}^{[m]})dQ_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s}}^{[m]}=\int f(\mathbf{X}^{[m]}_{t})dQ_{\mathfrak{u}(\mathbf{x})+\mathsf{s}}.

Let
\nu^{[m]}
be the
m
-reduced Campbell measure of
\nu
for
m\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\nu^{[0]}=\nu
. We set

\displaystyle Q_{\nu}^{[m]}=\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\times\mathsf{S}}Q_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s}}^{[m]}d\nu^{[m]}\quad\text{ for $m\in\{0\}\cup\mathbb{N}$}.

By definition,
Q_{\nu}^{[0]}=Q_{\nu}\circ\mathsf{X}^{-1}
. We make an assumption.

\{
S
ν
\}
For each
m\in\{0\}\cup\mathbb{N}
, the
m
-labeled process
\mathbf{X}^{[m]}
under
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\{Q_{\mathfrak{u}(\mathbf{x})+\mathsf{s}}\},\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\})

gives a symmetric, Markovian semi-group
T_{t}^{[m]}
on

defined by

\displaystyle T_{t}^{[m]}f(\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s})=\int_{C([0,\infty);(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{m}\times\mathsf{S})}f(\mathbf{w}_{t}^{[m]})dQ_{\mathbf{x},\mathsf{s}}^{[m]}.

Furthermore,
\nu^{[m]}
is an invariant measure of
T_{t}^{[m]}
.

Let
\theta\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
such that
0\leq\theta(t)\leq 1
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\theta(t)=0
for
t\leq\epsilon
, and

\theta(t)=1
for
t\geq 1-\epsilon
for a sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
.
Assume that
0\leq\theta^{\prime}(t)\leq\sqrt{2}
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
.
Let
\mathit{d}_{\mathsf{q}}^{Q}
be the function on
\mathsf{S}
defined by (
13.24
).
Let

\displaystyle\widetilde{\chi}_{\infty}(\mathsf{s})=\lim_{Q\to\infty}\sum_{q=1}^{\infty}\theta\circ\mathit{d}_{\mathsf{q}}^{Q}(\mathsf{s}).

(13.29)

The limit in (
13.29
) exists because
\theta\circ\mathit{d}_{\mathsf{q}}^{Q}(\mathsf{s})
is non-negative and non-decreasing in
Q
.
We make an assumption.

\displaystyle\int_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert\widetilde{\chi}_{\infty}\rvert^{2}d\nu<\infty.

(13.30)

In practice, (
13.30
) is easy to check.
Indeed, we have the following.

Lemma 13.7

(
[
16
, Lem. 5.4]
)
.

(
13.30
) follows from (
13.17
) and

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathsf{q}=1}^{\infty}\mathsf{q}^{2}\nu(\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{q}}]^{c})<\infty.

(13.31)

Lemma 13.8

(
[
16
, Prop. 6.3]
)
.

Assume that
\{\mathbf{UB}\}
,
\{\mathbf{MF}\}
,
\{\mathbf{BX}\}
,
\{\mathbf{S}_{\nu}\}
, and (
13.30
) hold. Let
\kappa_{\mathsf{q}}=\inf\{t>0;\mathsf{X}_{t}\notin\mathsf{K}[a_{\mathsf{q}}]\}
Then,

P(\lim_{\mathsf{q}\to\infty}\kappa_{\mathsf{q}}=\infty)=1
.

Proof.

The proof follows from
[
16
, Prop. 6.2]
, where an additional assumption
\{
D
\}
was imposed.
This assumption was not used in
[
16
, Prop. 6.3]
. (It was used in
[
16
, Prop. 6.2]
).
∎
